### Context

Hinge spec §2: the press commits the release (no recall, no undo, at the mechanism level too —
ruled gap #1); the lock as a ledger (A2's mechanism, GTC-168); universal, silent, per-change
versioning; the two-sentence threshold script (*"You can still change anything — I'll just keep
the history." / "You'll start to see replies coming in. I'll track them and flag anything that
needs you."*); the atomic-feeling rollout — mechanically many messages over an hour with retries,
experientially one act, one sentence, one handover.

### Scope

**Fix here:**
- The press action itself: triggers A2's lock, anchors a send timestamp on the Event, dispatches
  H2-composed (GTC-187) messages to all confirmed recipients (per C1's toggles, GTC-172) in an
  apparently-atomic rollout. *[recipients — "per C1's toggles" is superseded by the founder
  ruling of 2026-09-13 below: adults individually, children through their household]*
- Surfaces the two-sentence threshold script at the moment of commitment.
- Refuses a roadmap/options door showing the machine's plan (cadences, escalation, tone-over-time)
  per the spec's explicit refusal — the compressed two-sentence script carries the feeling without
  the inventory.

**Do not touch:**
- All Do-Not-Touch Zones in `GATHER-BUILD-CONSTANTS.md`, especially SMS opt-out (Zone 7).
- The pre-flight (I1, upstream) and the minutes-after screen (I3, downstream) — this ticket is the
  press itself, the threshold moment.

### Founder ruling — the recipient and channel model (Nigel, 2026-09-13)

Ruled by the founder on 2026-09-13 and recorded in the founder's words. Extended the same day by
the second-pass rulings below, which also approve the build shape.

> THE ASK. Every adult who owns an item is messaged directly — email first,
> text if they have no email, and if they have neither they become a line on
> the host's list, named with their item, for her to handle out of band.
> A child's item is carried in their household contact's message. That is the
> only case where a message carries someone else's ask, and it is what the
> household is for at the ask: the route by which a child's job reaches an
> adult. The household is not a messaging unit for adults.
>
> THE CHASE. Day four: text, or email if they have no phone. Day seven: the
> same. Then it is the host's. The ask prefers email and the chase prefers
> text, each falling back to the other, and a person with neither drops out at
> the same point in both.
>
> THE VOICE. The message carries the host's name as sender and her address as
> reply-to, sent from our verified domain because it cannot be sent from hers.
> The seam the Hinge §5 describes is what makes that honest rather than a
> pretence — the second movement names Gather, and it is doing real work.
>
> THE PRESS IS ONE ACT. It reports the send on acceptance, not on delivery.
> The board tells the truth afterwards: a bounce goes red with its why-line,
> which is the fourth RED_REASON. She finds out on the board, not at the press.

*[2026-09-14 — THE CHASE's "each falling back to the other" is narrowed by rulings O and P of the
third pass: the fallback is for a channel the person does not have, not one they have refused or
one the chase cannot use. How far P reaches is decision 15. THE PRESS's bounce red reaches a
carried child's rows (ruling S).]*

*[⚠ 2026-09-15, LATER THE SAME DAY — **ruling AG supersedes ruling Y and gives the email leg back,
gated.** THE CHASE's *"or email if they have no phone"* is now the host's to switch on per person at
the pre-flight: ticked, they are chased by email at day four and seven; unticked, they hand over to
her list. The DEFAULT is the note below. Read this one first.]*

*[⚠ 2026-09-15 — THE CHASE's EMAIL LEG IS RETIRED by ruling Y of the fourth pass, which settled
decision 15 WIDE. "Day four: text, or email if they have no phone" becomes text or nothing: a person
the chase cannot text hands over to the host's list at the moment of send, whatever the reason, and
no chase email is ever sent. "The ask prefers email and the chase prefers text, each falling back to
the other" now holds in one direction only — the ask falls back to text; the chase falls back to the
host. The ask is untouched. See ruling Y for what that costs.]*

#### The spec disagreement this settles — Hinge §4 against Moment 4 §10.7

Hinge §4 makes per-person toggles the household's voice (*"The toggled recipients ARE the
household's voice"*). Moment 4 §10.7 makes the recipient control one decision per household
(*"One decision per household, not a matrix"*). **Resolution, by this ruling: adults
individually, children through their household.** Neither reading survives whole. §4's toggle is
replaced by a rule — every adult owner is a recipient, and there is nothing to toggle. §10.7's
picker survives only as the route a child's item takes.

**Overturned with it, for adults: the no-channel adult routed through somebody else.**

**Banners added 2026-09-13, on the founder's instruction, dated and pointing here. The prose
beneath each is unchanged:**
- Hinge §4, above the *"Owner and channel are separate fields"* bullet (Grandma).
- Hinge §7, above the last bullet, *"No-channel people are not failures"*.
- Moment 4 §8.7, naming its last sentence, *"No-channel adults route via the Moment 1 recipient
  toggle"*.

**Four further passages were identified as candidates and are deliberately NOT bannered** — see
ruling K.

**What in the tree still encodes the other reading** (as of `b3e9f31`):

- `findProxyNudgeCandidates` in `src/lib/sms/proxy-nudge-eligibility.ts`, `sendProxyNudge` and
  `getProxyHouseholdReminderMessage` — text a household's channel about its unconfirmed members,
  adults included (`checkInCount` counts every non-host member). The household as a messaging
  unit for adults, in live code.
- The schema docstring on `Household.contactPersonEventId` and the header of
  `src/lib/households/channel.ts` — *"who should Gather talk to for this household?"*, for every
  member. The column survives; its meaning narrows to children.
- `Household.messagesMuted` and `resolveHouseholdMuted` — "household messages" now means carried
  child asks and nothing else.
- The pre-flight page's step blurb: *"One channel per household, and how hard the system chases."*
- This ticket's own Scope line, *"per C1's toggles"* — marked in place above.
- Dormant: `ReachabilityTier.PROXY` (read for display by `invite-status` and `ReachabilityBar`)
  and `PersonEvent.proxyPersonEventId` (no writer in `src/`).
- [[GTC-252]] (awaiting ruling) is about the proxy path's frequency. This ruling shrinks that
  path to children's items, so GTC-252's ruling should follow this one, not precede it.

### Founder rulings — second pass (Nigel, 2026-09-13)

Made the same day, on the audit below. Each is recorded with the founder's words where the
founder gave them.

#### A. Children in the host's OWN household are hers — CORRECTED, see below

The audit found that a child in the host's household has the host as contact ([[GTC-256]]
Ruling 7), the host is never messaged (Ruling 5), and so the child's ask reaches nobody — a hole
in the one household every event has.

**As first ruled, 2026-09-13:**

> A child in the host's household is hers to handle. Their item appears on her list, the same
> list as an unreachable adult, rather than being carried in a message she will never receive.
> The list is now two things: people Gather cannot reach, and children whose contact is her.
> She is not chased about her own children.

⚠ **CORRECTED THE SAME DAY, 2026-09-13 — the founder narrowed it, and the correction is the
ruling. This is not the original.** The phrase *"children whose contact is her"* was wider than
intended: it would have swept in a child of another household that has picked the host as its
contact.

> A child in the HOST'S OWN HOUSEHOLD is hers to handle. A child in another household that has
> picked her as its contact is not — GTC-256 already ruled she receives those as that household's
> ear, and my wording would have moved them onto her list for no reason.

**So the ruling is:**
- A child **in the host's own household**: their item is a line on her list, beside the
  unreachable adults. She is not chased about her own children.
- A child in **any other** household whose picked contact is the host: carried to her in a
  message, exactly as [[GTC-256]] founder answer 2 ruled — *"She volunteered to be their ear"* —
  and that household's own switch governs it.

**Consequences recorded:**
- The host's list holds two kinds of line, each named with its item: adults Gather cannot reach,
  and children of her own household.
- **The host can therefore receive an ask message — as a carrier, and only as a carrier.** That
  consequence has its own heading, A2, directly below.

#### A2. ⚠ The host as carrier — the one exception to the host exclusion

*Given its own heading on the founder's instruction, 2026-09-13: it is the one place ruling A as
corrected reaches into guarded behaviour, and slice 1 must meet it rather than discover it.*

**The exception, stated as narrowly as it is.** The host exclusion holds everywhere except in one
case: **the host is another household's picked contact, and that household has a child who holds
an item.** Then that child's ask is carried to her, in a message that names the child's item and
nothing of hers.

**What does not move:**
- **She is never sent her own ask.** No message to her names an item she holds, and no message is
  sent to her on her own account.
- **[[GTC-256]] Ruling 5 is untouched. She is not a message recipient in her own right.**
  `isAddressable` and `ADDRESSABLE_PERSON_EVENT` in `src/lib/eligibility/host-exclusion.ts` go on
  excluding her from every path they guard, and `HOST_SKIP_REASON` — *"never receives her own
  ask"* — stays true as written.
- **[[GTC-256]] founder answer 2 is what makes the exception legitimate rather than a breach:**
  *"She volunteered to be their ear."* She is reached because another household chose her, not
  because of what she is.

**Narrow by construction, not only by rule.** [[GTC-256]] Ruling 7 makes her the
`PRIMARY_CONTACT` of her own household and bars her joining another as a member, and
`resolveHouseholdChannel` falls back only to a household's own primary contact. So she is another
household's contact **only by an explicit pick** of `Household.contactPersonEventId`, never by
default. The children of her own household are not carried to her at all — they are on her list
(ruling A).

**⚠ THIS IS THE ONLY CASE IN THE MODEL WHERE THE HOST RECEIVES AN ASK.** Anyone widening it later
— to her own items, to the adults of a household she is contact for, to a household that did not
pick her — is **widening a ruling, not fixing a gap**, and needs a founder ruling to do it.

**What slice 1 meets:**
- The chooser admits her **only** when all three facts hold — another household, she is its picked
  contact, a child of that household holds an item — and that household's `resolveHouseholdMuted`
  reads as sending.
- A message to her carries carried items only. Nothing of hers is ever merged into it.
- A test pins both directions: the carried ask to her is produced, her own ask is not, and
  `test:host-never-messaged` stays green unchanged.

**⚠ Where it reaches a Do-Not-Touch zone — Zone 3, and it is not solved here.** The host
exclusion itself is [[GTC-256]] Ruling 5's invariant rather than one of the eight named zones. The
zone this exception reaches is **Zone 3**: a carried message needs a link she can answer from, and
Ruling 8 gives her **no PARTICIPANT token and no guest-side page** — `ensureEventTokens` in
`src/lib/tokens.ts` revokes any PARTICIPANT token held by a `role: 'HOST'` row (the
`hostRowPersonIds` sweep, [[GTC-256]] phase 3, approved narrow). How the host answers a carried
child's item is therefore **open** — unanswered decision 14 — and is not to be settled in passing
by issuing her a token. *[Answered 2026-09-14 by ruling N — a one-off link that answers only the
carried item, the single exception to [[GTC-256]] Ruling 8. Filed as [[GTC-297]]; Zone 3 is still
not solved here.]*

#### B. The shared email — filed as [[GTC-293]], Medium, not a blocker on the press

> I over-rated it: the case I built it on does not happen, because she types Mum and then Dad
> with their own details. The real case is two people who share one address between them,
> which is rarer.

**Dropped from blocker on that reasoning, not on effort.** Still worth the ticket, for two
reasons: a person disappearing with no error is bad whenever it happens, and the edit path
demoting a primary contact into an uneditable household is the worse half. Both halves are
recorded in [[GTC-293]].

#### C. `contactMethod` — the defect is that it is wrong, and it is wrong today

**The finding is not the derivation. It is that 184 of 269 values are wrong** — in gather_dev,
of 269 adult non-host memberships (as of 2026-09-13), 184 carry a `contactMethod` their own
`Person` fields contradict. **This is a defect that exists now, and it went unnoticed because one
path reads the column.** That path is `findProxyNudgeCandidates`, which skips a household channel
whose stored value is not `SMS` — so a contact with a valid phone stored as `NONE` or `EMAIL` is
never reached by it, and nothing else would notice.

How the tree lets it drift (verified by reading, as of `b3e9f31`):

- **A per-event snapshot of a global record.** `Person` is shared across events; `contactMethod`
  lives on each `PersonEvent` and is recomputed only on the event being edited
  (`reconcileHouseholdMembers`, `PATCH` in `src/app/api/p/[token]/contact-info/route.ts`).
- **An edit that never recomputes.** `PATCH` in `src/app/api/events/[id]/people/[personId]/route.ts`
  writes `email` and `phoneNumber` and leaves `contactMethod` alone.
- **Two phone columns.** `POST` in the batch-import route stores the legacy `Person.phone`, and it
  and `createMember` count that as a phone; every sender reads `phoneNumber`. Such a row says `SMS`
  and can never be texted.
- **Fixtures write it directly.**

All 184 rows sit on fixture and test events, so they measure drift in this database, not in real
capture; which mechanism produced each row was not established.

**The two pure choosers replace it** (slice 1): the ask's and the chase's, reading `email`,
`phoneNumber` and `smsOptedOut` at the moment of sending.

**Then the column is dropped, not kept** — *"a column five places write and nothing reads is the
drift we just measured."* The sequence: slice 8 stops the last read; then stop writing and keep
it for one ticket; then drop it and the `ContactMethod` enum in its own migration. **Filed as
[[GTC-295]]**, on the founder's instruction, rather than left outside the slices and unowned.

`reachabilityTier` is written at the same five sites from the same fields and has the same shape;
it is read for display by `invite-status` and `ReachabilityBar`. It is named and excluded in
[[GTC-295]], and is not covered by this finding or the ruling.

#### D. [[GTC-187]] decision 1 — narrowed to per recipient; the three gaps are work

*"One message per person"* becomes **one message per recipient**. The three composition gaps are
**work items, not notes**:

1. **Items carry no owner name.** `AskRecipient` has `itemNames` only. Slice 2.
2. **"Nothing for you to bring" is false when she carries a child's ask.** `askSystemVoice`
   branches on `itemNames.length === 0`. Slice 2.
3. **The guest page returns only the link holder's items.** `GET` in
   `src/app/api/p/[token]/route.ts`. ⚠ **Zone 3 — flagged, NOT solved.** Answering a carried
   item from the contact's page means a PARTICIPANT token authorising a write to another person's
   assignment, which is `AccessToken` scope. It needs its own approval and is not to be done in
   passing. Owner: [[GTC-191]]. It is a precondition of slice 5: the press must not send a link to
   a page that cannot answer what the message asks.

[[GTC-187]] carries this narrowing as of 2026-09-13.

#### E. Coordinators — a PARTICIPANT token as well, after [[GTC-262]]

Option (a).

> A coordinator who owns an item is a guest with an item and a job, and the job should not cost
> them the ask.

**Consequence recorded:** `ensureEventTokens` issues a PARTICIPANT token to a coordinator as well
as the COORDINATOR one. ⚠ Zone 3 — approval at execution, as [[GTC-256]] phase 3 had. **Ordered
after [[GTC-262]]**, which closes the unauthenticated endpoints handing out coordinator tokens.
A precondition of slice 5. **Filed as [[GTC-294]]**; [[GTC-248]] now records that its own scope
does not cover it.

#### F. Reply-to — `User.email`, shown at the pre-flight, no custom column

The reply-to reads `User.email`, and the pre-flight shows it (slice 3). **No column for a custom
reply-to** — *"that is a later decision and building it now is building for a want nobody has
expressed."*

**Consequence recorded:** in gather_dev (as of 2026-09-13), 2 of 29 events have a host `Person`
with no `User`, and so no `User.email`. Every event created through `POST /api/events` has one;
seed and script events may not. What the press does then is an unanswered decision below.

#### G. Acceptance — the press commits and reports; each clock starts at its own acceptance

The press commits and reports. **Each person's clock starts when their provider accepts their
message.** The clock is therefore **per person, not per press** — the honest version, and what
Hinge §6 implies.

**Consequences recorded:**
- `POST` in `confirm-invites-sent/route.ts` stamps every `PersonEvent.sentAt` at the press today.
  Under this ruling `sentAt` is written on acceptance, per person; `Event.sentAt` stays the press
  and the lock.
- A send rejected at submission never gets a `sentAt`, so no chase ever counts from it.
- ⚠ **`isMiniSend` is broken by this ruling, and it is filed against slice 5** (the founder's
  instruction, 2026-09-13: the slice that introduces the per-person clock meets it rather than
  discovers it). `isMiniSend` in `src/lib/lifecycle.ts` is `personEvent.sentAt > event.sentAt`.
  Once `sentAt` is acceptance time, every recipient's is later than the press and **every one
  reads as a mini-send.** It has no caller at `b3e9f31`, so it is latent — and it is wrong the
  moment anything calls it. Slice 5 replaces or removes the definition; it may not leave it
  standing.

#### H. The spec banners — added

Three banners, dated and pointing here, prose unchanged. Listed under the §4/§10.7 resolution
above.

#### I. The build shape — APPROVED as sliced, slices 5, 6 and 7 landing together. NOT STARTED.

> Amber says Gather is on it, and on a list line Gather is not — she is. That is the board lying
> to her, which is the failure the glance exists to prevent.

**The amber is wrong regardless of which fix is chosen.** That much is ruled. Moment 4 §3 makes
amber *"the system is on it"*, and a line on the host's list is precisely the opposite: the
system is out of moves and has said so at the press.

**Today's behaviour, measured by reading:** `deriveItemState` in `src/lib/glance/state.ts` gives

adult and a child of the host's own household both read as chased.

**Two options, and the choice is the founder's — see unanswered decision 10 for both and what
each costs.** Not decided here, on the founder's instruction. *[Answered 2026-09-14 by ruling M —
red, with neither option.]*

#### K. The remaining banners — no

> Three passages were named and three are bannered. A banner on every adjacent passage turns a

**The four candidates, recorded so the question stays visible and does not need re-deriving:**

bullet (*"closed by §4: toggled recipients are the household's voice"*); and Moment 4 §10.7's
*"Everyone is a potential owner; recipients are whoever's picked."* Each is narrowed by the
§4/§10.7 resolution above and carries no banner.

#### T. The don't-chase person stays grey — REVISED 2026-09-14 (second correction). Answers

**The sequence, recorded in full because the reasoning is what matters, not just the outcome:**

1. **First scenario** (third pass, as filed): his email bounces; he stays grey.
2. **First correction** (same pass): ruled wrong, not merely retracted. It said don't-chase stops
   the send before anything goes out, so nothing can bounce. Checked against the record above and
   found to contradict it — [[GTC-192]] Ruling 18 (*"the mark suppresses CHASING, never HOLDING"*),
   [[GTC-179]] Ruling 3 (suppresses the two automated *nudge* paths, not the ask), and the approved
   press, whose slice 5 dispatcher gates on the child rule, the host exclusion, opt-out and quiet
   hours — never the mark. **The first scenario stood.** A don't-chase person is sent the ask, and
   it can bounce.
3. **Second correction, replacing both, and now the ruling:**

> THE RULING, replacing both. Don't-chase means: Gather has done its bit, this one is mine. She has
> taken him over.
>
> GREY MEANS SHE HAS TAKEN IT, AND NOTHING PUTS A TAKEN THING BACK ON THE BOARD.
>
> The ask still goes out. The mark is not an exclusion — he is invited like anyone else, and she
> follows up herself to make sure he knows where and when.
>
> His bounce does NOT turn him red. He stays grey. She already told Gather she is handling him;
> surfacing him again is Gather taking back a job she took off it.
>
> An unreachable person who is ALSO marked don't-chase stays grey. Grey beats ruling M's red, and
> for the same reason — M's red means "this is yours", and the mark already said that. Red would be
> Gather telling her something she told it first.

**Ruled:** grey, on this ground. **Answers decisions 16 and 17, both struck below.**

⚠ **THE COST, recorded because it was accepted knowingly, in the founder's words:**

> If his address is dead, Gather knows and she does not. She will ring him anyway, which is what
> the mark says she will do, so it probably costs nothing. But Gather is holding a fact she has not
> been told, and that is the trade.

⚠ **CHECKED AGAINST [[GTC-192]] RULING 14 — CONFIRMS RATHER THAN CHANGES IT. THE TANGLE WAS THE
PRIOR PASS'S RECORDING, NOT A CONFLICT IN THE CODE.** Ruling 14 is built as
`worst !== 'GREEN' && person.nudgeMark === 'DONT_CHASE'` in `derivePersonState`
(`src/lib/glance/state.ts`), applied *after* worst-colour-wins rather than enumerated against the
red reasons, precisely so that *"any red source added later"* is displaced without a code change —
the module's own docstring says so. The bounce reason ruling M adds is exactly such a source, so
Ruling 14 already grants it the precedence this ruling now asks for, and **no code changes as a
result of this ruling.** The stated ground differs — Ruling 14's is *"no exceptions, over every
red"*; this ruling's is *"she has taken it, and a taken thing does not come back"* — but they
converge on the identical colour for the identical case, and this ruling supplies the ground for
why don't-chase belongs on Ruling 14's list at all, which Ruling 14 itself never argued. The prior
pass's decisions 16 and 17 arose from treating a founder correction as fact before checking it
against the record; checked now, there was never a conflict to resolve.

### Founder rulings — fourth pass (Nigel, 2026-09-15)

*Eight rulings answering the eight decisions the third pass left open, recorded in the founder's
words as given and lettered on from it, Y to AF in the founder's order. Each is matched to its
decision by subject, not by the founder's numbering. Documentation only: nothing is built, and
where a ruling creates work outside the approved build shape it is filed as its own ticket and not
scoped.*

**How the eight meet the list.** **Answered:** 15 (Y, all three parts), 4 (Z — the half ruling R
left), 21 (AA), 20 (AB), 12 (AC), 19 (AD), 8 (AE — the half ruling V left), 18 (AF).
**Matching no decision on the list:** none. What remains outstanding on this ticket beyond the
numbered list is at *What is still open and is not a numbered decision*, below.

⚠ **Ruling Y narrows three earlier rulings of this ticket and retires a leg of the approved build
shape.** It is the only one of the eight that does. See Y.

⚠ **RULING Y WAS SUPERSEDED THE SAME DAY BY RULING AG**, recorded in its own section after this
pass. Y is left in place and marked. Its default survives; its fourth consequence — red at the press
for a successfully-asked person — is what AG overturns. **AG reopened decision 15's rider (b) and
decision 19, answered both, and raised decisions 23, 24 and 25**, so this pass's *"open after this
pass: none"* no longer holds and has been removed rather than left to mislead.

#### Y. If the chase cannot text, it is the host's — answers decision 15, all three parts

> ⚠ **SUPERSEDED THE SAME DAY BY RULING AG, 2026-09-15 — see *Founder ruling AG*, below.** The body
> of this ruling is left exactly as it was recorded, because the sequence is the record and because
> most of it survives: the DEFAULT is still chase-by-text, and a hand-over is still a hand-over.
> **What AG overturns is consequence 4 below — red at the press for a person whose ask has just gone
> out successfully.** The founder's reason, in the founder's words: *"A relative I email
> successfully, who might reply within the hour, reading RED from the press because he has no
> mobile. Red means Gather is out of moves and this is mine. For someone asked thirty seconds ago
> that is false, and it is not a rare shape. Wide as ruled made the board wrong on day one."* Under
> AG the chase channel is a per-person default the host overrides at the pre-flight, so an unticked
> person is hers **because she decided**, not because a rule swept him there. Read Y for what the
> default is; read AG for what happens to the people it hands over.


> DECISION 15 — WIDE. If the chase cannot text, it is mine, whatever the reason. Nobody is chased
> by email.
>
> My reason, and it answers one of the riders: a hand-over shows RED, at the moment of send. Red
> means it is the host's to do, and that is consistent with the earlier ruling that an unreachable
> person is red from the press rather than amber.

**All three parts of decision 15 are answered, not only (a).**

- **(a) the reach — wide.** Ruling P's principle is read widely: *any* chase that cannot text hands
  over, not only a phone that exists and cannot be used. The chase has no email leg.
- **(b) the timing — the moment of send.** A person the chase can never text is on the host's list
  from the press, not at day four. Ruling O left this unstated and ruling P said day four; the
  reason given settles it, because a hand-over that shows red at the send cannot first appear on
  day four.
- **(c) the reading — red, from the send**, by ruling M's ground rather than a new one.

**⚠ WHAT WIDE COSTS — spelled out on the founder's instruction, because the option label
understated it.**

1. **The day-four and day-seven reminders become text-only.** A person with an address and no
   usable number gets the ask by email and is never reminded by any channel. Ruling Q's *"the
   invitation and both reminders"* keeps the invitation and loses both reminders: under this
   ruling no chase email is ever sent by the scheduler, so there is no reminder for an unsubscribe
   link to sit in.
2. **Everyone without a usable phone comes to the host's list at the press, not at day four.** The
   list stops being *"people Gather cannot reach"* and becomes two populations: people who were
   never asked, and **people who were asked successfully and will never be chased.** The second is
   new, and it is not small — an ordinary emailed guest with no mobile is in it.
3. **Email becomes an ask-only channel.** Gather sends email at the invitation and nowhere else in
   the chase. Every automated follow-up after the press is SMS.
4. **⚠ A consequence of (2) and (c) together, recorded because it is the surprising one.** A guest
   who is emailed successfully, and who may well reply within the hour, **reads RED on the board
   from the press** — because Gather will never chase him, not because anything has failed. Red in
   Moment 4 §3 is *"only you can do this; the system can't or has exhausted its moves"*, and on
   this reading the system has exhausted its moves at the moment it sends. That is the ruling as
   given and it is recorded as ruled; it is named here because red-at-the-press for a person whose
   ask has just gone out is the cost the option label hid.

**What already built assumes otherwise — checked against the tree, and it is less than it looks.**

- **The automatic chase has no email leg today, so wide removes work rather than adding it.**
  `findNudgeCandidates` in `src/lib/sms/nudge-eligibility.ts` filters `person: { phoneNumber:
  { not: null } }` in SQL, and `sendNudge` in `src/lib/sms/nudge-sender.ts` calls `sendSms` and
  nothing else. The chase is already text-only in code. **Slice 8's *"the sender gains the email
  leg"* is retired by this ruling** — see the build-shape note.
- **`sendNudgeEmail` in `src/lib/email.ts` has exactly two callers**, neither of them the
  scheduler: `POST` in `src/app/api/events/[id]/people/[personId]/nudge/route.ts` (the by-hand
  nudge, decision 19 / ruling AD) and `src/lib/wrap-up.ts` (the thank-you, not a chase). Confirmed
  by grep at this commit.
- **One line in slice 1 assumes the narrow reading, and it was built to be deleted.**
  `chaseChannelOf` in `src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts` carries
  `ANCHOR(GTC-189): decision 15 — narrow reading of ruling P` above `if (person.email) return
  { ok: true, channel: 'EMAIL' };`. **Deleting that line is this ruling.** The three `[D15-narrow]`
  assertions in `tests/channel-chooser-test.ts` are exactly the three a deletion fails, checked by
  mutation at slice 1. Inside slice 1's module and already anticipated: no new ticket.
- **The pre-flight preview does NOT already show the hand-overs, and this is the new work.**
  `readAskPreview` in `src/lib/preflight/ask-preview.ts` routes every membership through
  `chooseAskRoute` only; `chooseChaseRoute` has **no caller anywhere in `src/`**, verified by grep.
  So `HostListLine` is built purely from ask-routing, and a person who can be asked but not chased
  produces no line at all. **Filed as [[GTC-305]].**
- **The board does not know either.** `RED_REASONS` in `src/lib/glance/state.ts` holds three, and
  `whyLineFor` in `src/components/glance/strip.ts` renders only what is in `WHY_PRECEDENCE`, so a
  red with an unlisted reason renders with no why — the same gap ruling M recorded. Widening who is
  red widens that gap. Also [[GTC-305]].
- **[[GTC-300]] reaches further under this ruling.** `isValidNZNumber` in `src/lib/phone.ts` rejects
  `+61`, so every Australian number is a hand-over at the press instead of an email chase at day
  four. Recorded on GTC-300.

**What this ruling narrows — three earlier rulings of this ticket, and no spec passage.**

- **THE CHASE (2026-09-13): *"Day four: text, or email if they have no phone."*** The email leg is
  retired. Noted beside that ruling.
- **Ruling P (2026-09-14): *"THE LONDON COUSIN GOES ON THE LIST at day four."*** The list line
  stands; *"at day four"* becomes *at the send*. Noted beside ruling P.
- **Ruling Q (2026-09-14): *"the invitation and both reminders."*** The reminders have nobody to
  reach. [[GTC-296]] narrows to the invitation and to the two guest-facing emails that exist today.
  Recorded there.
- **No spec banner is needed, and the ruling moves toward the spec rather than away from it.**
  Moment 4 §8.3 already states the chase's channel: *"Channel: TNZ SMS as built."* Neither spec
  names email as a chase channel anywhere — checked. The email leg was introduced by THE CHASE on
  2026-09-13, not by a spec, so retiring it needs no banner.

#### AC. The press refuses to send when the host has no `User` — answers decision 12

> DECISION 12 — REFUSE TO SEND. An event whose host has no account is fixed before it sends, not
> sent with a hole in it.

**⚠ WHAT SHE SEES — checked against the tree and against gather_dev on the founder's instruction,
because the ruling is only right if she is told what to fix and can fix it.**

**She can fix it. The ruling stands.** The measurement and the reasoning:

- **Measured in gather_dev, 2026-09-15, read-only: 2 of 29 events have a host `Person` with no
  `User`** — unchanged from ruling F's count. **0 of them carry a `stripePaymentIntentId`**, so no
  paid event is in this state, and `POST` in `src/app/api/events/route.ts` cannot produce one: it
  creates the `User`, the `Person` and the `EventRole` in one transaction.
- **The refusal is reachable, on one of the two.** *Kate test event* has one HOST/COHOST
  `EventRole`, so a signed-in user can open the pre-flight on it while `Event.host.userId` is null
  and `replyTo` in `readAskPreview` answers null. *Summer BBQ Party* has no HOST/COHOST
  `EventRole` at all — nobody can sign in to it, so the refusal is unreachable there and the event
  is stranded for a different reason.
- **Why the reachable case exists at all.** `requireEventRole` in `src/lib/auth/guards.ts` keys on
  the **session user's** `EventRole`, while `replyTo` reads `Event.hostId -> Person.userId ->
  User.email`. They are different identities, so a co-host or a script-made event can pass the
  guard with a host `Person` that has no account.
- **The fix a host can perform: the claim flow.** `/h/[token]` sets `needsClaim` when
  `!person.userId` (`GET` in `src/app/api/h/[token]/route.ts`); `POST` in
  `src/app/api/auth/claim/route.ts` takes the address **from the request body**, so she types it
  and the host `Person` need not already have one — *Kate test event*'s host `Person` has
  `email: null` and is still fixable. `POST` in `src/app/api/auth/verify/route.ts` then sets
  `person.userId` and creates the HOST `EventRole` in one transaction.
- **⚠ THREE THINGS STAND BETWEEN THE RULING AND A HOST, and they are the work, not the answer.**
  (1) Nothing on the pre-flight names the problem or the fix — the screen would refuse with no
  route out. (2) The fix lives behind her **host magic link**, a surface the pre-flight does not
  link to. (3) The claim email goes through Resend, which does not authenticate in this environment
  ([[GTC-247]]), so the fix is performable in principle and unverifiable here.
- **Filed as [[GTC-309]].** The refusal itself belongs to slice 5, which owns the press route; what
  she is shown and how she reaches the fix belongs to no slice, and that is what the ticket carries.

#### AD. The by-hand nudge sends, and says what it is overriding — answers decision 19

> DECISION 19 — LET IT SEND, BUT TELL ME WHAT I AM OVERRIDING. The by-hand reminder is me choosing,
> and a rule I made for the automatic case should not silently overrule my own hand.

**⚠ THE ODDITY RULING Y CREATES — recorded on the founder's instruction rather than left to be
discovered.**

> NOTE THE ODDITY DECISION 15 CREATES: with wide, the by-hand reminder becomes the only path by
> which an email chase ever happens.

**Verified at this commit.** `chooseManualNudgeChannel` in `src/lib/sms/manual-nudge-recipient.ts`
returns `'email'` whenever the person has an address and the number is missing, invalid or opted
out. Under ruling Y no scheduler ever sends a chase email, and `sendNudgeEmail`'s only other caller
is the wrap-up thank-you. **So `POST` in `src/app/api/events/[id]/people/[personId]/nudge/route.ts`
is the sole remaining producer of a chase email in the whole product.** Two things follow and are
recorded, not ruled:
- Ruling Q's *"every email carries a way out"* still has one live chase email to reach, which is
  the one [[GTC-296]] must not drop when its scope narrows under Y.
- ⚠ **Zone 7.** `chooseManualNudgeChannel` sends email to an **opted-out** phone, and ruling O reads
  a no to one channel as a no to being chased. This ruling lets the host override that by hand; it
  does not change what opt-out means for anything automatic, and it is not an invitation to widen
  Zone 7.

**Consequences recorded:**
- The channel decision is unchanged; what is added is the host being told. `chooseManualNudgeChannel`
  returns a channel and no reason, so the reason has to come from somewhere — which is the work.
- **Filed as [[GTC-310]].**

#### AE. No gap — the old household reminder runs until slice 8 replaces it. Answers decision 8

> DECISION 8 — WHEN THE NEW CHASING LANDS. No gap. The old household reminder runs until slice 8
> replaces it.

**This is the approved build shape already**, so **no ticket is filed and no slice boundary moves.**
Decision 8 is now answered in both halves: ruling V gave the species change, this gives the timing.

### Founder ruling AG (Nigel, 2026-09-15) — SUPERSEDES RULING Y

*Made later the same day, on reading the fourth pass's own report of what wide cost. Recorded as
its own section rather than folded into Y, because the sequence is the record: ruling Y stands
above, legible and marked, and this is what replaced it and why. Documentation only. Nothing is
built. Where it creates work it is filed and not scoped.*

#### What changed the founder's mind, in the founder's words

> WHAT CHANGED MY MIND: your fourth consequence. A relative I email successfully, who might reply
> within the hour, reading RED from the press because he has no mobile. Red means Gather is out of
> moves and this is mine. For someone asked thirty seconds ago that is false, and it is not a rare
> shape. Wide as ruled made the board wrong on day one.

**That fourth consequence was recorded at ruling Y and is the thing this overturns.** It is worth
naming that the ruling was overturned by its own cost sheet rather than by anything new being
found — the spell-out the founder asked for at Y is what produced AG.

#### The ruling

> THE RULING: the chase channel is a default I can override, per person, on the pre-flight.
>
> - Default: chase by text. Someone with no usable phone gets no chase and comes to my list —
>   wide, as ruled Y.
> - But the pre-flight shows me each of those people, and I can tick "chase by email" on any of
>   them. Ticked, they are chased by email at day four and seven like anyone else.
> - Unticked, they come to my list — and now because I looked at them and decided, not because a
>   rule swept them there.
>
> WHAT THAT FIXES: nobody is red who has not failed. A person I have chosen to chase is amber,
> because he genuinely is being chased. A person I have chosen to handle is mine, because I chose
> it. The two populations your report warned were being collapsed into one red are now separated by
> my own hand.

#### What survives of ruling Y, and what does not

**Survives — the default, and the mechanism underneath it.**
- The chase's default channel is text. `chaseChannelOf` in
  `src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts` still loses its narrow-reading line and its
  `ANCHOR(GTC-189)`: absent a tick, a person with no usable phone is not chased by email. The three
  `[D15-narrow]` assertions still move.
- A hand-over is still a hand-over: no chase, and the person comes to the host's list.
- Ruling O is untouched by the default. Ruling P's hand-over stands.

**Does not survive — that the hand-over is automatic and unreviewable.**
- ⚠ **Red at the press for a successfully-asked person is REVERSED.** That was ruling Y's fourth
  consequence and is the specific thing overturned. A person the host has ticked is chased, so he
  is amber like anyone else; a person she has not ticked is hers *by her decision*, which is what
  makes ruling M's red true of him rather than merely applied to him.
- **The chase's email leg comes back, gated.** Ruling Y struck *"the sender gains the email leg"*
  from slice 8. Under AG it returns, conditional on the tick. The strike is reversed in the build
  shape with a note; it is not the unconditional leg THE CHASE originally described.
- **Ruling Q's *"both reminders"* is partly restored.** Under Y no scheduler email existed. Under
  AG a ticked person receives day-four and day-seven reminders by email, so ruling Q's unsubscribe
  requirement has automatic email to reach again. [[GTC-296]] is corrected.

#### Decision 15 rider (b) — confirmed, and one hole

**Confirmed: an unticked person comes to the host's list at the press.** That is what falls out, and
the reasoning is that the decision now precedes the send. The tick is made at the pre-flight,
before the press; by the time the press happens, every person the default hands over has already
been looked at. There is no later moment at which the answer could change, so there is no later
moment at which the list could grow. Ruling Y's *at the moment of send* is unchanged as a time; what
changed is what it means — *she decided this before pressing*, not *a rule swept him here*.

⚠ **THE HOLE: a person added AFTER the press never passed that screen.** Mini-sends are slice 5's
(Hinge §2 gap #5, and ruling G's per-person clock). A person added on day three has no tick, because
the pre-flight that would have asked for one is behind her. Three readings are available and none is
ruled — the default applies and he is hers without her being asked; the mini-send's own collapsed
pre-flight asks her then ([[GTC-189]] Hinge §2: *"The pre-flight for one person collapses to the
message preview"*); or the tick is askable at any time from the board. **Decision 23.**

⚠ **AND A SECOND: can she tick a person who has opted out of texts?** Ruling O reads a no to one
channel as a no to being chased at all, so ticking *chase by email* on an opted-out person
contradicts ruling O directly — and unlike ruling AD's by-hand nudge, this one makes the
**scheduler** email him, twice, without her in the room. Zone 7 is adjacent and the difference
between a button she presses once and a standing instruction is exactly the difference AD turned
on. **Decision 24.**

#### Decision 19 — ruling AD survives, and narrows in two places

**It survives.** The by-hand nudge is a different act at a different time. AG's tick is a standing
decision taken before the send; AD's button is a single act in the moment, and it still reaches
people the tick does not — an opted-out person, a person whose number the send cannot use, and
anyone she chose not to tick. *"Tell me what I am overriding"* still has something to tell her.

**It narrows, first: the notice must not fire for a ticked person.** For someone she has already
ticked *chase by email*, pressing nudge-by-email is not an override — it is the same decision she
already made, made again. Telling her she is overriding a rule at that point is the product
arguing with her own standing instruction. `chooseManualNudgeChannel` in
`src/lib/sms/manual-nudge-recipient.ts` does not read the tick and would not know.

**It narrows, second, and this is a correction rather than a narrowing.** ⚠ **Ruling AD's recorded
oddity is FALSIFIED by AG and must not stand as written.** AD recorded, on the founder's
instruction:

> NOTE THE ODDITY DECISION 15 CREATES: with wide, the by-hand reminder becomes the only path by
> which an email chase ever happens.

Under AG that is no longer true: a ticked person is chased by email by the scheduler. The sentence
was true of ruling Y and is false of ruling AG. **Corrected in place on [[GTC-310]] and
[[GTC-296]]**, both of which had recorded it as a standing fact, rather than left to be found by
someone reading a ticket written one ruling out of date.

**Not absorbed.** AD keeps its own ticket.

#### What it costs — measured, not estimated

**The storage.** One per-person, per-event fact that survives from the pre-flight to the chase.

- **Shape: a nullable column on `PersonEvent`.** That model is already the exact grain — one row
  per (person, event), `@@unique([personId, eventId])` — and it already carries the precedent.
  `PersonEvent.nudgeMark` is a host-set, per-person, per-event chase modifier whose own docstring
  argues the case: *"ON PersonEvent, NOT Person, and that is the whole point. The mark is a
  per-EVENT hosting judgement."* The same argument applies here unchanged, and `PersonEvent` carries
  no JSON column, so as with `nudgeMark` the only question was which table.
- **Nullable, not a boolean default.** `nudgeMark`'s docstring again: *"NULL means NO MARK — no
  opinion — and it is not a stored 'normal'."* AG needs the same three states, because decision 23
  exists: *she ticked*, *she looked and did not tick*, and *she was never asked* (the mini-send).
  A two-valued column cannot tell the third from the second, and the third is precisely where the
  behaviour is unruled.
- **It is NOT part of slice 4's outbound record.** Slice 4 adds a per-recipient row describing what
  was sent — event, membership, kind, channel, provider, provider message id, accepted at, rejected
  at, why. The tick is a host decision that exists before anything is sent, has a different
  lifetime, and is one per membership rather than one per message. Putting it on that row would
  make a standing instruction into a property of a message.
- **But it should ride in slice 4's migration.** Slice 4 is *"Schema, alone"*, already scheduled and
  already required to be rehearsed against a production copy per the Deploy caveat. A second
  migration for one nullable column buys nothing and costs a second rehearsal. This is the same
  reasoning ruling W recorded for GTC-299's third `kind`: *"cheaper to meet before that migration
  than after."* **The executor's recommendation, not a founder ruling.**

**What it does to slice 3, which is built and committed.** More than it looks, and the change is
one of kind rather than of size.

- **The screen stops being a read.** `GET` in `src/app/api/events/[id]/pre-flight/message/route.ts`
  assembles and answers; its `PATCH` writes exactly one thing, `Event.askAuthorLine`, last-write-
  wins. A per-person tick is a second write path with a different grain, and last-write-wins is the
  wrong contract for forty independent switches.
- **It needs the chase chooser, which slice 3 does not call.** `readAskPreview` in
  `src/lib/preflight/ask-preview.ts` routes every membership through `chooseAskRoute` only, and
  `chooseChaseRoute` still has no caller anywhere in `src/`. That was already GTC-305's finding;
  AG makes it a precondition of the control as well as of the display.
- **The host's list changes species.** `HostListLine` is a line to read, with one why from
  `hostListReason` in `src/lib/preflight/ask-preview-compose.ts`. Under AG some of those lines carry
  a control, and the list holds two kinds of person — cannot be asked at all, and can be asked but
  not chased — where slice 3's heading says *"Gather will not message these people"*, which is false
  of the second kind.
- **The words need a ruling.** Every word on that screen was founder-ruled at its slice, and
  `ADULT_WHY` / `CHILD_WHY` carry six entries kept deliberately so no case renders blank. AG needs
  new ones, and they are not ruled.
- **Step 5's five checks may mean something different.** `allChecked` gates the Send button on
  five *"I have looked"* acknowledgements. A step she must decide on is not the same as a step she
  confirms she read.

**What it does to [[GTC-305]] — differently shaped, and smaller where it mattered.**

- **Its board half shrinks, and its proposed severity drops from High to Medium.** GTC-305 was
  proposed High because ruling Y put ordinary successfully-emailed guests into red on day one. AG
  removes exactly that population: a ticked person is amber because he is genuinely being chased.
  What is left red is a person the host chose to take, which is what ruling M's existing red already
  means — so it is now an open question whether AG needs any new `RED_REASON` at all, where under Y
  it plainly did.
- **Its pre-flight half grows**, and moves to [[GTC-311]]: the line she reads becomes a row she acts
  on.
- **Net: re-scoped, not cancelled.** The board still has to know who is on the list —
  `deriveItemState` in `src/lib/glance/state.ts` still gives an unanswered row AMBER for everyone
  who is not the host, and `whyLineFor` in `src/components/glance/strip.ts` still renders nothing
  for a red whose reason is not in `WHY_PRECEDENCE`. GTC-305 is rewritten around AG rather than
  closed.

**Filed as [[GTC-311]]** — the per-person chase-channel override: the control, its storage, and the
chase honouring it. Not scoped.

#### ⚠ THE DEFAULT — measured against gather_dev, and I think it is wrong

*The founder asked for an honest answer rather than a diplomatic one, and asked to be told if the
ruling is a workaround for a bad default. Measured read-only on 2026-09-15, reusing `chooseAskRoute`
and `chooseChaseRoute` themselves so the numbers cannot drift from the product's own decision.*

**⚠ A CORRECTION INSIDE THIS MEASUREMENT, RECORDED BECAUSE IT REVERSED THE CONCLUSION.** The first
pass counted every `chooseChaseRoute` answer of kind `DIRECT` as *chased by text* without reading
its `channel`. Because the narrow-reading line is still in the tree, most of those answers were
`EMAIL`. That pass reported a tick population of 7.9% — small enough to call the default fine. Split
by channel, the same query reports **95.1%**. The first number was wrong and the conclusion built on
it was the opposite of the true one.

**The numbers, as they stand:**

- **253 of 266 recipients would be tickable — 95.1%.** Only **7 (2.6%)** have a usable phone and are
  chased by text under the default. On the largest seeded board, 61 of 61.
- **Across every `Person` row in gather_dev: 333 people, and 12 have a `phoneNumber` stored at all.
  Nine of those are usable NZ numbers.** 222 have an email.
- **On the ten paid events** — the closest thing in this database to real use — **52 adult guests
  carry one usable phone between them.** Thirty-nine have an email and no usable number; twelve have
  neither.

**One measured thing pushes the other way, and it is a defect rather than a distribution.**
**35 `Person` rows hold a valid, already-normalised `+64…` number in the legacy `Person.phone`
column while `phoneNumber` is NULL — and no row has both.** Every sender reads `phoneNumber`, so all
35 are invisible to the chase, and all 35 also have an email, which puts every one of them in the
tick population today. That is ruling C's *"two phone columns"* finding made countable. Reading that
column would move 35 people out of the tick population — leaving roughly **82%**, which is still
almost everyone.

**What the numbers cannot tell us, said plainly.** gather_dev is fixtures and seed data. Ruling C
already established that this database measures drift rather than capture, and the same caveat
governs here: the magnitude above is an artifact of how the seeds were written, and no real host has
ever filled this form in anger.

**So the honest answer is not from the data, and it is still yes.** Three structural facts point the
same way and none of them depends on gather_dev:

1. **Capture asks for neither.** In `Moment1InputForm`, both Email and Phone are rendered as
   `SkipForNowField`. Only the name is required — `'Name is required'` is the sole validator that
   blocks. Nothing tells a host a phone is worth more than an address, because under the ask it is
   not.
2. **The ask is email-first, so an email is sufficient.** A host who types addresses and no numbers
   has a product that works perfectly at every step until the pre-flight, where she is suddenly
   asked to tick forty boxes to undo a default she had no way of knowing she was setting.
3. **The only real-world evidence in the repo is an email send.** Hinge §6's ground truth — *"Kate's
   last Christmas, email sent 8:58am"* — is the one observation of an actual invitation in this
   product's history, and it has no phone numbers in it.

**Recommendation, and it is the executor's, not a ruling.** Keep the per-person override: it is the
half that fixes the board, and it is not in question. Change the default it overrides. Two shapes,
both preserving everything AG achieves:

- **(a) One event-level default, on.** *"Chase by email when there's no mobile"* — one decision at
  the pre-flight instead of forty, with the per-person tick surviving as the exception. The
  populations are still separated by her hand; her hand just moves once. Smallest screen, smallest
  storage, and the per-person column is still needed for the exceptions.
- **(b) Per-person, pre-ticked.** Everyone with an address arrives ticked, and she unticks the
  people she would rather handle herself. Same screen as ruled, opposite starting point.

I prefer (a). Under (b) she still reads forty rows to confirm a default, and the screen's claim on
her attention is the thing that made ruling AG necessary. Under (a) the pre-flight says one true
sentence, and the list she reads is the short one — the people she is actually taking on.

**Not decided here, and not treated as decided.** AG is recorded as ruled; this section is the
measurement the founder asked for and the recommendation the founder asked for, and neither changes
the ruling. **Decision 25.**

> ✅ **RULED THE SAME DAY — decision 25 is answered by ruling AH of the fifth pass, and option (a)
> is taken.** One event-level default, *"chase by email when there's no mobile"*, ON, with the
> per-person control surviving as the exception — which **inverts it to *hand this one to me***.
> Option (b) is rejected on the reason given above. The founder ruled it on the structural argument
> rather than the numbers. ⚠ **The measurement error described above is recorded as a finding at
> ruling AH**, on the founder's instruction, beside [[GTC-273]] and [[GTC-276]].**

### Founder rulings — fifth pass (Nigel, 2026-09-15)

*Four rulings, lettered on from AG. Three answer the decisions ruling AG raised — 25, 24 and 23 —
and the fourth is an instruction to file a finding the fourth pass under-rated. Documentation only:
nothing is built, and where a ruling creates work it is filed and not scoped.*

**How the four meet the list.** **Answered:** 25 (AH), 24 (AI), 23 (AJ). **Matching no decision on
the list:** AK, the two phone columns, which is a filing instruction rather than a ruling.
**Raised by this pass and open:** 26, the composition rule between the new event default and the
per-person exception.

#### AH. The default changes — one event-level switch, on. Answers decision 25

> DECISION 25 — CHANGE THE DEFAULT, your first option. One event-level default, "chase by email
> when there's no mobile", ON. The per-person tick survives as the exception.
>
> Your reasoning carries it and it is the structural half, not the numbers: capture asks for
> neither an email nor a phone, the ask is email-first, so a host who types addresses and no
> numbers has a product that works perfectly until this screen — where she is asked to undo a
> default she had no way of knowing she was setting. And the only real-world evidence in the repo
> is an email send with no phone numbers in it.
>
> Your second option is rejected for the reason you gave: pre-ticked still makes her read forty
> rows to confirm a default, and the screen's claim on her attention is what made AG necessary.

**⚠ WHAT THE PER-PERSON CONTROL NOW MEANS — it inverts, and this is the substantive consequence.**
Under ruling AG as first stated, the default handed people over and the tick said *chase this one*.
Under AH the default chases them and the exception says **_hand this one to me_**. Same column, same
screen, opposite polarity. Anything written against AG's wording before this ruling is describing
the exception backwards.

**Consequences recorded:**
- **Ruling AG's fix survives intact and is what made this rulable.** Nobody is red who has not
  failed; the two populations are still separated by the host's hand. AH moves where her hand
  starts, not whether it decides.
- **Two fields, not one, and they are siblings of an existing pair.** The event default is a
  sibling of `Event.nudgePace` — nullable, *"NULL means NOT SET"*, per event, host-set. The
  exception is a sibling of `PersonEvent.nudgeMark` — nullable, *"NULL means NO MARK — no
  opinion"*, per person per event. The precedent is exact in both cases and the schema docstrings
  already argue it.
- **⚠ THE EXECUTOR'S NULLABLE RECOMMENDATION SURVIVES AND ITS STATED REASON DOES NOT.** At ruling
  AG the per-person column was argued nullable because three states were needed — *she ticked*,
  *she looked and did not*, and *she was never asked*. **Ruling AJ removes the third**, because the
  mini-send asks too. The column is still nullable, for a different reason: NULL now means *follow
  the event default*, which is the common case and under AH is most rows. Recorded as a correction
  rather than left standing, because a right conclusion resting on a wrong reason is the shape
  [[GTC-273]] and [[GTC-276]] are both about.
- **Both columns should still ride slice 4's migration**, for the reason ruling W gave. Executor's
  recommendation, not a founder ruling.
- **[[GTC-311]] is rewritten around this**, and is smaller for it: one sentence at the pre-flight
  and a control on the exceptions, rather than a control on every row.
- **Decision 26 is raised and not answered.** See below.

##### ⚠ Decision 26 — how the two compose, and why it is not obvious

`Event.nudgePace` and `PersonEvent.nudgeMark` compose by **QUIETER-WINS**, and `nudgePace`'s
docstring is explicit that this is *"not an override ladder — a GENTLE person on an OFF event gets
NO nudges, not gentle ones."* The pair AH creates looks identical and **must not inherit that rule
by analogy without a ruling**, because the two do not agree in every case:

- Default ON, person marked *hand them to me* — quieter wins and the ladder agree: she takes him.
- Default OFF, person marked *chase by email* — **they disagree.** Quieter-wins says do not chase;
  an override ladder says chase him. The case is a host who wants to handle everyone herself except
  her brother.

Under AH's default the two rules give the same answer for every case a host reaches without first
turning the event switch off, so this is narrow. It is recorded because an executor reading
`resolveNudgeOffsetDays` will find a composed pair next door with a settled rule and a stated
reason, and copying it is the obvious move. **Decision 26.**

##### ⚠ The measurement that produced decision 25 got it backwards first — recorded as a finding

*Recorded in this ticket on the founder's instruction, not only in the report, and framed as the
class rather than the incident.*

**The defect: a measurement that does not read the field it is measuring.** The first pass over
gather_dev counted every `chooseChaseRoute` answer of kind `DIRECT` as *chased by text*, and never
read the `channel` on the answer it had just received. Because the narrow-reading line is still in
the tree, most of those answers carried `channel: 'EMAIL'`.

- **Reported: 7.9% of recipients would need ticking.** Small enough to call the default sound, and
  that is what it was about to be called.
- **True, with the same query, split by channel: 95.1%.**
- **The conclusion built on the wrong number was the exact opposite of the right one.** Nothing in
  the output looked wrong — 239 of 266 *chased* is a plausible sentence, and the number was
  internally consistent. It was caught only because a second, independent measurement of the raw
  channel shape disagreed with it, and the disagreement was too large to be rounding.

**It is the same family as two findings already in the ledger**, and belongs beside them:
[[GTC-273]], where the route scanner's FAIL-OPEN detector matched the spelling of a credential
rather than evaluating it; and [[GTC-276]], where a detector inherited the defect the ticket that
wrote it had just named. All three are checks that examine something adjacent to the thing they
claim to examine, and all three pass convincingly while doing it.

**The rule this leaves.** A measurement that classifies a structured answer reads every field its
classification depends on, or it is measuring the shape of the answer rather than its content. Two
independent measurements that should agree are the cheapest way to find out which one you built,
and the disagreement is the signal — not the smaller number.

⚠ **AND THIS RULE HAS A SECOND HALF, ADDED 2026-09-18 — it is necessary and it is not sufficient.**
At slice 5's shape proposal a measurement of the same channel field read every field it depended on,
came out **exactly right**, and was still attributed to the wrong cause: 231 EMAIL to 1 TEXT was
blamed on [[GTC-312]]'s two columns, and building GTC-312 did not move it by one recipient, because
ruling AK had made the ask email-first. **Reading every field gets you a true number; a true number
still does not tell you why it is true.** The first failure is caught by a second measurement that
disagrees. The second is caught only by a change that should move the number and does not. Recorded
in full as the second finding at the slice 5 build-shape entry, and paired with this one there.

#### AI. The tick is refused on an opted-out row — answers decision 24

> DECISION 24 — THE TICK IS REFUSED ON AN OPTED-OUT ROW. Ruling O stands unnarrowed. Your
> distinction is the reason: AD is one press in the moment, with me in the room; a tick is a
> standing instruction that makes the scheduler email him twice with me nowhere near it. A no to
> being chased is not overridden by a switch I set once and forget.
>
> Say what the screen shows for him instead — he still belongs on my list, and the reason has to
> say why the tick is not available.

**Consequences recorded:**
- **Ruling O is unnarrowed.** *"A no to one channel is treated as a no to being chased."* It
  survives ruling Y, ruling AG and ruling AH intact, and it now has a stated boundary against
  ruling AD: the difference is not the channel, it is whether the host is in the room.
- **He is asked, and he is not chased.** `askChannelOf` in
  `src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts` checks `email` before opt-out, so an opted-out person
  with an address is a DIRECT email recipient of the ask. `chaseChannelOf` refuses him
  `SMS_OPTED_OUT`. He is exactly the *asked but not chased* population, and under AH he is the one
  member of it the host cannot switch on.
- **⚠ He produces no line today.** The host's list is built from `chooseAskRoute` alone in
  `readAskPreview`, and his ask succeeded, so nothing puts him on it. [[GTC-311]] owns the line.
- **⚠ A structural note the words depend on.** `ADULT_WHY` in
  `src/lib/preflight/ask-preview-compose.ts` already has an `SMS_OPTED_OUT` entry — *"No email, and
  has opted out of texts."* — but that is the **ask** refusal, for a person with no address at all.
  Decision 24's person has an address and is refused by the **chase**. Same why-code, different
  question, and reusing the map would put a false sentence on his line. The chase refusals need
  their own words, keyed separately.

**The words — PROPOSED at this ruling and RULED AS WRITTEN at ruling AM, same day.** They stand
exactly as proposed below; ruling AM records the founder's confirmation and the reason. Gather speaks
as *"I"* on this screen (slice 3 words, answer 1):

- **His reason line:** *"They've opted out of texts — so I won't chase them at all."*
- **Where the control would be:** *"[can't be chased — opted out]"*, in place of the switch rather
  than instead of showing one. This follows [[GTC-294]]'s `LINK_NONE` precedent and the founder's
  ruling on it — *"Honest beats a promise that never arrives"* — so she can see that the option
  exists and why it is not hers to take, rather than wondering why one row has no switch.

Both were the executor's proposals and **both are now ruled** — see ruling AM. Every other word on
that screen was ruled at its slice, and these two are no exception.

#### AJ. The mini-send asks the same question at its own send — answers decision 23

> DECISION 23 — THE MINI-SEND. Take your second reading: the collapsed one-person pre-flight Hinge
> §2 already describes asks me then. A person added on day three gets the same question the others
> got, at his own send. That keeps one rule rather than two, and it keeps the decision before the
> send where AG put it.

**Consequences recorded:**
- **It uses a surface the spec already has.** Hinge §2, gap #5: *"The pre-flight for one person
  collapses to the message preview."* Nothing new is invented; the collapsed pre-flight gains the
  same question the full one asks.
- **It is slice 5's**, which owns mini-sends, and it is met there rather than discovered — the same
  treatment ruling G gave `isMiniSend`. Noted in place in the build shape.
- **One rule, not two.** The alternative readings — the default applying silently, or the tick
  becoming askable from the board — would each have made a person added on day three governed by
  different machinery from a person added on day one.
- **⚠ It removes the third state the AG storage recommendation was argued on.** Nobody is ever
  *never asked* under this ruling. The column stays nullable under ruling AH for a different
  reason — NULL means *follow the event default*. Corrected at AH rather than left standing.
- **A consequence not ruled and not filed as a decision, because it falls out:** the collapsed
  pre-flight must show the event-level default too, or she is answering a per-person exception to a
  rule she is not being shown. Recorded on [[GTC-311]].

#### AK. The two phone columns — filed, and it is both. Matches no decision on the list

> ⚠ THE 35 ROWS with a usable number in the legacy column and nothing in phoneNumber — that is a
> bigger finding than its place in your report suggests. Thirty-five people are invisible to the
> chase entirely, today, whatever the default is. File it if it is not already filed under ruling
> C's two-columns finding, and say whether it is data, code, or both.

**It was named, never filed.** [[GTC-295]] records the two phone columns, but as one of four
reasons `PersonEvent.contactMethod` drifts — evidence for that ticket's case, not work that ticket
owns. Its title and scope are `contactMethod` and the `ContactMethod` enum. Nothing in the ledger
owns the phone column. **Filed as [[GTC-312]].**

**It is both, and there is a third leg neither word covers.**

1. **CODE — one of three capture paths writes the wrong column, unnormalised.** `POST` in
   `src/app/api/events/[id]/people/batch-import/route.ts` creates a `Person` with
   `phone: personData.phone || null` and never sets `phoneNumber`. The other two paths are correct:
   `createMember` in `src/app/api/events/[id]/households/route.ts` and `POST` in
   `src/app/api/events/[id]/people/route.ts` both write `phoneNumber` through
   `normalizePhoneNumber`. Batch-import is host-reachable — `PeopleSection` in
   `src/components/plan/PeopleSection.tsx` calls it — so this is a live path, not a fixture-only
   one. And because it does not normalise, a real CSV carrying `021 123 4567` would fail
   `isValidNZNumber` even if the column were read.
2. **DATA — 35 rows, measured read-only on 2026-09-15.** 35 `Person` rows hold a value in `phone`
   with `phoneNumber` NULL; every one of the 35 is already in valid `+64` form; **no row in the
   database has both columns set.** All 35 also carry an email, so all 35 are in the population AH's
   default now chases by email — correctly reached, and reached on the wrong channel, because the
   number they own is invisible.
3. **⚠ DISPLAY — and this is the leg that makes it a trust problem rather than plumbing.** The host
   is shown the unusable number. `GET` in `src/app/api/events/[id]/people/route.ts` and `PATCH` in
   `src/app/api/events/[id]/people/[personId]/route.ts` both serialise `person.phone`, and
   `CopyPlanAsText` and the host view read `p.phone || p.email` for a contact line. So she sees a
   mobile number on the screen for someone Gather will never text — and under ruling AH she will
   also see him chased by email, with no way to tell why.

**And it corrupts the column ruling C measured.** `createMember` and `POST /people` both set
`contactMethod`/`reachabilityTier` from `person.phoneNumber || person.phone`, so a batch-imported
person is marked `SMS` / `DIRECT` on the strength of a column no sender reads. That is ruling C's
own sentence — *"Such a row says SMS and can never be texted"* — and it is now countable.

**Not scoped here.** [[GTC-312]] carries the three legs, the measurement, and the ordering question
against [[GTC-295]].

### Founder rulings — sixth pass (Nigel, 2026-09-15)

*Four rulings and one documentation instruction, lettered on from AK. AL answers decision 26, the
last open one. AM and AN rule words and keying that were held as proposals. AO sets a severity.
Documentation only: nothing is built, and this pass files nothing new.*

**How they meet the list.** **Answered:** 26 (AL). **Matching no decision on the list:** AM and AN,
which rule proposals held at ruling AI; AO, a severity; and the polarity instruction, which is a
documentation fix. **Open after this pass: none.**

#### AL. The named exception wins — answers decision 26

> DECISION 26 — NOT A LADDER. The per-person exception WINS, whichever way the event default is
> set.
>
> My reason: the event switch is a default and the exception is a decision about one named person.
> A decision about a person always beats a rule about everyone — that is what makes it an exception
> rather than a preference.
>
> So: event default ON plus "hand this one to me" means he comes to my list. Event default OFF plus
> "chase this one by email" means he is chased.
>
> ⚠ You are right that copying nudgePace's quieter-wins is the obvious move and wrong here. Record
> why the neighbour does not apply: nudgePace composes two settings about intensity, where quieter
> is a safe direction. This composes a default with a named exception, and there is no safe
> direction — either answer silently discards something I said.

**The rule, stated so it needs no contested word: THE NAMED EXCEPTION WINS.** Where a
`PersonEvent`-level exception is set, it decides. Where it is NULL, the `Event`-level default
decides. Both directions, no precedence table, no third case.

**Why the neighbour does not apply — the founder's reason, recorded because it is the argument and
not just the outcome.** `Event.nudgePace` and `PersonEvent.nudgeMark` compose two settings about
**intensity**, and on that axis *quieter* is a safe direction: choosing the gentler of two
instructions cannot send a message nobody asked for. This pair composes a **default with a named
exception**, and on that axis there is no safe direction — whichever way it resolves, one of two
things the host said is silently discarded. Safety cannot pick the winner here, so the meaning of
the two settings has to, and the meaning is that one of them is about a person she named.

⚠ **A TERMINOLOGY COLLISION, RECORDED SO THE RULING IS NOT IMPLEMENTED BACKWARDS. THE BEHAVIOUR
ABOVE IS THE RULING; THIS IS ABOUT THE WORD.**

`Event.nudgePace`'s docstring in `prisma/schema.prisma` reads:

> It composes with `PersonEvent.nudgeMark` by QUIETER-WINS (Ruling 4), which **is not an override
> ladder** — a GENTLE person on an OFF event gets NO nudges, not one. Reading this column alone
> would get that backwards.

**In that sentence, "override ladder" means exactly what this ruling chose: the per-person setting
overrides the event setting.** So the founder's heading — *"NOT A LADDER"* — reads, against the
schema's own vocabulary, as the opposite of the behaviour the same paragraph then rules in two
worked examples. An executor who takes the heading as the instruction, goes next door to see what a
ladder is, and finds `nudgePace` refusing one, will implement quieter-wins — **which is the rule
this decision rejected.**

**The behaviour governs, not the label.** The two examples in the founder's own words are
unambiguous and are the test: *default ON plus hand-this-one-to-me* → he comes to her list;
*default OFF plus chase-this-one-by-email* → he is chased. Read as a rejection of the neighbour's
quieter-wins — which is what the paragraph beneath it argues at length — the heading and the
behaviour agree. Read as a rejection of person-wins, they contradict.

**Use "the named exception wins" in code, comments and tests, and do not use either "ladder" or
"quieter-wins" for this pair.** Both words now carry a settled meaning eight lines away in the same
schema file, and neither can be used here without inviting the reading this note exists to prevent.

⚠ **It is the same family as the rest of this week's findings, and that is why it is written down at
this length: two questions sharing one answer because they share a word.** [[GTC-273]], a detector
matching the spelling of a credential rather than evaluating it. [[GTC-276]], a detector inheriting
the defect the ticket that wrote it had just named. Ruling AN below, an ask refusal and a chase
refusal sharing the why-code `SMS_OPTED_OUT`. Ruling AH's measurement, which read the kind of an
answer and not its channel. And now a composition rule whose chosen behaviour is what the adjacent
docstring calls by the name the ruling's own heading rejects.

**Consequences recorded:**
- **The resolver is a new function, not a branch inside `resolveNudgeOffsetDays`** in
  `src/lib/nudge-cadence.ts`. That function implements quieter-wins for the intensity axis and must
  keep doing so; a second axis with an opposite rule inside it is how one of them starts answering
  the other's question.
- **The storage is unaffected**, which was true before the ruling and is why decision 26 blocked the
  resolver and not the migration. Two nullable fields, as ruling AH recorded.
- **`nudgeMark` composes independently and downstream of both.** A person handed to the host is not
  chased whatever `nudgeMark` says, and a don't-chase person is not chased whatever the exception
  says. Recorded on [[GTC-311]].

#### AM. Decision 24's two lines — ruled as written

> DECISION 24's WORDS — both proposed lines are ruled as written:
>   "They've opted out of texts — so I won't chase them at all."
>   "[can't be chased — opted out]"
> Showing the control's absence with a reason rather than just omitting it is right, and it is
> GTC-294's precedent applied correctly.

**Ruled, unchanged from the proposal at ruling AI.** The anchor marking them proposed comes off when
they are built, per the Citations rule that a provisional marker names the ticket that ends it.

**Consequences recorded:**
- **The absence is shown, not hidden.** `LINK_NONE` in
  `src/lib/preflight/ask-preview-compose.ts` is the precedent and the founder's ruling on it is the
  ground: *"Honest beats a promise that never arrives."* A row with no switch and no explanation is
  a promise of a different kind — that the option was never available to anyone.
- **Gather speaks as "I"**, consistent with the slice 3 words, answer 1, and with the six existing
  host's-list reasons.
- Recorded on [[GTC-311]].

#### AN. The chase refusals get their own why-codes — ruled

> THE SEPARATE WHY-CODES for chase refusals — yes, keyed separately. Reusing ADULT_WHY's
> SMS_OPTED_OUT would put a false sentence on his line, since his is a chase refusal and that entry
> is an ask refusal. Good catch and it is the same family as everything else this week: two
> questions sharing one answer because they share a word.

**Ruled: keyed separately.** `ADULT_WHY` and `CHILD_WHY` in
`src/lib/preflight/ask-preview-compose.ts` answer *"why is this person's ask on the host's list"*.
The chase refusals answer *"why will this person never be chased"*, and the two questions have
different true answers for the same person.

**The worked case, which is the whole argument.** A person with an email who has opted out of texts
is a DIRECT email recipient of the ask — `askChannelOf` in
`src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts` checks `email` before opt-out — and is refused by
`chaseChannelOf` with `SMS_OPTED_OUT`. `ADULT_WHY.SMS_OPTED_OUT` reads *"No email, and has opted out
of texts."* **He has an email.** One of the two sentences on his row would be false, and it would be
false quietly, because the code path that produced it is correct for the other question.

**Consequences recorded:**
- **The `Record` type is the guard.** `ADULT_WHY` is typed `Record<HostListLine['why'], string>`, so
  a missing case is a compile error. A second map over `ChaseNoneWhy` gets the same treatment, and
  the same *"every case has words"* rule the founder ruled at slice 3, answer 4.
- **Six entries in `ADULT_WHY`/`CHILD_WHY` are deliberately unreachable and stay** — kept by founder
  ruling so a new route through `chooseAskRoute` cannot produce a blank line. The chase map will
  have its own unreachable set and the same protection.
- Recorded on [[GTC-311]].

#### AO. [[GTC-312]] — High confirmed, escalation stands

> GTC-312 — HIGH CONFIRMED, brand/trust escalation stands. The display leg is what earns it: she is
> shown a number, in the list, the printable plan and the host view, for someone Gather will never
> text. A product that shows her a contact it cannot use is lying on three screens at once.

**Severity set to High on [[GTC-312]]**, `severity_proposed` resolved, the escalation retained.

#### Instruction — the polarity inversion goes to the head of [[GTC-311]]

> THE POLARITY INVERSION — make it impossible to miss. It is the finding most likely to survive

### Founder answers — the slice 1 flags (Nigel)

*Six answers to the six readings slice 1 reported before commit, recorded in the founder's words. No
date was stated with them; the machine clock read 2026-09-13 NZST, and they follow the third pass,
which the founder dated 2026-09-14. Answers 1, 2, 4 and 5 settle behaviour slice 1 built. Answers 3
and 6 name defects, filed and not fixed. They are numbered, not lettered, because they answer the
slice's flags in the order it raised them.*

⚠ **DECISION 15 IS NOT AMONG THEM AND STAYS OPEN.** See the last heading of this section.
*[ANSWERED 2026-09-15 — ruling Y, WIDE. The narrow line slice 1 built is deleted by that ruling.]*

#### 1. The host as carrier is not chased — the built reading, confirmed as a ruling

> THE HOST AS CARRIER IS NOT CHASED. A2 let her receive an ask as a carrier because a child's item
> had nowhere else to go. Chasing the host is Gather nagging the person who pressed the button, and
> nothing in the model wants that.

**So:** ruling R's *"a carried ask is chased like one"* holds for every carrier except the host. A2
is unchanged — she receives the carried ask and nothing after it. `chooseChaseRoute` in
`src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts` answers `NONE` with `HOST_AS_CARRIER`; the branch and its
`ANCHOR(GTC-189)` are kept on the founder's instruction. **Chasing her would be widening A2**, and
A2's own warning applies.

#### 2. An unusable phone and no email — the host's list. A founder ruling, not a reading

> UNUSABLE PHONE, NO EMAIL — the host's list, as you built it. Sending a text that will be rejected
> is worse than saying plainly that Gather cannot reach him.

**Recorded as a founder ruling on the founder's instruction, because ruling P covered the chase and
not the ask.** For the ask, a phone the send cannot use is no phone: a person whose only number

### Founder answers — the slice 2 words (Nigel)

*The words slice 2 proposed before commit, and the founder's answers, in the founder's words. No
date was stated with them; the machine clock read 2026-09-13 NZST. Answers 1 and 3 change words
