order they landed in the build.*

#### 1. WOULD, not could — the whole register

> WOULD, not COULD, everywhere. "Would you bring the pavlova", not "could you". Could asks whether
> she is able; would asks whether she is willing, and willing is what is being asked. Change every
> instance in ASK_REGISTER too, not only the new lines — a register that says would in one sentence
> and could in the next is two voices.

**Built** in the recipient's own ask and in the carried question. Every line it moved is in
[[GTC-187]], *Founder rulings — WOULD, not could; a sentence, not a form field*.

#### 2. On the child's behalf, not "answer for"

> THE "ON HIS BEHALF" RULING NEVER LANDED. It was in the prompt before the would/could one and got
> overtaken. Build it now — it matters more than the verb did.
> "Would you answer for Ollie?" -> "Would you please answer on his behalf?"
> Two or more children -> "Would you please answer on their behalf?"
> With her own item too -> "...on his behalf?" still, and the "too" that currently attaches to
> "answer for" attaches wherever it reads best — show me.
> Answering FOR him reads as speaking in his voice. She is confirming on his behalf, which is a
> different thing and the true one.

**The pronoun — asked, and answered by the founder.** No pronoun is captured for anyone, so a fixed
*"his"* would be wrong for every girl carried. Offered three ways, the founder chose **the child's
name** — *"on Ollie's behalf"*. Two or more children are *"their"*. Not
taken: *"their"* for one child as well, and holding the one-child line until a pronoun field
exists, which would be a schema change.

**Where "also" sits — the executor's placement, confirmed by the founder.** Beside the carrier's
own ask the question reads *"Would you also answer on Ollie's behalf?"*. A closing *"too"* sits on
*"behalf"* and can read as on somebody else's behalf as well.

> "Also" before "answer" stays, for your reason: it reads as "as well as bringing yours" rather than
> "on somebody else's behalf as well".

**"Please"** — the founder asked for a close reading of it, and then dropped it. Answer 5.

#### 3. A sentence, not a colon list — and the names under it, filed as [[GTC-302]]

> THE COLON LIST. "Would you bring: pavlova?" is a form field, not a sentence. It should read "Would
> you bring the pavlova?" — and "the pavlova and the trifle" for two, and a comma list with "and"
> before the last for three or more.
>
> ⚠ AND CHECK THE ITEM NAMES FIRST. If items are stored as "The pavlova" or "Pavlova", an added
> "the" reads wrong. Report what the names actually look like in gather_dev before you write the
> sentence, and if they are inconsistent, say so — that is a data problem, not a wording one, and I
> would rather know than paper over it.

**The names were measured before the sentence was written, and they are inconsistent** — in full in
[[GTC-302]]. Offered three ways, the founder chose to **build as ruled, with each name exactly as
stored, and file the data**, to be fixed before slice 5 sends. Not taken: holding the colon list,
and fixing names inside the composer. **Both the recipient's own ask and the carried sentences take
the form:** *"Ollie has been asked to bring the dishes."*

#### 4. "Whether you can make it" stays

> "One tap to say whether you can make it" stays as it is. Can is right there: it genuinely is about
> ability, whether she can get there on the day.

#### 5. No "please" — found by reading the two questions side by side

> DROP "PLEASE" — your reading is right and it is the same test I applied to would/could. One
> question polite and the next plain is two voices.
>
> "Would you also answer on Ollie's behalf?"
> "Would you answer on their behalf?"
>
> "On his behalf" already carries the courtesy; the word was doing it twice. Record the reasoning,
> not just the change — you found it by reading the two questions next to each other, which is the
> only way it shows.

**The reasoning, recorded as the founder asked.** Read one at a time, both questions pass. *"Would
you please answer on Ollie's behalf?"* is a courteous sentence and *"Would you bring the pavlova?"*
a plain one, and neither is wrong. The defect lives only between them. In the message that carries
both — *"Would you bring the pavlova? Ollie has been asked to bring the dishes. Would you please also
answer on Ollie's behalf?"* — one question is polite and the next is plain, and the plain one reads
curt beside it. That is two voices, the test that turned *could* into *would* (answer 1). A second
reading pointed the same way: written down with no tone to carry it, *"Would you please answer"* is
a common form of impatience. And the courtesy was already there — *"on Ollie's behalf"* asks a favour
by its shape, so *please* was saying it twice.

**What that means for checking the register:** a defect of voice shows only in the composed message
read whole, never in a sentence read alone. Layer W of `tests/carried-ask-composition-test.ts` pins
whole movements and holds *no please* across every shape together, which is the form a check for this
has to take.

**Built:** *"Would you also answer on Ollie's behalf?"* and *"Would you answer on their behalf?"*.

#### 6. A job is done, not brought

*Ruled after slice 2 was committed as `c5f3976`; built in the addendum to Evidence (slice 2).*

> RULE THE JOB SENTENCE. A job is DONE, not brought — that is the whole ruling and it changes one
> word.
>
> Own job: "Would you do the dishes?"
> Carried job: "Ollie has been asked to do the dishes. Would you also answer on Ollie's behalf?"
>
> The carried half is unchanged. The child named before the item, "on his behalf", "also" where she
> has something of her own — all as ruled.
>
> MIXED, ONE PERSON, BOTH KINDS: one sentence, not two. "Would you bring the pavlova and do the
> dishes?"
>
> MIXED ACROSS PEOPLE needs nothing: her dish and Ollie's job are already two sentences.

> ⚠ THE FIELD EXISTS AND THE DATA DOES NOT. Item.kind is ITEM or TASK, zero TASK rows in a
> thousand, and only plan generation ever sets it. So this sentence is ruled on a case that has
> never occurred, and the first job assigned by hand will be stored as ITEM and read "bring".

> ⚠ AND DO NOT FILTER JOBS OUT. Your own finding: a filtered job reaches nobody, and a child's job
> reaching an adult is exactly what the carried ask exists for. The answer was always a sentence,
> never a filter.

**Recorded, both halves, as the founder asked:**
- **The sentence is ruled now**, because jobs are the common case for children and will arrive the
  moment one is assigned.
- **It is unreachable today.** The routes where a host adds an item set no kind — [[GTC-302]]'s
  defect — so the first job assigned by hand is an `ITEM` and reads *"bring"*. And a job can be
  assigned by hand: neither assign route reads `kind`. *[CORRECTED 2026-09-13, on the founder's
  instruction — [[GTC-302]] *Corrections* 1 and 2. **The host assign route does read `kind`**, through
  `mayHoldRow` in `src/lib/assignment/same-team.ts`; only the coordinator's does not. A job typed
  into an ordinary team is assignable and reads "bring"; one typed under a generated job team is
  refused for every guest by the same-team rule. **And "Wash the dishes" has never written a row:**
  every stored name predates the prompt that makes jobs, so the sentence below is what the prompt
  would produce, not anything gather_dev holds.]* Two more things stand between the ruling and
  a guest, both in [[GTC-302]]: the preview route reads no kind, so every caller passes
  `jobNames: []` until slice 3 splits them; and the generator's example job name is *"Wash the
  dishes"*, which reads *"do the Wash the dishes"*.
- **A sentence, never a filter.** Slice 3 splits jobs from dishes and drops none. It must also count
  a job in the chooser's `ChooserMembership.holdsItems`, or a child whose only row is a job is
  `CHILD_WITHOUT_ITEM` and carried to no one — a filter by another name.

**A carried child holding both kinds is one sentence too — the executor's extension of the founder's
one-person rule, and NOT a founder ruling.** *"Ollie has been asked to bring the plates and do the
dishes."* The child is one person, so the rule was read as reaching them. The founder, on reading it:

> THE CHILD HOLDING BOTH KINDS reading as one sentence — right, and I had not considered it. Record
> it as your extension of my one-person rule rather than as something I ruled, because it was your
> reading.

**Two or more to bring beside a job — decision 22, since answered by answer 7.** The founder asked
whether the one sentence reads badly with three or more of one kind, and for a proposed shape, not a
settled one. The reading given:
- **It reads badly from two to bring, not three.** *"Would you bring the pavlova and the trifle and
  do the dishes?"* — the list's own *"and"* sits beside the *"and"* before *"do"*, and *"the trifle
  and do"* briefly reads as a third thing in the list. Three is the same: *"the pavlova, the trifle
  and the ham and do the dishes"*.
- **Two or more jobs do not.** *"Would you bring the pavlova and do the dishes and the bins?"* — the
  job list ends the sentence, so nothing follows its *"and"*.
- **Proposed:** a comma before *"and do"* when there are two or more to bring — *"Would you bring the
  pavlova and the trifle, and do the dishes?"*, *"Would you bring the pavlova, the trifle and the
  ham, and do the dishes?"*.
- **Built first as the plain join**, under an unruled anchor and an `[UNRULED mixed shape]` label,
  both since removed. The comma is answer 7.

**Built:** `whatIsAsked` in `src/lib/messages/ask-register.ts`; `AskRecipient.jobNames` and
`CarriedChildAsk.jobNames`, both required. Layer J of `tests/carried-ask-composition-test.ts`.

#### 7. The comma — answers decision 22

> DECISION 22 — TAKE THE COMMA, for your reason: with two or more to bring, the list's own "and"
> sits beside the "and" before "do", and "the trifle and do" reads for a beat like a third dish. The
> comma breaks it.
>
> Would you bring the pavlova and the trifle, and do the dishes?
> Would you bring the pavlova, the trifle and the ham, and do the dishes?
>
> One to bring stays as built: "Would you bring the pavlova and do the dishes?" — no comma, because
> there is no list "and" to collide with.

**Found by reading TWO of one kind, not three — recorded on the founder's instruction.** The
question put was whether the sentence reads badly *"with three or more of one kind"*, and three is
the obvious test case because it is the long one. The founder: *"the collision is worst at the short
one."* The reading that found it was the two-dish sentence, *"Would you bring the pavlova and the
trifle and do the dishes?"*, where the list is nothing but its own *"and"*, and only *"the trifle"*
stands between it and the next one. A check built around the long case can pass the sentence that reads worst, so layer J pins
two first.

**Where the comma does not go:** one to bring, which has no list *"and"*; and a list of jobs, which
ends the sentence — *"Would you bring the pavlova and do the dishes and the bins?"*. Through
`whatIsAsked` it reaches a carried child the same way: *"Ollie has been asked to bring the plates and
the cups, and do the dishes."*

**Built** in `whatIsAsked`. The unruled anchor and the `[UNRULED mixed shape]` label are gone;
decision 22 is struck.

RULED — see *Founder answers — the slice 3 words, second pass*, answers 1 and 2.** What the record
says:
- **Today a text reply never reaches Gather.** `POST` in `src/app/api/sms/inbound/route.ts` is
  Twilio-shaped and unauthenticated; [[GTC-229]], which authenticates it, is open and blocks

  payload to catch an opt-out. Non-opt-out bodies stay *"discarded on purpose pending that
  decision"* — its unknown 2, whether Gather stores inbound bodies, is a product and privacy
  decision with no schema and no owner.
- **And some replies never arrive at all.** On TNZ's shared shortcode a reply that follows another
  TNZ customer's message is attributed to that customer (GTC-288's H1); only a dedicated shortcode
  closes it, and it is unpriced.

So *"yet"* promises something no ticket is building, and *"goes nowhere"* stops being true when
[[GTC-288]] lands, because Gather then reads a reply for STOP. **Proposed, and true both before and
after [[GTC-288]]:** *"A text reply won't reach you — I have no way to pass it on."* Built under an
anchor marking it proposed; the ruling removed the anchor.

An adult is refused only by `askChannelOf`, so an adult never carries a household why; a child's
channel refusal always names the carrier. Named in `hostListReason`'s comments, and each is pinned
`[UNREACHABLE kept]` in `tests/ask-preview-test.ts`, so deleting one fails a test.

### What the ruling collided with — verified 2026-09-13 at `b3e9f31`

*The audit behind the rulings above. Each heading carries what was ruled.*

#### 1. `contactMethod` cannot express the inversion — *ruled: C, filed as [[GTC-295]]*

- `PersonEvent.contactMethod` is one stored `ContactMethod` (`EMAIL | SMS | NONE`), written
  phone-first at five sites: `createMember` inside `POST` in
  `src/app/api/events/[id]/households/route.ts` (counts the legacy `Person.phone` as a phone),
  `reachabilityFor` in `src/lib/households/reconcileMembers.ts` (does not), `POST` in
  `src/app/api/events/[id]/people/route.ts`, `POST` in
  `src/app/api/events/[id]/people/batch-import/route.ts`, and `PATCH` in
  `src/app/api/p/[token]/contact-info/route.ts` (*"SMS takes precedence"*).
- **One send path reads it:** `findProxyNudgeCandidates` skips a channel whose method is not SMS.
  `findNudgeCandidates` and `findDecideByFollowupCandidates` read `Person.phoneNumber` directly,
  and `chooseManualNudgeChannel` chooses from `phoneNumber`, `smsOptedOut` and `email`.
- **The cost of the inversion is no column.** The channels a person HAS are already on `Person`.
  The two choosers are shaped like `chooseManualNudgeChannel` and called by the preview and the
  sender alike, so the two cannot disagree.
- **It touches the nudge paths, and by more than the column.** The chase's email leg does not
  exist: `findNudgeCandidates` filters `phoneNumber: { not: null }` in SQL and `sendNudge` calls
  `sendSms` only — [[GTC-178]]'s closed machinery.
- The choosers must read `phoneNumber`, which every sender reads — not the legacy `phone`.

#### 2. `Person.email` is `@unique`, and capture matches on it — *ruled: B, filed as [[GTC-293]]*

Two people sharing an address: on create the second is never made; on edit the primary contact is
demoted and the household becomes uneditable; moving a member onto a held address fails the save;
and the match crosses events, dropping the typed name. The mechanism, the fix's constraints — among
them [[GTC-256]]'s reliance on the match for the host — and the reproduction are in [[GTC-293]].

#### 3. [[GTC-187]] decision 1 — *ruled: D*

- `AskRecipient` carries `itemNames` only, with no owner. `askSystemVoice` would tell a contact
  holding nothing herself but carrying a child's item *"Nothing for you to bring."* The docstring of
  `src/lib/messages/ask-register.ts` defers owner-versus-channel framing to this ticket.
- Decision 1's all-at-once control must name the child in what it says it agrees to.
- `GET` in `src/app/api/p/[token]/route.ts` returns the token holder's own assignments.
- **A child's maybe is never followed up.** `findDecideByFollowupCandidates` skips a CHILD
  assignee by the child rule, and nothing routes the follow-up to the contact.

#### 4. The ask preview does not know who is reachable — *changes land in slice 3*

- `GET` in `src/app/api/events/[id]/pre-flight/message/route.ts` lists every membership except
  host rows and CHILD rows. **No role filter** — a COORDINATOR is listed, and its link stays the
  stand-in forever. **No channel filter** — an adult with neither is shown a message they will
  never receive. It returns `hasEmail` and `hasPhone` and chooses nothing.
- **Children's items appear in no message.** Assignments are fetched only for the people listed.
  In gather_dev (as of 2026-09-13), 36 CHILD memberships hold items; all 60 CHILD rows have a
  household.
- The page shows the email subject when `hasEmail` — which, email-first, is exactly when the ask
  goes by email, so that survives. Its *"longest is N texts"* counts every recipient, which does
  not.
- **Changes:** per recipient, the chosen channel and any carried asks; the host's list, with both
  kinds of line (ruling A as corrected); the reply-to from `User.email` (ruling F); the segment
  count over text recipients only; coordinators linked once [[GTC-294]] is built.

#### Also found

- **The press sends nothing today, and starts the clocks anyway.** `POST` in
  `src/app/api/events/[id]/confirm-invites-sent/route.ts` stamps `Event.sentAt` and every
  `PersonEvent.sentAt`, logs `INVITE_SEND_CONFIRMED` and writes `SEND_PRESSED` (the prior
  send-time map, established). *Ruled: G.*
- **Email keeps no provider id.** `SendResult` is `{ success, error }`, and `resultOf` in
  `src/lib/email.ts` never reads Resend's id. That answers [[GTC-289]] Unknowns 2: no. A bounce
  cannot be joined to a send until it does.
- **Nothing records what each recipient was sent** ([[GTC-187]]'s 2026-08-29 premise correction).
  The fourth red needs a per-recipient send row to hang on.
- **"Then it is the host's" has no producer.** `EXHAUSTED_SILENCE` is declared in `RED_REASONS`
  with none; [[GTC-251]] is open.
- **A bounce on a don't-chase person would read grey.** `derivePersonState` applies [[GTC-192]]
  Ruling 14 as `worst !== 'GREEN'`, which by design displaces every red source, including one
  added later. *[Ruling T, revised 2026-09-14: grey, confirmed — a don't-chase person is sent the
  ask and it can bounce, and this line was already right. See decisions 16-17, both struck.]*
- **The glance has no child branch.** `deriveItemState` turns a DECLINED row red whoever holds
  it; Moment 4 §8.7 says a child's row cannot go red. [[GTC-192]]'s — whether one of its rulings
  already covers this was not checked. *[Ruling S, 2026-09-14: the carrier's bounce turns a
  carried child's rows red, and §8.7 is bannered. Declined and expired carried rows are not
  ruled.]*
- **A host's-list line reads amber.** *Ruled: J — the amber is wrong regardless of the fix. Which
  fix: answered 2026-09-14 by ruling M — red, with neither option.*
- **Email has no opt-out.** Zone 7 and `OPT_OUT_KEYWORDS` are SMS only, and the chase emails a
  no-phone adult twice. *[Ruling Q, 2026-09-14: every email carries a way out — filed as
  [[GTC-296]].]*
- **A text ask carries no sender name**; the host's name lives only in its body. Replies to a
  text land at TNZ inbound ([[GTC-229]], [[GTC-288]]); replies to an email land in her inbox.
  Neither moves a box — only a tap does.
- **The composed body carries no STOP suffix**, by [[GTC-187]] decision 5; dispatch appends it
  on text.
- **Children of the host's own household were carried to no one.** *Ruled: A, as corrected.*
- **Coordinators are reachable on any event.** `PeopleSection`, mounted on the plan page, offers
  the Coordinator role and `AssignCoordinatorsModal`. In gather_dev (as of 2026-09-13), 7
  coordinator memberships hold items, all on "Henderson Family Christmas 2025"; 0 households
  point at a coordinator. *Ruled: E, filed as [[GTC-294]].*
- **The dev sender is Resend's shared test domain**, and the dev key does not authenticate
  ([[GTC-247]]). "Our verified domain" does not exist in this environment.

10. ~~**How a host's-list line reads on the board.**~~ **ANSWERED 2026-09-14 — ruling M: red, with
    neither option below.** The amber is wrong either way (ruling J). The two options offered, and
    what each costs, are kept as the record:

    **Option 1 — its own state on the grid.** `ItemState` and `PersonState` in
    `src/lib/glance/state.ts` gain a member, so `deriveItemState`, `derivePersonState`,
    `worstItemState`'s ordering and `summarisePeople`'s three counts all have to place it, and so
    does every consumer: `src/components/glance/{paint,strip,reading,assistant}.ts`,
    `GlanceLive.tsx`, `PersonSurface.tsx`, and `src/lib/glance/{read,live,replay,actions}.ts`,
    plus the five `tests/glance-*` suites. The design reference's states table is fixed at five by
    [[GTC-192]] Ruling 11 and Moment 4 §3 names three colours, so a fourth reading is a
    spec-level addition and must pass §9's three feature tests. `replay.ts` has to reconstruct it
    for a past instant, and whether contact details are versioned anywhere was not checked.
    `derivePersonState` also has to say how it meets Ruling 14's grey and OUT.

    **Option 2 — off the grid.** A second surface. Moment 4 §3 refuses everything on that screen
    but the strips and one banner-class element (*"the only banner-class element the surface
    has"*), so a list there has to overturn that refusal's reason (§9's refusal test) or live only
    at the pre-flight — which leaves the board silent about the people she must handle.
    [[GTC-192]] Ruling 5 keeps every settled person a strip, the wall of names; taking a person
    off the grid is the removal that ruling refused for green. A person on the list who also holds
    other items still needs a strip, so the line and the strip can disagree, and
    `summarisePeople`'s counts still have to say something about them.

    **Both need** the grid to know who is on the list, so `read.ts` consumes slice 1's chooser;
    and the host's own children sit inside her household card while being hers (§10.8,
    person-primary).

    **A fact, not a third option:** Moment 4 §3 defines red as *"only you can do this; the system
    can't or has exhausted its moves"*, which is the nearest existing meaning to a list line,
    while Hinge §7's *"No-channel people are not failures"* bullet (now bannered) argued the
    other way. Whether the new reading is red, a shade of red, or neither is part of this
    decision.

*Added 2026-09-18, later the same day — raised by slice 5 at the shape proposal and ruled the same

29. ~~**Does withhold-and-record satisfy ruling D item 3, or dodge it?** The dispatcher can create an
    outbound row for every recipient, route it through the chooser at drain, and write
    `withheldAt`/`withheldWhy` instead of sending wherever the recipient's link cannot answer what
    their message asks. Ruling D item 3 forbids SENDING such a link, and this does not send. The
    argument against is that the press then has a mode in which it visibly fails to reach someone
    the host expects it to reach, and nothing says so until slice 7's red exists.~~
    **ANSWERED 2026-09-18 — IT SATISFIES THE RULING, AND THE ARGUMENT AGAINST IS FIXED RATHER THAN
    ACCEPTED.** *"Not sending a link that cannot answer its message is obeying the ruling, not
    dodging it — the ruling forbids sending, and this does not send."*
    ⚠ **AND THE FIX: THE PRE-FLIGHT MUST SHOW IT BEFORE THE PRESS, NOT AFTER.** *"A recipient whose
    link cannot answer their message is not READY, and slice 3's LinkState has to carry it the way
    it already carries the coordinator and host-carrier cases. That is the fix that makes
    withholding honest."* So this decision is work in **slice 3's own module**, done by slice 5:
    `LinkState` in `src/lib/preflight/ask-preview.ts` gains the state, and the host sees the
    withholding on the screen whose whole purpose is to show her what the press will do. ⚠ See the

   *[⚠ **THE SECOND FINDING OF THIS PROPOSAL — A MEASUREMENT THAT WAS EXACTLY RIGHT AND
   ATTRIBUTED TO THE WRONG CAUSE.** Recorded as the finding it became rather than as a footnote to
   the ruling it revises — the founder's instruction, 2026-09-18: *"THE CORRECTION IS THE FINDING
   and it should read that way."*

   **WHAT WAS MEASURED, AND EVERY FIGURE OF IT SURVIVED.** `readAskPreview` over all 29 events in
   `gather_dev` answers **231 EMAIL to 1 TEXT** across 232 recipients. Of 333 `Person` rows: **12**
   carry a number in `Person.phoneNumber`, which `ChooserPerson` in
   `src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts` and every sender read; **35** carry one only in
   `Person.phone`, which nothing reads; and **the two sets are DISJOINT** — 35 legacy-only, 12
   new-only, no overlap. That 35 is [[GTC-312]]'s own number, reproduced independently by a walk
   that was not looking for it. Of the 12, three hold local-format `021…` numbers, on seven events
   each, which `isValidNZNumber` in `src/lib/phone.ts` rejects, so they are `PHONE_UNUSABLE` lines
   on the host's list rather than text recipients; one more is the host, one is on no event.

   **WHAT WAS CONCLUDED FROM IT, AND IT WAS WRONG.** ~~So TNZ's importance is understated by every
   measurement taken before GTC-312 is fixed, and *Verifiable without TNZ* below is true by accident
   rather than by design. Fix GTC-312 and up to 35 people move from EMAIL to TEXT.~~ **STRUCK THE
   SAME DAY, BY BUILDING IT.** [[GTC-312]] was built on 2026-09-18 — see its Evidence — and **the
   ask split did not move: 231 to 1 before the backfill, and 231 to 1 after it.**

   **WHY IT WAS NEVER GOING TO MOVE, AND RULING AK SAID SO IN ITS OWN WORDS.** `askChannelOf` in
   `src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts` is **email-first by ruling** — *"THE ASK: email first,
   text if they have no email"*, and its first line is `if (person.email) return { ok: true,
   channel: 'EMAIL' }`. All 35 backfilled people hold an email, so the ask reached them by email
   before and reaches them by email now, **correctly, by ruling rather than by defect.** Where the
   35 actually were is **the CHASE**, which is text-first — ruling AK's own framing, *"Thirty-five
   people are invisible to the chase entirely"*, read past here. Measured after the backfill: all 35
   carry an email, **0 are opted out, 0 are marked don't-chase, and all 35 are now textable**, so all
   35 move from the email chase to the text chase. **That is slice 8's leg, not slice 5's.**

   ⚠ **WHAT IT STRENGTHENS — recorded because a correction that only subtracts is half-read.** The
   claim removed is that [[GTC-312]] changes what slice 5 can verify. It does not. **The press is
   email-dominant BY DESIGN**, because ruling AK made the ask email-first — so slice 5's
   *"verifiable without TNZ"* is true **by design, not by accident**, which is a stronger position
   than this finding originally claimed and was reached by being wrong about why. What GTC-312 moved
   belongs to slice 8, and it makes *Verifiable without TNZ* **more** true about the chase, not less.

   ⚠ **THE RULE, AND IT IS THE SHARPER HALF OF THIS FINDING. A measurement can be exactly right and
   still be attributed to the wrong cause — and the attribution goes unmeasured until a fix fails to
   move the number.** 231-to-1 was a true number, measured correctly, twice. *Why* it was 231-to-1
   was never measured at all. Nothing in the figure carried its own cause, and nothing was going to:
   a correct number is not evidence for the story told about it.

   ⚠ **AND IT IS THE SECOND OF A PAIR. Put them together, because they are the two halves of one
   mistake.** The first is at **ruling AH**, decision 25's measurement: a query that counted every
   `chooseChaseRoute` answer of kind `DIRECT` as *chased by text* and **never read the `channel` on
   the answer it had just received** — reported 7.9%, true figure 95.1%, and the conclusion built on
   it was the exact opposite of the right one. Both are about the channel field. In the first, the
   field was never read and **the number came out wrong**. In the second, every field was read and
   **the number came out right** — and the cause attached to it was still wrong. So the AH rule
   (*"a measurement that classifies a structured answer reads every field its classification depends
   on"*) is necessary and is not sufficient: reading every field gets you a true number, and a true
   number still does not tell you why it is true. **The pair, stated once: a check can fail by not
   reading the field, and it can fail by reading every field and then explaining the answer from
   somewhere else. The first is caught by a second measurement that disagrees. The second is caught
   only by a change that should move the number and does not.** Beside [[GTC-273]] and [[GTC-276]],
   and beside the first finding of this same proposal, which fails in the other direction again — a
   gap the data model does not name reads as green.]*

### Dependencies

A2 (GTC-168), H2 (GTC-187), I1 (GTC-188).

### ⚠ Inherited from Epic A: mini-sends, and the affordance waiting on them

**This ticket owns mini-sends** (Hinge §2, gap #5), and two things are parked until it does.

`PersonEvent.sentAt` exists and is written ([[GTC-168]], [[GTC-196]]): the press stamps every
existing member at once, and anyone added afterwards gets their own send date.
`isMiniSend = personEvent.sentAt > event.sentAt` needs no extra field. What does **not** exist is the
machinery that actually *sends* to those people — the three-movement message on their own clock,
which is this ticket's.

*[2026-09-13 — ruling G moves the stamp to each person's acceptance, which falsifies the
`isMiniSend` definition above. It is filed against slice 5, which owns replacing or removing it.]*

**Auto-Assign is hidden on a sent event because of that gap** ([[GTC-201]], ruled 2026-08-03). It is
a **UI absence, not a server block** — the route allows and records it, because Moment 4 §7 forbids
hard-blocking the host. But post-send it creates N asks at once, and until mini-sends work nobody it
asks would ever be told. Asking someone silently is worse than not offering the button.

**When this ticket lands (with [[GTC-178]] / E1 for the cadence those clocks run on), delete the
`!isSent &&` condition in `src/components/plan/PeopleSection.tsx` and the tombstone comment above
it.** The comment names this ticket as its expiry. The ledger side is already done: the batch carries
one why as one changeSet.

### Acceptance

- [ ] The press locks the plan via A2's mechanism and anchors a send timestamp.
- [ ] Messages dispatch to all confirmed recipients, presented as one atomic act regardless of the
      actual rollout mechanics underneath.
- [ ] The two-sentence threshold script appears verbatim (or to the same effect) at commitment.
- [ ] No roadmap/options door is shown — verify against the explicit refusal in review.
- [ ] `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, build passes, security suite exits 0.

### Stop Conditions

Standard eight from `BUG-TICKET-TEMPLATE.md`.

⚠ **Do-Not-Touch Zone 7 (SMS opt-out) adjacent.** Flag for explicit approval before merging.

Status: Complete | Branch: [...] | Commit: [hash] | Effort: xhigh

## Evidence (slice 1) — Executor-Completed

> **Slice 1 of eight. GTC-189 stays open.** The closed-ticket format above is for the last slice.

**What was built**
Two pure functions deciding, per person, how they are reached — `chooseAskRoute` and
`chooseChaseRoute` in `src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts` — over `Person` fields, membership
role, household contact, the household switch, the don't-chase mark and `Event.hostId`. No schema,
no route, no send. **Dark:** nothing imports them, and the suite asserts it; slice 3, the first
caller, removes that assertion.

**Root cause addressed — ruling C**
`PersonEvent.contactMethod` is a stored snapshot that is wrong for 184 of 269 adult non-host
memberships in gather_dev. The chooser decides at send time from `email`, `phoneNumber` and
`smsOptedOut`, and the preview and the senders are to call the same two functions.
**`contactMethod` is not touched** — none of the five writers, the one reader or
`prisma/schema.prisma` is in this commit. The column is named only in prose and in the suite's
assertion that the chooser does not read it. [[GTC-295]] owns its removal, after slice 8 stops
`findProxyNudgeCandidates` reading it.

**Files changed**
- `src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts` (new) — `chooseAskRoute`, `chooseChaseRoute`, their input
  types (`ChooserMembership`, `ChooserEvent`, `ChooserHousehold`, `ChooserPerson`) and why-codes.
  Calls `isMessageableRole`, `isHostMembership`, `resolveHouseholdChannel`, `resolveHouseholdMuted`,
  `isChaseable` and `isValidNZNumber` from their own modules. Two `ANCHOR(GTC-189)` tokens.
- `tests/channel-chooser-test.ts` (new) — 116 assertions in six layers; no database, no server, no
  clock. Labels carry `[D15-narrow]`, `[RULED slice 1 answer n]` and `[DEFECT GTC-300]`.
- `package.json` — `test:channel-chooser`.
- `docs/tickets/GTC-189.md` — the slice 1 answers, the decision 15 note, the build-shape note, and
  this section.
- `docs/tickets/GTC-299.md` — *Who this reaches* and cost 2 corrected (answer 4).
- `docs/tickets/GTC-294.md` — unknown 1 marked answered in part (answer 4).
- `docs/tickets/GTC-300.md`, `docs/tickets/GTC-301.md` (new) — the two defects (answers 3 and 6).

**The six slice 1 answers** — in full, in the founder's words, under *Founder answers — the slice 1
flags* above:
1. **The host as carrier is not chased.** The built reading, confirmed as a ruling; the
   `HOST_AS_CARRIER` branch and its anchor are kept.
2. **Unusable phone, no email — the host's list.** A founder ruling, because ruling P covered the
   chase and not the ask. `PHONE_UNUSABLE`.
3. **+61 numbers — a defect, filed as [[GTC-300]].** Left as built; the chooser's
   `[DEFECT GTC-300]` assertion moves when the predicate is fixed.
4. **Adults with no items are asked** — [[GTC-187]] decision 8, itemless coordinators included.
   [[GTC-299]] corrected, [[GTC-294]] unknown 1 annotated.
5. **A child in a muted household — the host's list.** A founder ruling: when the carrier route is
   closed, the item comes to her. `HOUSEHOLD_MUTED`.
6. **Two opt-out facts — a defect, filed as [[GTC-301]].** The chooser keeps taking a boolean; slices
   3, 5 and 8 must each say which fact they pass.

**⚠ Decision 15 — OPEN. Built as the narrow reading, not answered.**
`chaseChannelOf` chases a person with no phone and an email by email. That is one line, under
`ANCHOR(GTC-189): decision 15 — narrow reading of ruling P`, and **deleting it is the wide
reading.** Three assertions carry `[D15-narrow]` — the email-only adult's chase, the itemless
email-only adult's chase, and the chase through an email-only carrier — and a mutation deleting the
line failed exactly those three and nothing else. Decision 15 (b) and (c) are not touched: the chase
returns a why, never a time or a colour.

**How rulings O and P were read**
- **O:** an opted-out person is not chased and never falls to email, even with an address. Read as
  the refusal rather than the number, so it stands after the number is removed. The ask still goes
  by email — the ask is not a chase.
- **P:** a phone that exists and cannot be used is not chased and does not fall to email. *"Cannot"*
  is `isValidNZNumber`, which P records as today's deployment fact and [[GTC-300]] now names a
  defect.
- **T, where it meets them:** a don't-chase person is still asked. For the chase's why, the mark
  outranks the channel facts and opt-out outranks the mark, as `nudge-mark.ts` requires.

**Test results**
- **RED — 116 assertions, 10 passed, 106 failed.** Behaviour: M 0/46, C 0/52, P 1/4. Structure: S
  4/9. Isolation: I 2/2. Types: T 3/3. The two functions threw; the types and why-codes were real.
  **Every passer is independent of the behaviour:**
  - four controls — the fixture is frozen, the structure matchers and the importer matcher catch
    planted strings, and a complete row typechecks — which test the suite's own instruments;
  - *copies no gate*, *reads nothing stored*, and *touches no database, network, framework or
    clock* — absences, true of a stub and of the build alike;
  - *nothing imports the chooser* — the brief's own requirement, and also an absence;
  - the two negative type probes — a row without `nudgeMark` or `smsOptedOut` does not compile — a
    type property the stub already carried, the reason [[GTC-264]] phase 2's layer 9 passed at RED.
- **A RED finding, fixed before GREEN.** The first RED run failed *copies no gate* on the stub: the
  chase why-code was spelled `'DONT_CHASE'`, identical to the mark's value — a false positive of the
  family [[GTC-264]] phase 2 finding 2 records. **The why-code was renamed `MARKED_DONT_CHASE`; the
  matcher was not loosened**, since a shared spelling also invites a `why === nudgeMark` comparison.
  RED was re-run, and the line above is that run.
- **GREEN — 116/116.**
- **Mutation checks** — each edit verified to have landed before its result was read, and the module
  restored byte-identical by `diff`: deleting the decision 15 line fails exactly the three
  `[D15-narrow]` assertions; replacing `isChaseable` with a copied `'DONT_CHASE'` comparison fails
  *copies no gate*; adding a `contactMethod` read fails *reads nothing stored*; planting an importer
  under `src/` fails *nothing imports the chooser*; deleting the host-as-carrier branch fails the A2
  chase assertion. One failure each, no collateral.

**Assertions checked**
- [x] Ask and chase asserted separately for every combination: email only, phone only, both,
      neither, a child, a child in the host's household, don't-chase, an opted-out phone, a non-NZ
      number, and the host.
- [x] The inversion: *both* is asked by email and chased by text.
- [x] A2 in both directions: a carried ask reaches the host; her own ask is never produced.
- [x] The gates are imported from their modules and not restated.
- [x] Pure: frozen inputs, repeatable, roster order irrelevant.
- [x] Nothing imports the chooser — no caller, no preview change, no route.
- [x] `contactMethod` untouched: none of its five writers, its one reader, `prisma/schema.prisma` or
      `prisma/migrations` is in this commit, and the chooser's code — comments stripped — does not
      name it.
- [x] Adjacent flows verified: `test:host-never-messaged` 20/20 (A2's guard, unchanged),
      `test:child-exclusion` 19/19, `test:nudge-controls` 62/62, `test:message-composition` 54/54,
      `test:security` 157/157. `test:nudge-provider-gate` 37/38 — its `[CONTROL]` needs an SMS
      provider in the ambient environment and `tsx` does not load `.env.local`; the result is
      identical with the chooser moved out of the tree, so it is not this change.

**Preflight**
`prisma migrate status`: 43 migrations, up to date — run instead of `npm run db:migrate`, which is
`migrate dev` and can offer to reset a transferred database. Client in step with the schema.
`npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0 before and after. Dev-server boot not run: the slice adds no route.

**Security suite:** 157/157.

**Commit:** the commit that adds `src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts` —
`git log --diff-filter=A --format=%h -- src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts`. A commit cannot
carry its own hash.

## Evidence (slice 2) — Executor-Completed

> **Slice 2 of eight. GTC-189 stays open.** Committed as `c5f3976`. Answer 6, the job sentence, was
> ruled after that commit, with answer 7's comma; both are the addendum at the end of this section.

**What was built**
A child's ask carried inside an adult's message. `composeAsk` in `src/lib/messages/ask-register.ts`
takes `AskRecipient.carried`, a list of `CarriedChildAsk` — a child's first name and item names — and
`askSystemVoice` writes them into Gather's movement beside the recipient's own ask. Pure: no send, no
route. **The carried half is dark:** every caller passes `carried: []`, and layer D asserts it; slice
3 fills it and removes that layer.

**⚠ ONE LINE OF IT IS NOT DARK.** The slice was described as landing dark, as slice 1 did, and the
carried half does. The recipient's own ask does not: `MessageStep` in
`src/app/plan/[eventId]/pre-flight/page.tsx` already composes every guest's own ask through
`composeAsk`, so rulings 1 and 3 reach the pre-flight preview as soon as this lands. **Hosts see the
new wording today** — *"Would you bring the pavlova?"* where it read *"Could you bring: pavlova?"*,
and on today's data [[GTC-302]]'s names. Nothing is sent. The founder noted it and accepted it:
*"THE OWN-ASK CHANGE IS NOT DARK — noted, and it is fine."*

**Root cause addressed — ruling D items 1 and 2**
- *Items carry no owner name.* `AskRecipient` had `itemNames` only. A carried item now arrives with
  its child's name and cannot be built without one.
- *"Nothing for you to bring" is false for a carrier.* `askSystemVoice` chose the itemless branch on
  `itemNames.length === 0`. It now takes that branch only when there is nothing of either kind.
- *"A contact with no items has no message at all"* was checked against the tree and is only partly
  true. An ordinary adult contact already got decision 8's itemless message — the wrong one. It is
  true of the preview route, which lists no child's item (slice 3's), and of the host as carrier —
  decision 20.

**Decision 1 is not widened.** `CarriedChildAsk` is the only way a message names another owner, and
its owner field is `childFirstName`. The recipient's own `itemNames` stays a list of strings, so no
owner can ride on it. Layer T pins both, by type probes and by the exact field lists. The chooser
still decides who carries; composition sees no roles. No coordinator message, no new register, and
`src/lib/messages/compose.ts` is untouched.

**The words, as ruled** — Gather's movement only; movements 1 and 2 do not change with carrying:
- *"Would you bring the pavlova?"* — the recipient's own ask.
- *"Ollie has been asked to bring the dishes. Would you answer on Ollie's behalf?"*
- Beside the recipient's own item: *"… Would you also answer on Ollie's behalf?"*
- Two children: *"Ollie has been asked to bring the dishes. Mia has been asked to bring the fruit
  salad. Would you answer on their behalf?"*

**"Please" — read closely at the founder's request, then dropped (answer 5).** The reading, as given
before the ruling:
- **Beside the recipient's own ask, it stuck out.** *"Would you bring the pavlova? Ollie has been
  asked to bring the dishes. Would you please also answer on Ollie's behalf?"* is two questions in a
  row, and only the second said please. By ruling 1's own test — one sentence with it and the next
  without is two voices — the plain ask read curter beside it, or the carried one as the bigger
  favour.
- **Alone, it could read as impatience.** *"Would you please answer"* is a stock written form of
  exasperation (*would you please answer me*), and a first message has no tone of voice to rescue it.
- **In the carried-only messages** there was one question, so no contrast; only the second reading

### Addendum — answers 6 and 7, the job sentence and its comma

**What was built**
A job is done, not brought. `whatIsAsked` in `src/lib/messages/ask-register.ts` words what one person
is asked to take on — *"bring the pavlova"*, *"do the dishes"*, *"bring the pavlova and do the
dishes"* — for the recipient's own ask in `askSystemVoice` and for each child in
`carriedAskSentences`. `AskRecipient.jobNames` and `CarriedChildAsk.jobNames` carry `Item.kind` TASK
rows apart from dishes. Both are required, because a caller with nowhere to put a job puts it among
the dishes, where it reads *"bring"*. Someone holding only a job is not itemless, and a child whose
only row is a job is still carried. **Nothing is filtered.**

**Dark, and not by choice.** Both callers pass `jobNames: []`. `GET` in
`src/app/api/events/[id]/pre-flight/message/route.ts` selects no `Item.kind`, so every row, a
generated job included, still arrives in `itemNames` and reads *"bring"* in the preview. Slice 3
splits them, and layer D pins the empty list until it does. Why the ruling is unreachable today is
answer 6 and [[GTC-302]].

**Files changed**

  `CarriedChildAsk`; `askSystemVoice` and `carriedAskSentences` read it; `theItems` names jobs too.
  With two or more to bring, a comma before *"and do"* (answer 7).
- `tests/carried-ask-composition-test.ts` — layer J, 15 assertions; `jobNames` in every fixture; the
  second-person matcher covers *do* as well as *bring*; a layer D assertion for `jobNames: []`; layer T
  field lists, and a probe that a recipient without `jobNames` does not compile. 69 assertions in
  eleven layers. A second `[DEFECT GTC-302]` label; answer 7's three comma pins and its no-comma
  assertion replace the `[UNRULED mixed shape]` assertion.
- `tests/message-composition-test.ts` — `jobNames: []` in three fixtures.
- `src/app/plan/[eventId]/pre-flight/page.tsx` and `scripts/verify-gtc260-live-roundtrip.ts` —
  `jobNames: []`, with the reason given at the page.
- `docs/tickets/GTC-189.md` — answers 6 and 7; decision 22 added and struck; the child holding both
  kinds recorded as the executor's extension; the slice 2 build-shape note; **slice 3's build-shape

## Evidence (slice 3) — Executor-Completed

> **Slice 3 of eight. GTC-189 stays open.** Built RED first and held before commit on the founder's
> instruction; committed after the founder answered the slice 3 flags and ruled the words. Lands
> dark in the sense that matters: nothing sends, no schema, no token is issued, and the Send button
> stays disabled.

**What was built**
The preview shows what the press will do. `readAskPreview` in `src/lib/preflight/ask-preview.ts`
reads the event and routes every membership through `chooseAskRoute` — the chooser's first caller.
It returns, per recipient, the channel, their own rows split by `Item.kind` into `itemNames` and
`jobNames`, the children's asks their message carries, and the link as the press will leave it. It
returns the host's list, each line with its why, a child's line naming the carrier who could not be
reached. And it returns the reply-to from `User.email`. `composePreview` in
`src/lib/preflight/ask-preview-compose.ts` composes each message through `composeAsk`, client-side as
before, with a segment count only for a texted recipient and a subject and reply-to only for an
emailed one. `GET` in `src/app/api/events/[id]/pre-flight/message/route.ts` now guards, reads and
answers; `MessageStep` in `src/app/plan/[eventId]/pre-flight/page.tsx` renders it. `PATCH` is
unchanged.

**Root cause addressed** — *What the ruling collided with* item 4: the route listed every non-host,
non-child membership, chose no channel, fetched no child's rows, gave a coordinator a stand-in link
the press never replaces, read no `Item.kind`, and counted segments over everyone.

**⚠ The warning — a job is something a child holds.** `holdsItems` is `rowsByPerson.has(personId)`,
a row of either kind. Asserted with five children whose only row is a job, one on every route a child
can take: carried by email (Ollie), carried by text (Finn), on the host's list because the carrier
cannot be reached (Jack), on the host's list as a child of her own household (Poppy), and carried to
the host under A2 (Leo). The mutation that counts dishes only fails 18 assertions, all five among
them.

**Split, never filtered.** Every assigned row goes into exactly one list, by kind. Layer J's
conservation assertion accounts for all 26 rows of the fixture event (the host's own excepted)
exactly once, against its holder, in the list for its kind.

**Choices made in building — for the founder**
1. **The opt-out fact ([[GTC-301]]).** Opted out if `Person.smsOptedOut` is true OR an `SmsOptOut`
   row exists for the number under THIS event's host — per host, as `checkOptOut` in `sendSms` reads
   it, not account-wide as [[GTC-288]] rules the table will be. Pinned by two fixture people: Otto,
   refused by the table alone, and Pia, whose row is another host's and who is texted.
   **ANSWERED — per host, deliberately; [[GTC-288]] owns account-wide. Slice 3 answer 1.**
2. **The host as carrier (A2) is shown and not composed.** Her row names the child's ask she carries
   and nothing of hers. Her message's voice is decision 20 and her link is [[GTC-297]], so the screen
   says so rather than composing "Hi Kate - We're doing…" to her.
3. **Links, told as the press will issue them.** `READY`, `AT_PRESS`, `NONE_COORDINATOR`,
   `NONE_HOST_CARRIER`. The rule is read from `ensureEventTokens` step 4, not changed — Zone 3 not
   entered. A coordinator's message now ends `[no guest link yet]`, not the at-the-press stand-in,
   with an amber line naming them and [[GTC-294]]. Measured on either side of a real
   `ensureEventTokens` call. **ANSWERED — kept; [[GTC-294]] owns the fix. Slice 3 answer 2.**
4. **The host's third identity path** — a second Person on her `User` — is kept, as the old route had
   it: not a recipient, not a line.
5. **Words** — the host's-list reasons, the heading, the line under it, the reply-to line.
   **RULED, with four changes and Gather in the first person** — *Founder answers — the slice 3
   words*. The words now live in `src/lib/preflight/ask-preview-compose.ts` and the page renders
   them. The text-reply sentence was proposed because *"yet"* overstated, then ruled (second pass); the no-reply-to
   sentence was not named and is unchanged.
6. **Not touched:** step 3's blurb *"One channel per household, and how hard the system chases"*,
   listed by the audit as the other reading but outside slice 3's entry.
7. **The fixture writes non-host households directly**, because it needs stored `contactMethod`
   values that contradict the person; the host's household goes through `createHostHousehold`.

**What names look like on this screen — [[GTC-302]], left untidy on purpose.** From the walk:
*"Would you bring the Fruit-Based Mocktail Mix (Passionfruit and Mint Lemonade) and the L&P (Lemon &
Paeroa)?"*; *"Charlie has been asked to bring the Marlborough Sauvignon Blanc, the NZ Craft Beer and
Mainstream Lager Mix and the Rosé."*; *"Would you bring the BBQ (gas), the Chilly bins, the Serving
platters and utensils and the Trestle tables?"*. Capitals mid-sentence, qualifiers in brackets, and a
name that is already a list reads as more list. The seed board's host name is lower case too:
*"helping nigel with this one"*.

## Evidence (slice 4a) — Executor-Completed

> **Slice 4a of eight — the migration, and nothing else in its commit.** GTC-189 stays open.
> Committed as `cb9ca01`. **4b, the email senders returning Resend's id, is a separate signature
> and is not built.**

**What was built**
Schema and one migration. `OutboundMessage` — the per-recipient outbound record — plus
`Event.chaseWhenNoMobileDefault` and `PersonEvent.chaseException`, both `ChaseWhenNoMobile?`, and
three enums: `OutboundKind`, `OutboundChannel`, `ChaseWhenNoMobile`. **Nothing reads or writes any
of it.** No route, no dispatcher, no resolver, no preview change, no chooser change. The two chase
columns are storage for [[GTC-311]]'s control and slice 8's reader; the outbound row is what slice
5's dispatcher will drain and what slice 6 will match a bounce against.

**The shape was proposed and held before anything was generated**, on the founder's instruction, and
two questions were raised at the proposal rather than discovered inside the migration — decisions 27
and 28, both ruled the same day. That ordering is the point: a migration is the one phase where a
question found late costs a second rehearsal.

**The four founder rulings this slice carries**
In full at the slice 4 build-shape entry above, and in the schema docstrings where the next reader
meets them:
1. **Decision 27 — NO STORED BODY.** *"The record says what was sent, to whom, on which channel,
   and what came back. Not the words."* Ruled rather than inferred from the approved column list,
   which is the question `Event.askAuthorLine`'s docstring defers here. Two consequences recorded as
   the price: the pre-flight preview is the only account of what each person was asked, and ruling
   U's *"send again"* recomposes from today's plan rather than resending what was sent.
2. **Decision 28 — CASCADE from `PersonEvent`.** *"A row about a person no longer on the event is
   worse than a missing row."*
3. **The three executor judgement calls kept as proposed** — `eventId` denormalised but
   single-written; the three attempt columns; and **two outcome doors rather than one**, which is the
   one the founder named as mattering most: *"'Why would the provider not take it' and 'why did
   Gather not send it' are two questions, and collapsing them is the exact family this week keeps
   catching."*
4. **The production rehearsal is a precondition on THIS PHASE, not only on the deploy** — see
   *Migration rehearsal* below.

**⚠ The polarity, and what was done about it in the schema**
Ruling AH inverted the per-person control on 2026-09-15 and every sentence written under ruling AG
still reads correctly. **So there is no boolean anywhere in this migration.** `ChaseWhenNoMobile`
holds `BY_EMAIL` and `HAND_TO_HOST`, each naming its own behaviour, because a boolean can be read in
either polarity and a field name, comment or test label about one would read perfectly well while
meaning the opposite. Two values and not one, because ruling AL rules both directions.

Ruling AL is honoured in the words as well: **THE NAMED EXCEPTION WINS** appears three times, and
**neither "ladder" nor "quieter-wins" appears anywhere in the commit** — verified by grep over the
added lines. The docstring gives the founder's reason rather than only the outcome, and points at
`resolveDecideByOffsetHours` in `src/lib/decide-by.ts` as the structural sibling — per-item, else
per-event, else system — rather than at `resolveNudgeOffsetDays`, which must go on implementing the
intensity axis's rule for the intensity axis.

**Files changed**
- `prisma/schema.prisma` — `OutboundMessage`; `OutboundKind`, `OutboundChannel`,
  `ChaseWhenNoMobile`; `Event.chaseWhenNoMobileDefault` and a back-relation; and
  `PersonEvent.chaseException` and a back-relation. 337 insertions and no semantic deletions —
  `git diff -w` reports insertions only. The 13 lines git shows as deleted are `prisma format`
  realigning `PersonEvent`'s attribute column, because `ChaseWhenNoMobile?` is longer than any type
  that was there before.
- `prisma/migrations/20260918051158_gtc189_slice4_outbound_message_and_chase_columns/migration.sql`
- **Not touched:** `src/`, `tests/`, `package.json`, and every other ticket. The documentation
  belonging to this slice is the commit after it, so the migration commit carries nothing else —
  [[GTC-264]] phase 1's precedent at `a84aa53`, and the house convention.

**Decisions that shaped the column set, recorded because the reasoning outlives the model**
- **No `@@unique`, and `@@unique([personEventId, kind])` is the one that looks right and is wrong.**
  It would make the press idempotent at the row level, and then ruling U's three bounce-door actions
  — each a new row of the same kind for the same membership — would fail the save. [[GTC-264]] phase
  1 finding 2's asymmetry, applied to a lifetime rather than a vocabulary: a wrong constraint is
  unrecoverable, duplicate rows are not.
- **The claim is set BEFORE the provider is called.** `dispatchPendingWrapUpMessages` marks its rows
  after sending, so a crash between the send and the mark re-sends on the next tick and two
  overlapping cron runs both send. For the wrap-up thank-you that is a duplicate thank-you; for the
  press it is a second invitation to everyone in the window, against a press ruled one act with no
  recall. This is the one place the model departs from the house dispatcher pattern deliberately.
- **Ordinal chase legs, never day-named**, for GTC-178 Ruling 7's reason. Two legs covers every
  cadence `NUDGE_PACE_OFFSET_DAYS` and `NUDGE_MARK_OFFSET_DAYS` resolve to, and maps one-to-one onto
  `firstNudgeSentAt`/`secondNudgeSentAt`.
- **`COORDINATOR_BRIEF` is present and unwritten**, for the reason ruling W gave about it. Its
  existence is the ruling's; its spelling is the executor's, and [[GTC-299]] may rename it before
  anything writes it.
- **An enum for `channel`, not a String, on the tree's own evidence:** `WrapUpLink.channel` is a
  String and has grown `'skipped'`, which is not a channel and is read as one in two places.
- **`withheldWhy` is a String and the whys are typed in TypeScript**, following `HostListWhy` and
  `ChaseNoneWhy` in `channel-chooser.ts` and the ledger's own actions behind
  `AuditEntry.actionType String`. That keeps a vocabulary slice 5 has not finished discovering out
  of the migration.
- **A typed column and not `EventSetup` JSONB** for the event default — the same question the
  founder ruled for `nudgePace` (GTC-179 decision 10 and Ruling 5), now counted: gather_dev holds
  **29 Events and 7 `EventSetup` rows**, so 22 of 29 would need a row created purely to hold a

- **No backfill, and the reason differs from `nudgePace`'s.** For `nudgePace`, *"null is existing
  behaviour"*. Here it is not — the chase has no email leg in code at all. No backfill is needed
  because nothing reads the column until slice 8, and every existing row then wants exactly the
  ruled default.

**Migration rehearsal (Stop Condition 7)**
Generated with `prisma migrate dev --create-only --skip-generate` and **held unapplied for the
founder to read the SQL**, then rehearsed, then applied.

Rehearsed on `gather_rehearsal_189` — a `pg_dump --no-owner --no-acl` clone of `gather_dev` carrying
29 Events, 333 People, 335 PersonEvents, 45 InviteEvents, 185 Assignments and 43 applied migrations
— via `prisma migrate deploy`, **deliberately not `migrate dev`**, which can offer to RESET a
transferred database. Findings:

- Applied cleanly. The SQL is **purely additive**: three `CREATE TYPE`, two `ADD COLUMN`, one
  `CREATE TABLE`, five `CREATE INDEX`, two `ADD CONSTRAINT` on the new and empty table. No `UPDATE`,
  no `DELETE`, nothing dropped, no type changed.
- **No table rewrite, measured rather than asserted.** `pg_class.relfilenode` for `Event` (26916)
  and `PersonEvent` (27023) is identical before and after, so both `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN`
  statements were metadata-only. **That is what locates the risk: it is the `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock
  queue, not a rewrite.**
- Row counts unchanged afterwards.
- **Eight behaviour probes, each in a transaction rolled back, counts confirmed intact after:** an
  insert on a real membership succeeds; **two `ASK` rows for the same membership both insert**, which
  is ruling U's resend and the case a unique constraint would have broken; the guarded claim returns
  one row and then zero, with `attemptCount` at 1; deleting the `PersonEvent` leaves zero outbound
  rows (decision 28); deleting the `Event` leaves zero outbound rows; the foreign key refuses an
  orphan membership id by name; and the enum refuses `CHASE_THIRD` by name, so a wrong `kind` is a
  database error rather than a stored string nobody notices.
- `migrate status` on the clone: 44, up to date. `migrate diff`: *"No difference detected."*
- Scratch database dropped.

Then applied to `gather_dev` with **`prisma migrate deploy`** and the client regenerated afterwards,
per KB-005 and the local-setup notes' order — migrations first, generate second, because
regenerating alone moves the client further ahead of the database. Verified on `gather_dev`: **44
migrations applied**, *"Database schema is up to date!"*, `migrate diff` reports no difference,
`OutboundMessage` carries its five indexes and two foreign keys and is empty, both new columns are
nullable with no default and NULL on all 29 Events and all 335 PersonEvents, the three existing
`nudgeMark` values are untouched, and Person / InviteEvent / Assignment / SmsDeliveryReport counts
are unchanged at 333 / 45 / 185 / 0.

⚠ **THE PRODUCTION REHEARSAL IS OWED, AND IT IS A PRECONDITION ON THIS PHASE RATHER THAN ONLY ON
THE DEPLOY — which corrects how [[GTC-264]] recorded its own.** GTC-264's migration altered no
existing table, so *purely additive* and *harmless* were the same sentence. **This one alters two
live tables**, `Event` and `PersonEvent`, and once an `ACCESS EXCLUSIVE` lock can queue behind a
long-running query they stop being the same sentence. What can and cannot be rehearsed from here,
said plainly:
- **CAN:** a `gather_dev` clone, as above. That is what was done.
- **CANNOT:** a copy of production. `.env` and `.env.local` both point at

  Railway CLI installed, and there is no production dump anywhere on it. `gather_dev` is fixtures and
  seed data moved between machines — ruling C established that it measures drift rather than capture.
- **SO: SLICE 4 IS NOT DEPLOYABLE AND IS NOT REPORTED AS SUCH.** The founder will supply a production

  nothing on `feat/moment-one-redesign` has deployed in weeks, so the rehearsal owed is of the whole

**⚠ A false-positive check of my own, recorded because it is this week's family in a new place.**
A first NUL-byte check was written as `grep -qU $'\x00'` in the Bash tool's zsh. **A shell cannot
pass a NUL in an argument, so the pattern was EMPTY and matched every file** — it reported a NUL in
all six changed files, including the generated migration SQL. Re-run through Python against the raw
bytes, all six are clean. It is [[GTC-273]]'s shape exactly: a check that examined something adjacent
to the thing it claimed to examine, and it failed loudly only because its answer was absurd. **A
quieter version of the same defect would have passed.** The rule this leaves beside KB-006's: a check
for a byte cannot be written in a language that cannot hold that byte.

**What was built**
Three code lines. `SendResult` in `src/lib/email.ts` gains `providerMessageId?: string`;
`resultOf`'s `response` parameter gains `data?: { id?: string } | null`; and the success return reads

and what this ticket's *Also found* answered with *"no"*.

**Why it is slice 4's and not slice 6's** — recorded in the `SendResult` docstring, where someone
reading the field meets it rather than having to find it here. **Slice 5's dispatcher writes this
value onto `OutboundMessage.providerMessageId` at acceptance, so the sender has to return it before

never touches this return value.** The consumer is slice 5. That holds even though slices 5, 6 and 7
land as one release, because the dependency is in the code and not in the release date.

asserted rather than asserted-of: see layer 2b's last two assertions.

**⚠ NEVER A PLACEHOLDER ON FAILURE**, and this is the half that matters more. A rejected send has no
provider id. Slice 6 joins on this value, so a stand-in would match nothing and read as a **lost
bounce** rather than as a send that never happened. Pinned by an assertion and by mutation M2.

### ⚠ Finding — TWO checks caught today only because their results were checked rather than believed

*Recorded together, because they are one defect in two places and the pairing is the point. Neither
was found by being careful; both were found because an answer was read instead of accepted.*

**1. M2's substitution did not land, and the run after it read exactly GREEN.**

`return { success: false, error: message };` occurs **three times** in `src/lib/email.ts` — once in
`resultOf` and once in each sender's `catch`. The substitution asserted on a unique match, aborted,
and left the file unmutated. The suite then ran and reported **25/27: byte-identical to GREEN.**

**Read as a mutation result, that says the assertion is weak and the placeholder is unpinned. It says
nothing of the kind. The mutation was never there.** Retargeted on the surrounding context, verified
present by grep at line 89, and it then failed the one assertion it should and nothing else.

**⚠ THIS IS [[GTC-277]] FINDING 8'S RULE BITING IN THE FILE THAT RECORDS IT.** That finding is
titled *"a mutation that reports nothing has TWO explanations"*, and
`tests/email-send-result-test.ts` already carries a twenty-line comment about the same thing
happening to GTC-265's own mutation M3, ending: *"a mutation that reports no failures has made two
claims, not one — that the assertion is weak, AND that the mutation applied. Verify the second before
believing the first."* **It happened again, in that file, to the next person to run a mutation
against it.** The comment is not enough; only the aborting assertion caught it.

⚠ **A citation to correct, and not here.** That comment attributes the rule to *"GTC-267's"*.
[[GTC-267]] is the unauthenticated-reads ticket, whose evidence merely lists mutation results;
**the rule is [[GTC-277]] Finding 8's.** Left as written because it is [[GTC-265]]'s prose and not
slice 4b's to rewrite — named so whoever owns it can fix it, per the Citations rule.

**2. The NUL-byte check, at slice 4a, earlier the same day.**

Written as `grep -qU $'\x00'` under the Bash tool's zsh. A shell cannot pass a NUL in an argument, so
**the pattern was EMPTY and matched every file** — it reported a NUL byte in all six changed files,
generated migration SQL included. Re-run in Python against the raw bytes, all six are clean.

**What the two have in common, and the rule they leave.** Both are checks whose *result* was
implausible enough to be looked at — six files out of six including one Prisma had just written; a
mutation run identical to GREEN to the assertion. **A quieter version of either would have passed
and been believed.** The NUL grep hitting one file, or the mutation shifting one unrelated assertion,
and neither would have been questioned. So the rule is not *be careful writing checks*; it is:

> **A check's output is evidence about the check as well as about the code. Read it as both. And a
> claim that a check can only make if its own instrument worked — a mutation's absence of failures,
> a grep's absence of matches — is a claim about the instrument first.**

It is the same family as [[GTC-273]]'s detector matching the spelling of a credential rather than
evaluating it, [[GTC-276]]'s detector inheriting the defect the ticket that wrote it had just named,
ruling AN's two refusals sharing a why-code, and ruling AH's measurement reading an answer's kind and
never its channel. **Five instances now sit in this ledger and two of them are from today.**

---

### ⚠ The honest limit — what this change cannot prove, and what closes it

**What is proven:** the read of Resend's documented success envelope. The SDK's own type is
`CreateEmailResponseSuccess { id: string }`, and the suite's success stub already models it — it
hands back a real `Response` carrying `{ id }`, which is what the SDK parses. So no new fixture and
no key were needed, and 4b is verifiable entirely offline.

**What CANNOT be proven here:** that Resend actually returns `data.id` on a real accepted send.
[[GTC-247]] records that this environment's `RESEND_API_KEY` does not authenticate, and layer 4's own
precondition **asserts that the provider rejects it**. A rejected send has no id to return. So the
live half is unprovable on this machine, and no claim is made about it.

**⚠ AND THE LIMIT UNDERNEATH THAT ONE, STATED BECAUSE IT IS THE ONE THAT COULD BITE. The stub models
Resend's success envelope from the SDK's TYPE rather than from an OBSERVED response.** If the real
body were shaped otherwise, the code and the stub would be wrong together and the suite would not
notice — [[GTC-264]]'s *Isolate the wire shape* concern, in a smaller place and without its
machinery. **Three lines do not earn a provenance record.** What closes it is the first live send on
a working key and a verified domain, which needs no code change; and **[[GTC-289]]'s investigation
is where that shape gets read properly**, because it must read Resend's event vocabulary against the
documentation before it designs anything.

---

Founder's call, recorded in the founder's terms: three lines behind an optional field that
typechecks cannot break a build, and KB-005 means proving it costs stopping the dev server that the
157-assertion security suite needs up. `npx tsc --noEmit` exits 0, no caller changed, no module
boundary moved, and no route or component is in the commit. The build was exercised at **slice 4a**,
hours earlier and against the larger change, exit 0 with the server stopped and `.next` removed.

- [x] NO CALLER READS IT, asserted over the four files that call the three senders. Deliberately not

      `src/lib/sms/tnz-delivery-contract.ts`, which is [[GTC-264]]'s SMS side and a different

## Evidence (slice 5a) — Executor-Completed

> **Slice 5a of eight, and the first piece of the press. GTC-189 stays open.** One route
> (decision 30), renamed (decision 31), writing the lock, one `OutboundMessage` per addressed
> recipient and the ledger's first entry — **inside one transaction, and sending nothing.**
> The dispatcher is 5c and does not exist, so every row this writes is unclaimed and
> unfinished.

### Preflight

- `npm install` — exit 0, audit advisories only, no peer-dep errors.
- `npm run db:migrate` — *"Already in sync, no schema change or pending migration was found."*
  Client regenerated, exit 0. **Slice 5a carries no migration**; slice 4a's is what it writes to.
- Boot — a dev server was already listening on `:3000` and was probed (`GET /api/events`
  answering 401 with no cookie) before any HTTP result was trusted.
- `npm run test:security` — **159/159**, exit 0, before and after.
- `npx tsc --noEmit` — exit 0 before and after.
- Tree at the start: `d14f026` = [[GTC-320]], built first in this session on founder instruction.

### The eight founder answers of 2026-09-19, and where each one went

**Built here, five of them:**

1. **Q1 — SIX MEMBERSHIPS, NOT EIGHT.** *"The outbound row is addressed to someone, and the
   pre-flight's own arithmetic says '8 messages' — two of those eight are not messages. It also
   makes the withheld count a truthful answer to 'how many did not go out'."*
   Implemented as: a recipient gets a row **iff the press has a link to put in their message**.
   `ensureEventTokens` runs first, inside the transaction, so the only states left are
   `NONE_HOST_CARRIER` ([[GTC-297]] owns her link) and `NONE_NOT_ISSUED`. Neither is a message,
   and `composeAsk` could not compose one for them anyway — its `link` is a required string.
   ⚠ **AND DECISION 29 IS SATISFIED EITHER WAY, RECORDED SEPARATELY AS INSTRUCTED:** *"The
   pre-flight is what makes it honest; this is bookkeeping."* It is in the module docstring
   and in the suite's own header, so no reader takes the row set for the thing that tells her
   anything.
2. **Q2 — THE RECIPIENT COUNT INSIDE THE TRANSACTION.** *"A recipient count written outside it
   is a number that can disagree with the rows it counts."* `SEND_PRESSED`'s `recipients` is
   `addressed.length`, written by `recordChange` in the same transaction as the `createMany`.
   ⚠ **It is also a DIFFERENT and truer number than the old route wrote.** That one counted
   every addressable membership through `isAddressable`; this counts the messages that exist.
   Under Q1 those differ, and the difference is exactly the states that are not messages.
3. **Q3 — REFUSE ON UNHEALTHY, FAIL CLOSED.** `RECIPIENTS_UNAVAILABLE` exists as a refusal.
   The mechanism is a `PressRefused` thrown from inside the transaction and unwrapped outside
   it, because a `return` would commit what came before and a bare `throw` would reach the
   route as a 500 — the *"500-then-nothing"* shape the founder named.
4. **Q4 — 409 ON A STATE REFUSAL**, with the reason recorded in the type's own docstring: *"a
   402 says pay, a 403 says you may not, and neither is true of an event that is not ready."*
   404 is kept for an event that is not there, because that is not a state refusal.
   ⚠ **AND IT MOVED TWO EXISTING REFUSALS FROM 400** — the second press and the
   not-CONFIRMING guard, which are state refusals by exactly that ground. **Measured before
   changing them:** no suite pinned the status, and neither live caller branches on it —
   `InviteStatusSection` renders `data.error` on any `!res.ok`, and the host view ignores the
   response entirely. Named rather than left to be found. If only the NEW refusal was meant,
   say so and the two go back.
5. **Q5 — RENAME NOW, AND TAKE THE REDIRECT.** `POST /api/events/[id]/send` and
   `POST /api/h/[token]/send`. ⚠ **THE REDIRECT IS IN `next.config.js`, NOT IN TWO ROUTE
   HANDLERS, AND THAT CHANGE WAS FORCED BY A MEASUREMENT — see finding 3 below.**

**Recorded here and built in the slices after this one, three of them:**

6. **Q6 — TAKE THE SLOWER PATH.** *"A shape that works in this environment because nothing
   authenticates is a shape nobody has seen work. Build the dispatcher properly and accept
   that its acceptance path is unproven until a key exists."* That is slice 5c and 5d. What it
   costs is under **What remains unproven**, below, which is the ⚠ the founder attached to it.
7. **Q7 — NO FAKE.** *"A fake provider that never ships is a second code path nobody
   exercises, and the first thing it hides is the one thing we most need to see: what the
   board looks like when everything fails."* The executor's seam proposal is **withdrawn**.
   Layer N asserts the consequence now rather than at 5c: the press module names no
   `NODE_ENV`, `MOCK`, `FAKE` or `DRY_RUN`, so there is no second code path in existence to
   forget to turn off.
   ⚠ **ONE DISTINCTION KEPT, BECAUSE Q7 DOES NOT REACH IT.** A fake PROVIDER is refused. A
   fake RECIPIENT is not the same object: this slice's fixture addresses `@example.com`, which
   RFC 2606 reserves and guarantees undeliverable. That is how the press is walked at all
   without addressing the 37 real consumer mailboxes on the only pressable board in
   `gather_dev`. Named so the two are not conflated later.
8. **Q8 — THE LEDGER DUPLICATION FIRST.** Done: [[GTC-320]] is built and committed at
   `d14f026`, ahead of this slice.

### What was built

- **`src/lib/press/press.ts` — `pressSend`, the one writer.** Both doors call it; each keeps
  its own auth, which is the one thing they must not share. Refusals are a typed union with a
  `code` beside the prose, so [[GTC-309]]'s screen can branch on the state without parsing a
  sentence.
- **Inside one transaction, in this order:** `ensureEventTokens`; `readAskPreview` (so routing
  and tokens are decided at the same instant); `Event.sentAt`; one `OutboundMessage` per
  addressed recipient; the `inviteAnchorAt` sweep; `recordChange` with `SEND_PRESSED`; and
  `logInviteEvent` **with the `tx` passed** — both old routes declined to pass one, so a
  rolled-back press could leave an `INVITE_SEND_CONFIRMED` row claiming a send that never
  happened.
- **The transaction takes `{ timeout: 30_000, maxWait: 15_000 }`**, because the default is 5s
  and this transaction does a write per tokenless membership, a full preview walk, a
  `createMany`, an anchor sweep and `recordChange`'s sequence allocation. Named under *What I
  cannot size*.
- **Two new routes**, each auth and a call: `src/app/api/events/[id]/send/route.ts`
  (`requireEventRole`, with the auth check outside any try that returns 500) and
  `src/app/api/h/[token]/send/route.ts` (`resolveToken` + HOST scope).
- **The two old paths are a `redirects()` entry in `next.config.js`**, 307 so the method
  survives the hop. The two old route FILES are deleted.

### What came out

- **`personEvent.updateMany` — both routes.** Ruling G. Nothing breaks in the drain window:
  `findNudgeCandidates` records `sentAt: { not: null }` as *"A FAIL-SAFE, NOT A TIDY-UP"*.
  ⚠ **The consequence carried forward:** a WITHHELD or REJECTED recipient never gets a
  `PersonEvent.sentAt`, so they are never chased, ever — correct, and it makes the
  `OutboundMessage` row the only record they were meant to be asked.
- **`isMiniSend` — REMOVED, not redefined (decision 33).** The function, suite 5 of
  `tests/lifecycle-predicates-test.ts` (28 → 24 assertions) and the comment inside the old
  route, with the `updateMany` it explained. **Two tombstones left in its place**, naming the
  ruling that killed it, in `src/lib/lifecycle.ts` and in the suite — the house shape, as
  `removePerson()` has. So the assertion holding decision 33 is about the EXPORT and the
  CALLS, never the word; see finding 2.
- **`isAddressable` from the press.** ⚠ Not a weakening: the host's exclusion is inside
  `chooseAskRoute`, which answers `NOT_A_RECIPIENT / HOST_OWN_ASK` for her own ask and admits
  her only as a carrier under ruling A2. One predicate, every caller — deeper than the inline
  filter, not looser. `tests/host-never-messaged-test.ts`'s guarded-file list follows it to
  `src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts`, with the reason written in place.

### What was deliberately NOT done

- **The Auto-Assign tombstone stays.** GTC-189's build shape says the `!isSent &&` in
  `src/components/plan/PeopleSection.tsx` comes out *"when this ticket lands"*. It has not:
  5a is the route, dark; the dispatcher is 5c and the mini-send path is 5e. And the tombstone's
  **second** condition, [[GTC-178]] / E1, is not built at all. Deleting it today would offer a
  button creating N asks that reach nobody — the exact failure it was written against. **The
  comment is amended in place** to say which half is met, rather than left for the next reader
  to work out from two ticket numbers.
- **The pre-flight Send button is still wired to nothing** — 5f. `tests/ask-preview-test.ts`
  already asserts that page references neither path, and it still holds.
- **`Person.inviteAnchorAt` still written at the press, and it now disagrees with
  `PersonEvent.sentAt` by the drain window.** Three surfaces read it — the plan page's

### Mutation checks

Five, each verified to have LANDED by grep before its result was read, each restored
byte-identical by `diff` — [[GTC-277]] Finding 8's rule, which slice 4b re-learned the hard way.

- **M1 — the link filter deleted** (`preview.recipients` in place of the `READY` filter) →

- **M2 — the press stamps `PersonEvent.sentAt` again** → fails exactly 2: the structural

- **M3 — the press takes the dispatcher's claim** (`attemptedAt: now` on the created rows) →
