### ⚠ FINDING 1 — the fixture proved the COUNT and never proved the FILTER, and only a mutation said so

**M1 deleted the link filter outright and the suite reported 52 passed, 0 failed.** The
substitution was verified present by grep first, so [[GTC-277]] Finding 8's two explanations
reduce to one: **the assertion was weak.**

**Why.** The fixture had six recipients and six addressed recipients — nobody in it was a
recipient the press has no link for. So `preview.recipients` and the filtered list were **the
same list**, and founder Q1's whole distinction was unexercised. The assertion said *six rows
for six memberships the press has a link for* and it was true for a reason that had nothing to
do with the filter.

**The repair is in the fixture, not the assertion.** Household B now picks the **HOST** as its
contact and holds a child with an item — ruling A2, the one case in the model where the host
receives an ask, and therefore the one recipient with `linkState: 'NONE_HOST_CARRIER'`. The
fixture is now **seven recipients, six addressed**, a control asserts that exactly one is
unaddressed, and the headline assertion reads *six rows for seven recipients*. M1 then fails
two assertions and nothing else.

⚠ **AND THE RULE IT LEAVES, which is not the one already in this ledger.** The existing pair
is *a check can fail by not reading the field* and *a check can read every field and attribute
the answer wrongly*. This is a third: **a check can be exactly right about the case it was
given and never be given the case it exists for.** A count assertion over a set where the
filter is a no-op is not a weak assertion — it is an assertion about a different question, and
it reads as the right one. Nothing in the GREEN output could have shown it. **Only a mutation
that should have failed and did not.** That is the same detection route as the second finding
of the slice 5 shape proposal (*"caught only by a change that should move the number and does
not"*), arriving from the opposite direction: there a fix failed to move a number, here a
break failed to move an assertion.

### ⚠ FINDING 2 — a matcher for a CALL that matched its own SENTENCE

Decision 33 removes `isMiniSend`, and the tree keeps a tombstone naming it — so the assertion
cannot be a string search. It was written as a call matcher, `/isMiniSend\s*\(\s*[^)\s]/`,
walking `src/` and `tests/`.

**It reported `tests/press-route-test.ts` as a caller.** The match was inside its own assertion
label: *"EXPORTS no isMiniSend (decision 33 — removed, not redefined)"*. **Prose puts a space
between a name and a bracket; code does not.** The `\s*` made a code matcher into a prose
matcher, and the file it accused was the file doing the accusing.

Two repairs, both kept: the pattern tightened to `/isMiniSend\([^)]/`, and a **third control**
asserting it matches a call, does **not** match the tombstone's `isMiniSend()`, and does **not**
match a sentence naming the function. The planted strings are built by concatenation, so this
file honestly contains no call and needs no exclusion list to rot.

**It is [[GTC-273]]'s family in a new place** — a check examining something adjacent to what it
claimed to examine — and it failed loudly only because its answer was absurd. A tree with one
real caller and this pattern would have reported two files and nobody would have read the second.

### ⚠ FINDING 3 — the redirect as two route handlers cost two unguarded handlers, and the scanner said so within a minute

The redirect was first built the obvious way: two `route.ts` files returning
`NextResponse.redirect(..., 307)`. It worked, and the press suite went green.

**`npm run test:security:routes` then failed 7 of its 118 pinned assertions**, including
*"exactly 12 handlers carry no guard and no other credential of any kind"* and *"the scanner
discovers exactly 106 route files"*. **Baseline confirmed rather than assumed**: the whole
change was stashed (`--include-untracked`) and the control re-run — **118/118**. All seven were
mine.

They were right. A redirect handler needs no auth — and *needs no auth* and *has no
credential of any kind* are the same row in that table. Two more entries in the population
[[GTC-267]], [[GTC-268]] and [[GTC-273]] spent three tickets counting down, for a file whose
whole job is to have no behaviour.

**Moved into `next.config.js` `redirects()`.** There is no handler, so there is nothing to
guard and nothing to count: **118/118 again, every pinned number where it was.** The two old
route files are deleted, the two new `send` routes replace them one for one, and both carry a
guard. The founder's *"take the redirect"* is honoured and the API surface does not grow by a
line.

⚠ **And the redirect is now asserted over HTTP rather than read off a source file**, because a
grep for the word *redirect* proves a word is present and not that **the method survives the
hop** — which is the only property the two live buttons depend on. Four assertions: 307 on both
paths, the `location` on both, and a followed POST landing on a route that answers **401**.

### ⚠ FINDING 4 — ruling AC's refusal was masked by a second refusal, and the mutation is what showed it

M5 disabled ruling AC's check and the suite failed **one** assertion, not two: the 409-status
assertion still passed. The no-account fixture event had only a HOST membership, so with AC
disabled the press ran on to `NO_RECIPIENTS` — **which is also a 409**. The status assertion was
true of a refusal that had nothing to do with ruling AC.

Two repairs: the event was given a real addressable guest with an item, so the only thing wrong
with it is the missing account; and the status assertion became a conjunction with the `code`,
so it cannot pass off another refusal's status. **The refusal ORDER is now observable too** — AC
fires before the transaction opens, and the recipient check inside it.

**It is the same shape as finding 1 in a different dress:** an assertion that was right about
the case it was handed, where the case could satisfy it for the wrong reason.

### ⚠ What is NOT built, and what it costs — the founder's ⚠ on Q6, answered precisely

Q6 asked for the named gap rather than the proven fiction. Precisely, then.

**What slice 5a proves, end to end, with no key and no provider:** the lock; the row set and
that it equals the addressed recipients; the channel stored from the chooser; that no row is
claimed, attempted, accepted, rejected or withheld; that no `PersonEvent.sentAt` is written;
that the ledger's count equals the rows; the five refusals and that each writes nothing; the
redirect's method; and that no provider and no fake is named anywhere in the module.

**What is not built:** 5b (decision 29's `LinkState` state), 5c (the dispatcher — cron route,
claim, the four gates, quiet hours, backoff, both transports), 5d (acceptance and rejection
writing, including `PersonEvent.sentAt`), 5e (the mini-send and ruling AJ's collapsed
pre-flight), 5f (the Send button and the threshold script).

**What will remain unproven after 5c and 5d are built, under Q7's "no fake" — the named gap:**

- **That a provider ever ACCEPTS.** [[GTC-247]]: `RESEND_API_KEY` is present in `.env.local`
  and does not authenticate. So `acceptedAt`, `provider`, `providerMessageId` and — through
  ruling G — **`PersonEvent.sentAt` itself** have no path to being written on this machine.
  The per-person clock, which is the whole of ruling G, is **unprovable here**. Slice 4b
  recorded the same limit for the id it returns; this is that limit reaching the column it
  feeds.
- **That the chase ever starts.** `findNudgeCandidates` gates on `sentAt: { not: null }`, so
  with no acceptance there is no nudgeable recipient, and slice 8's entry conditions cannot be

- **Any text at all.** `TNZ_AUTH_TOKEN` is absent from both `.env` and `.env.local`, so
  `sendSms` to a `+64` number returns `blocked: 'SMS_DISABLED'` **before any provider call**.
  ⚠ It therefore never even produces a provider error: it produces a WITHHOLDING shape at the
  provider-error door, which is the two-doors collapse ruling AN warns about, arriving as the

- **What a real rejection's words look like** — which is what `providerError` is shaped around,
  and which [[GTC-289]]'s investigation is where it gets read properly.
- **`EMAIL_FROM` is `"Gather <onboarding@resend.dev>"`**, Resend's sandbox sender. Ruling F's
  *"sent from our verified domain because it cannot be sent from hers"* has no verified domain
  behind it here, so the reply-to and display-name half of the ask email is unprovable too.

**What the rejected path DOES prove, which is Q7's own argument and is not small:** every row
carrying `rejectedAt` and a provider error is exactly the state slice 7's fourth red has to
read, and it is the state a fake would have hidden. The board-when-everything-fails is the one
thing this environment can show better than a working key would.

## Evidence (slice 5b) — Executor-Completed

> **Slice 5b of eight — decision 29, and what is left of it.** GTC-189 stays open. The
> pre-flight's own arithmetic now tells the truth about what the press will do, through **one
> predicate the press gates on**, and the press **refuses** a link issuance that did not happen
> rather than dropping it in silence. Still nothing sends.

### Preflight

- `npm run db:migrate` — *"Already in sync"*. 5b carries no migration.
- Dev server already up on `:3000`; probed before any HTTP result was trusted.
- `npm run test:security` **159/159** and `npm run test:security:routes` **118/118**, before and
  after. `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0 before and after.
- Tree at the start: `3e2a4a8`.

### ✅ DECISION 29 FOLLOWED TO ITS PURPOSE RATHER THAN ITS LETTER — RULED, NOT A DEPARTURE

Decision 29 says *"slice 3's `LinkState` has to carry it the way it already carries the
coordinator and host-carrier cases."* **No fifth `LinkState` member was added.**

⚠ **THIS WAS REPORTED AS A DEPARTURE AND THE FOUNDER RULED IT IS NOT ONE (2026-09-19).** Recorded
in the founder's words, because the distinction is the whole point — an executor reading this
later must not find a ruling honoured in outline and conclude the wording is negotiable in
general:

> NO FIFTH MEMBER — your judgement, confirmed. It follows the rule I set when I chose GTC-191's
> order: a state written after the gap closes is a state nobody has seen fire, and
> `NONE_COORDINATOR` was retired on exactly that logic. Decision 29's ground was the honesty of
> the screen, not a member of an enum, and the arithmetic is where the dishonesty actually lived.
>
> Record that the decision's wording was followed to its purpose rather than its letter, and that
> I ruled it so rather than leaving it as a departure.

**So the record is: the decision is MET, not bent.** Its ground — *"that is the fix that makes
withholding honest"* — is what was built, and the enum member was the mechanism the ruling
reached for when the gap it was ruled on still had a population. The ruling that governs the
choice is the founder's own from GTC-191's ordering, which this slice inherits rather than
invents.

⚠ **AND THE BOUNDARY, STATED SO IT IS NOT READ AS A LICENCE.** What made purpose-over-letter
legitimate here is that the letter's own subject had ceased to exist, measured — and that the
ground was written into the ruling in the same sentence. Where a ruling's letter still has a
population, the letter is the ruling. This is not a precedent for reading any instruction for its
intent; it is a precedent for reporting the mismatch and letting the founder rule, which is what
happened.

**The population it was ruled on is gone.** It was eighteen recipients in `gather_dev` carrying
thirty-four children's asks whose page returned only the link holder's own rows. [[GTC-191]]
built the answer to exactly that. Measured today: **still 18 carriers and 34 carried asks, and
their links now answer what their message asks.** So a fifth member would represent nothing —
which is the thing the founder warned against when ruling GTC-191's order: *"a state written
after the gap closes is a state nobody has seen fire."* `NONE_COORDINATOR` was retired by
[[GTC-294]] on the same logic.

**And the model already names what is left:** `NONE_HOST_CARRIER` ([[GTC-297]]) and
`NONE_NOT_ISSUED`, both first-class.

**So what decision 29 still buys is its own ground — *"the fix that makes withholding honest"* —
met by the arithmetic instead of by a member.** Which is where the real defect was.

### The defect, and the founder named it in passing

Founder Q1, 2026-09-19: *"the pre-flight's own arithmetic says '8 messages' — two of those eight
are not messages."*

- The count line read `rows.length`, which is every RECIPIENT.
- The no-link banner filtered `NONE_NOT_ISSUED` **alone**, so the host as carrier was **counted
  as a message AND went unnamed** — two halves of one defect.

⚠ **Since slice 5a that is a disagreement with the code, not only a wording problem.**
`pressSend` writes a row per recipient it holds a link for. The screen promised more messages
than the press sends, **and it was wrong in the direction that reassures** — the same direction
as decision 29's original finding, arriving in a number instead of a state.

### What was built

- **`pressWillMessage(linkState)` in `src/lib/preflight/ask-preview.ts`** — the one rule. True
  for `READY` and `AT_PRESS` (because that is what AT_PRESS *means*), false for the other two:
  a message needs a link to carry, and `composeAsk` says so in its type — `link` is a required
  string.
- **`NotMessagedLinkState`**, typed as `Exclude<LinkState, 'READY' | 'AT_PRESS'>` rather than
  listed, so a state added later is a compile error until somebody writes its words.
- **`messageRows` / `notMessagedRows` / `messageCountLine` / `NOT_MESSAGED_WHY`** in
  `ask-preview-compose.ts`. Both lists are returned: a recipient the press cannot message
  **stays on the screen** — the host as carrier is carrying a real child's ask and slice 3 shows
  her deliberately. What changes is that she is no longer counted as a message she will not get.
- **The page** counts messages, and its banner names **every** not-messaged recipient with a
  reason from the keyed map.
- **`src/lib/press/press.ts`** gates on `pressWillMessage` instead of its own
  `linkState === 'READY'`, and gains **`LINKS_NOT_ISSUED`**.

### ⚠ The silent drop slice 5a left, and why it is a refusal rather than a fix

`readAskPreview`'s `linkOf` **mirrors** `ensureEventTokens` step 4, and its own docstring
already warns that a restatement drifts — [[GTC-294]] is the occasion it drifted. The two do not
agree in every case:

- `linkOf` answers `AT_PRESS` for **any** PARTICIPANT or COORDINATOR membership.
- Step 4 mints only where `role === 'PARTICIPANT' && !coordinatorIds.has(personId)`, and
  `coordinatorIds` is built from every `Team.coordinatorId` unioned with every COORDINATOR
  membership, **with no role filter**. Step 4b reaches only `role === 'COORDINATOR'`.

**So a PARTICIPANT membership whose person is a team's `coordinatorId` is promised a link by the
screen and minted none by issuance — and slice 5a dropped them from the row set in silence.** No
row, no withholding, and a screen that had said they would be messaged. That is decision 29's
own failure mode arriving from inside the press.

**Measured in `gather_dev` on 2026-09-19: 0 such memberships. Latent, not live.** Two live routes
write a team with a host as its coordinator ([[GTC-294]]'s finding), so the shape is reachable
rather than theoretical. The press now refuses `LINKS_NOT_ISSUED`, 409, and the whole transaction
rolls back — **including the tokens `ensureEventTokens` had already minted**, because a refusal
that left tokens behind would be a partial press.

### Test results — RED first

- **RED — 31 assertions, 9 passed, 22 failed.**
- ⚠ **THREE OF THE NINE PASSERS WERE THE PRESS HALF, AND THEY PASSED BECAUSE SLICE 5a WAS
  ALREADY RIGHT.** *the press still succeeds*, *it wrote two rows for three recipients*, and
  *the ledger's count equals the rows* were all green before this slice. That is the finding in
  one line: **the press's arithmetic was correct at 5a and the screen's was not**, so 5b is a
  screen change, one shared predicate and one refusal — not a change to what the press sends.
- The other six passers are the two harness controls, the two fixture controls, the page-read
  control, and the `LinkState` declaration control.
- **GREEN — 35/35** (31 at RED plus four behavioural assertions added by finding 3).

### Mutation checks

Five, each verified landed by grep before its result was read, each restored byte-identical.

- **M1 — the predicate lets the host as carrier through** (`linkState !== 'NONE_NOT_ISSUED'`) →
  **fails 6**, including *the press still succeeds*. ⚠ That failure is the new refusal working:
  with her counted as intended, `addressed` and `intended` differ and the press refuses instead
  of writing three rows. **One edit in one file moved the screen and the send together**, which
  is what "one predicate" means and what no structural assertion can show.
- **M2 — the page counts `rows.length` again** → fails exactly 1, the count-line assertion.
- **M3 — `notMessagedRows` returns `[]`** → fails exactly 1, the complement assertion.
- **M4 — the `LINKS_NOT_ISSUED` throw deleted** → fails exactly 2. ⚠ **See finding 3: the first
  run of M4 reported NOTHING.**
- **M5 — a fifth `LinkState` member added.** → fails the four-member assertion, **and `tsc`
  refuses the tree**:
  `error TS2741: Property 'NONE_SOME_NEW_GAP' is missing in type '{ NONE_HOST_CARRIER: string;
  NONE_NOT_ISSUED: string; }' but required in type 'Record<NotMessagedLinkState, string>'`.
  ⚠ **That is the "every case has words" guard PROVEN rather than asserted, and the founder
  instructed it be recorded as such (2026-09-19):** *"record it as the guard proven rather than
  asserted. `ADULT_WHY` has carried the same claim since slice 3 with nothing demonstrating it,
  and now something does."*
  The docstring claims a new state cannot ship without words; M5 is the evidence, and it is the
  first time in this ticket that a `Record`-keyed-on-a-union guard has been demonstrated rather
  than described. **A pointer is added at `ADULT_WHY` itself**, so the next reader of the older
  claim finds the demonstration rather than taking the sentence on trust.
  ⚠ **And the demonstration is a TYPE-CHECK failure, not an assertion failure**, which is why no
  suite could ever have produced it: the guard's whole value is that the tree does not compile, so
  the only instrument that can read it is `tsc`. A mutation table that only runs suites cannot see
  a compile-time guard at all — recorded because every other mutation in this ticket was measured
  by a suite.

### ⚠ FINDING 1 — a declaration parser that a comment's semicolon truncated

The four-member assertion sliced the raw source from `export type LinkState` to the first `;`.
**The first docstring inside the declaration contains a semicolon** — *"A PARTICIPANT token
exists; `link` is its URL"* — so the slice stopped there, the member list came back empty, and
the assertion reported the wrong count with complete confidence.

It failed at GREEN, which is the only reason it was looked at. **A tree with three members and
this instrument would have read zero and failed identically**, and the diagnosis would have been
the code.

Repaired by stripping comments before slicing, in a named helper, and **the control that caught
it is kept as an assertion**: the reader must find all four members BY NAME, not just count
four. `stripComments` existed in the file and was not used on this one path — which is the
smallest possible version of the family, and it is the family.

### ⚠ FINDING 2 — the press was right and the screen was wrong, and the RED run is what said so

Three assertions passed at RED. On any other slice that is a warning; here it is the result.
Slice 5a's press already wrote two rows for three recipients, because Q1's rule was implemented
in `pressSend` on the day. **What had never been corrected was the screen**, which is the
surface decision 29 is about: *"the pre-flight must show it BEFORE the press, not after."*

**The rule it leaves:** when a two-sided disagreement is found, the RED run says which side was
already right. Both sides looked equally suspect from the ticket; only the run distinguished
them, and it changed the slice from *make the press honest* to *make the screen agree with a
press that already was*.

### ⚠ FINDING 3 — a refusal asserted by its NAME, which survived the refusal being deleted

M4 deleted the `throw new PressRefused(... 'LINKS_NOT_ISSUED' ...)` outright. **The suite
reported 31 passed, 0 failed.** The substitution was verified landed first, so the assertion was
weak — and the reason is exact: both assertions about the refusal were **source greps**, and the
string `LINKS_NOT_ISSUED` still appeared in the `PressRefusalCode` union. **The name of a
refusal is not the refusal.**

Repaired behaviourally, with **the real mechanism rather than a contrived one**: a fixture whose
PARTICIPANT member is also a team's `coordinatorId`, so the screen promises him `AT_PRESS` (a
control asserts it) and issuance mints him nothing. Four assertions: the refusal fires, its code
and status together, and the transaction rolls back leaving no row, no `sentAt` and **no
tokens**. M4 then fails exactly two.

⚠ **AND THE MASKING CAME BACK A THIRD TIME.** Under the repaired M4, *"and it is a 409"* still
passed — because with the link check gone the event refuses `NO_RECIPIENTS`, which is **also a
409**. Every state refusal is a 409 by founder Q4, so **a status assertion alone can always be
satisfied by a different refusal.** Fixed as a conjunction with the code, the third time in this
slice pair: slice 5a's M5 was the same shape, and so was the AC fixture that needed a real guest
before its refusal could be unmasked. **The rule: where one status covers a family of refusals,
the status is never the assertion — the code is, and the status rides with it.**

per the Citations rule that a provisional marker names the ticket that ends it — and the founder's
reason is kept in the module beside them, because *why* the line stops where it does is the part a
later editor would otherwise undo: it tells her where to look and does not explain why, which is
this screen's register.

Every other word on this screen was founder-ruled at its slice. The three:

## Evidence (slice 5c, FIRST HALF) — Executor-Completed

> **The cron route and the claim.** GTC-189 stays open. ⚠ **The route takes NO CLAIM and sends
> nothing** — it reports what a tick would drain. The claim primitives are built and proven against
> real rows. **The second half is held on the founder's instruction until the two live buttons are
> ruled.**

### Preflight

- `npm run db:migrate` — *"Already in sync"*. 5c carries no migration.
- Dev server up on `:3000`, probed before any HTTP result was trusted.
- `npm run test:security` **159/159** and `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0, before and after.
- `npm run test:security:routes` **118/118** before; it moved during the build and was brought
  back to 118/118 — see *The route-scan control*, below.
- Tree at the start: `3fe8daa`.

### ⚠ WHY THIS HALF CLAIMS NOTHING, WHICH IS THE ONE DESIGN DECISION TO READ FIRST

The founder asked for *"the cron route and the claim"*. The claim is built and proven. **The route
does not call it**, and the reason is the schema's own sentence:

> *"a row left with `attemptedAt` set and no end state below is a crashed attempt — a visible fact
> rather than a silent resend."*

A route that claimed rows before a transport existed would turn **every row it touched** into a
permanent crashed attempt, on a press ruled one act with no recall. There is no honest
`withheldWhy` for *"the dispatcher is not finished"*, and inventing one would put a false sentence
in the column ruling AN separated from `providerError` to keep true.

So: `describeDrain` reads both passes and reports them; the claim primitives sit beside it, proven
by the suite against real rows; and **they go live in the same commit as the thing that can finish a
row.** The route says so on the wire as well as in a comment — `drainsNothingYet: true` — because a
monitor reading 200 with `sent: 0` for a week should be able to tell *nothing was due* from
*nothing can send yet*.

### What was built

- **`src/app/api/cron/outbound-dispatch/route.ts`** — GET and POST, GTC-270's shape exactly: the
  secret at module scope, then `isCronSecretConfigured` with an error-level log, then
  `cronSecretAccepted` with a warn, **both refusing `if`s written out in the route file**. Not
  behind a helper, and the reason is a measurement rather than taste: `cron-secret.ts` records that
  GTC-268's scanner reads the `if` conditions inside a handler and does not follow imports, and a
  helper that swallowed the check reported all six cron handlers as *"no credential of any kind"*.
  Response through `withoutRecipientNames`.
- **`vercel.json`** — `*/2 * * * *`. ⚠ **A proposal with no measurement behind it**, ruled as such:
  the other three run at 10 and 15 minutes, which does not serve Hinge §7's *"one act, one
  sentence, one handover"*, and no accepted send's latency has ever been observed here. One number
  to change if it is wrong.
- **`src/lib/press/dispatch.ts`** — `findNeverAttempted`, `findDueForRetry`,
  `claimForFirstAttempt`, `claimForRetry`, `describeDrain`, `DRAIN_BATCH`.

**The first claim is the schema's instruction:** `updateMany` on `attemptedAt: null`, act only when
the count is 1. Two overlapping cron runs cannot both send.

⚠ **THE RETRY CLAIM IS THE EXECUTOR'S AND THE SCHEMA DOES NOT SETTLE IT**, recorded as such on the
founder's confirmation: *"your compare-and-swap on nextAttemptAt is right and the schema genuinely
does not settle it. Record that as yours rather than the schema's."* On a retry `attemptedAt` is
already set, so the schema's predicate matches nothing. The compare-and-swap is on `nextAttemptAt`,
which the claim **clears** — so a row is either WAITING or IN FLIGHT and never both, and **the two
claim predicates are disjoint**, which is what stops one tick taking the same row through both
passes. That disjointness is asserted directly, in both directions.

### The three rulings recorded in the module for the second half to meet

Written where the send will go, so the second half meets them rather than discovers them — and
asserted, so they cannot be quietly dropped.

1. **`SMS_DISABLED` is a WITHHOLDING, not a provider error.** Ruled, **and ruled for a reason that
   is recorded as the reason:** ruling AN separated the two outcome doors precisely so this could
   not be settled by whichever branch got written first, and this is the case that would have
   settled it. It is also the **only** text outcome this environment can produce, because
   `TNZ_AUTH_TOKEN` is absent from both env files.
2. **The ask's text send will write a false `NUDGE_SENT_AUTO`, as a ruled stopgap**, with the fix
   filed on [[GTC-288]] — properly, in its own section, not as a note. The module carries an
   explicit ⚠ **do not "fix" this by adding an enum member**, because that is a migration bought to
   feed an index GTC-288 is retiring.
3. **A 401 or 403 from either provider is terminal and loud**, because every provider answer here
   is an auth failure and a retry-any-error policy queues every row three times against a key that
   will never work.

And the order: **gates, then quiet hours, then claim, then send** — with the founder's reason as the
ruling, that claiming first and then deferring leaves `attemptedAt` set with no outcome.

### Test results — RED first

- **RED — 41 assertions, 6 passed, 35 failed.** The six are the three Layer 0 controls, the control
  that the other three cron schedules are untouched, and the two fixture controls.
- ⚠ **The first RED run CRASHED** on `dispatch.findNeverAttempted` of a null module, so the layers
  below never reported. Fixed before the fix was written, with guarded `find`/`claim` stand-ins —
  GTC-192's standing warning: *"a suite that cannot report its red is not a red."* This is the
  second slice in a row to need that, and both times it was the same shape: a module imported
  inside a `try` and then called outside one.
- **GREEN — 42/42** on the first run, after one assertion was strengthened (below).

### ⚠ One assertion was replaced before the mutations, not after

*"Both passes take a bound"* was written as a source grep for `take:`. That proves the word is
present, not that the query is bounded — **the same defect as slice 5b's M4, where a refusal
asserted by its NAME survived the refusal being deleted.** Replaced with the claim itself: a take
of 2 against a three-row fixture returns 2, plus a control that the same pass returns all three
unbounded, so the 2 is the take working rather than a query finding too little. Mutation M4 then
has something to fail.

**The rule this is the second instance of: a source grep is the right instrument only when there is
no behavioural one available.** Here there always was.

### Mutation checks

Five, each verified landed by grep before its result was read, each restored byte-identical.

- **M1 — the first claim loses its `attemptedAt: null` predicate** → **fails 4**: the second claim
  succeeds, the count increments twice, the crashed-attempt assertion, and the disjointness
  assertion. No collateral.
- **M2 — the retry claim stops clearing `nextAttemptAt`** → **fails exactly 2**: the
  compare-and-swap assertion and the second-retry-claim refusal. The row would be WAITING and IN
  FLIGHT at once, which is precisely what the swap exists to prevent.
- **M3 — the retry pass drops `lte: now`** → **fails exactly 1**: a row whose backoff has not
  elapsed appears. The backoff would become a queue position instead of a wait.
- **M4 — the never-attempted pass loses its `take`** → **fails exactly 1**, the bound. It would
  have failed nothing an hour earlier; see above.
- **M5 — the route claims rows with no transport behind them** → **fails exactly 1**, the
  no-claim assertion.
  ⚠ **AND M5 WROTE TO THE WHOLE TABLE, NOT TO THE FIXTURE.** `findNeverAttempted` is global by
  design, so the mutated route claimed every unclaimed `OutboundMessage` row in the database. It
  happened to be only the fixture's three, because the table holds 0 rows otherwise and the fixture
  deletes its own in `finally` — **checked afterwards rather than assumed: `outboundMessage` 0, of
  which 0 claimed.** Recorded as a rule: **a mutation of a query with no tenant filter is not scoped
  to the fixture, and its blast radius is the table.** On a populated database that mutation would
  have claimed live rows and left them crashed.

### ⚠ The route-scan control, and a green assertion carrying false prose

Adding a route moved **6 of the 118** pinned assertions in `tests/security-route-scan-control.ts`.
That file's own docstring instructs the update — *"When a route is legitimately added, this number
is updated in the same commit, and the update is visible in review"* — so it was, deliberately:

- **106 → 107 files, 134 → 136 handlers, 23 → 25 unguarded**, with the reason in the docstring.
- **Both handlers added to `HEAD_VERDICTS`** as
  `SHARED_SECRET:PROVEN+SHARED_SECRET:PROVEN`, beside the other three cron routes' six.
- ⚠ **The "no credential of any kind" count did NOT move and is still 12.** That is the scanner
  confirming the new route carries its credential, rather than the executor asserting it — and it
  is the difference between this route and slice 5a's redirect handlers, which moved that number
  and were therefore moved into `next.config.js` instead.
- **The became-unguarded diff** expected exactly one entry and now sees three. Widened by **naming
  the two new handlers individually**, never by loosening to a count, because that assertion's whole
  job is to catch a handler silently losing its guard — so a FOURTH addition still fails and
  whoever makes it has to come and say why.

⚠ **AND ONE ASSERTION WAS GREEN WHILE ITS LABEL WAS FALSE, which is worse than a red.** *"The
arithmetic closes: 32 - 10 + 1 = 23"* computes from the live sets, so it stayed green while the tree
said 25. A true assertion carrying false prose goes red for nobody. Both were updated —
`32 - 10 + 3 = 25` — and the note is kept in place, because the next person to add a route will be
in exactly this position.

### ⚠ And a tripwire in slice 5a's suite fired, exactly as intended

`tests/press-route-test.ts` asserted *"there is no dispatcher: no cron route for the outbound drain
exists yet"*. It went red the moment this half landed. **It was replaced rather than deleted**, with
the thing that must stay true forever instead of the thing that was true for two commits:

> ⚠ **NO INLINE DRAIN AT THE PRESS.** Ruled 2026-09-19, with the founder's reason as the ruling:
> *"a request that can be killed halfway is not one act with no recall."*

So the press module is now asserted to name neither the dispatch module nor any of its claims, with
a control that the dispatcher really does exist — so the absence is an absence and not a module that
was never there. Press suite 60 → 61.

## Evidence (slice 5c, SECOND HALF) — Executor-Completed

> **The gates, quiet hours, the transports, the outcome — and both pressing surfaces stood down.**

### Preflight

- `npm run db:migrate` — *"Already in sync"*. No migration.
- Dev server up on `:3000`, probed first. `npm run test:security` **159/159** and
  `npx tsc --noEmit` exit 0 before and after. Tree at the start: `e275623`.

### What was built

- **`sendAskEmail` in `src/lib/email.ts`** — the sender slice 4b was written for.
  `sendNudgeEmail` could not be reused: it sets no `reply_to` and has no per-message display name,
  and ruling F wants **the host's name as sender and `User.email` as reply-to**. It answers through
  the same `resultOf`, so it inherits 4b's `providerMessageId` rather than getting a second reading
  of Resend's envelope. ⚠ The docstring states the seam plainly and forbids the obvious "fix": the
  display name is hers, **the address is ours**, and putting her address in `from` would fail
  SPF/DKIM on a domain she does not control — which is the pretence Hinge §5 and ruling F both
  decline.
- **`OutboundWithheldWhy`** — our words, keyed in a `Record` so a value added later is a compile
  error until somebody decides whether it is terminal.
- **`blockedToWithheld`** — ⚠ **`SMS_DISABLED`, `OPTED_OUT` and `INVALID_NUMBER` are
  WITHHOLDINGS; `SEND_FAILED` is not.** Ruled, and the module records *why* it was ruled rather
  than only that it was: ruling AN separated the two doors precisely so this could not be settled by
  whichever branch got written first, and `SMS_DISABLED` is the case that would have settled it
  silently, because it is the ONLY text outcome this environment produces.
- **`quietHoursDefers`**, per row and TEXT only, and it **defers** — no claim, no `withheldAt`.
- **`nextBackoffAt`** (1 minute, 5 minutes, then terminal — fixed, not computed) and
  **`isRetryableProviderError`** (401/403 terminal at once; 429 and 5xx retryable; nothing else).
- **`recordAcceptance` / `recordRejection` / `recordWithholding` / `scheduleRetry`**, and
  **`drainOnce`** running the ruled order: **gates, quiet hours, claim, send.**
- **`PersonEvent.sentAt` stamped from the acceptance instant** (ruling G), **first acceptance
  wins** — the executor's reading, raised at 5a and now asserted: `sentAt: null` in the predicate,
  because ruling U's bounce door makes a second ASK row and a resend that moved the clock would give
  someone a fresh four days because Gather tried again.
- **The route drains.** **Both buttons stood down** — see below.

### ✅ OPTION D — both pressing surfaces, and what was recorded with them

Founder ruling, 2026-09-19. `InviteStatusSection` on `/plan/[eventId]` and the host view at
`/h/[token]` both read **"Review and send"** and navigate to the pre-flight. Neither posts to the
press. Both name [[GTC-321]].

**Recorded in the code as the founder's reason rather than as a change:** *"the pre-flight exists to
be the one screen where she sees what the press will do before doing it, and three pressing surfaces
is the thing it was built to prevent."*

⚠ **AND THE CORRECTION IS RECORDED AS THE BETTER FRAMING, NOT AS A CHANGE OF PLAN.** The first
report called the buttons urgent because of a second press. That was wrong: the second press was
closed at slice 5a by GTC-169's guard — `pressSend` refuses `ALREADY_SENT` with a 409 once
`Event.sentAt` is set, and both buttons were hidden by then. **The danger was always the FIRST
press** — one irreversible act, on two surfaces, triggered by a button whose words promised the
opposite of what it did, behind no preview and none of the pre-flight's five checks. Both button
notes say so.

⚠ **AND ONE SURFACE COULD NOT TAKE OPTION D AS WRITTEN, WHICH IS REPORTED RATHER THAN PAPERED
OVER.** `/h/[token]` is the host MAGIC-LINK view: it authenticates by token and its reader may hold
**no session**. The pre-flight is session-gated (`requireEventRole`) and `middleware.ts` does not
match `/plan/*`, so it issues **no redirect to sign-in** — a signed-out host sent straight there gets
the page shell and then 401s on every fetch. So that surface is a link with the requirement said out
loud — *"You will need to be signed in"* — and the reason is in the file. A 401 is not what
"navigate to the pre-flight" meant.

### The question this half raised and did not answer — [[GTC-321]], filed

Filed on instruction, as the question it is: **after rulings AC and G, Gather is the sender, so "I
sent them myself" is a different act the model has never ruled on** — and it has a consequence,
because a hand-sent person has no provider acceptance, so no `PersonEvent.sentAt`, so
`findNudgeCandidates` never chases them. Recording a hand-send honestly means ruling whether those
people are chased at all. The ticket says plainly that **the workflow is older than the press, that
nothing has ruled it dead, and that option D deliberately does not foreclose it.**

⚠ **The `Person.inviteAnchorAt` coupling is in that ticket, not a separate one.** Found by checking
rather than assuming: `invite-status` derives `NOT_SENT` from `inviteAnchorAt`, not from
`PersonEvent.sentAt`, so the anchor is **what makes button 1 disappear**. Move it to acceptance to
tidy the disagreement 5a raised and the button never disappears in an environment where acceptance
never happens — a permanently visible button whose every press answers 409. **Two questions, one
ruling.**

- **GREEN — 47/47**, after four assertions were added or repaired by the findings below.

### Mutation checks

Each verified landed before its result was read, each restored byte-identical.

- **M1 — the two doors collapsed** (`SEND_FAILED` returns a withheld why) → fails the assertion
  that keeps them apart.
- **M2 — quiet hours applied to every channel** (the batch-level shape) → **fails 3**, including
  the behavioural one: the email row in the same night batch would have been held for a rule that
  binds text. ⚠ It failed only **1** before that assertion was repaired — see finding 3.
- **M3 — a resend moves the per-person clock** → fails first-acceptance-wins.
- **M4 — the auth guard deleted from `isRetryableProviderError`** → ⚠ **failed the WRONG
  assertion.** See finding 2.
- **M5 — the gate order reverted** was not needed as a mutation: the wrong order was the code's
  actual first state, and the assertion caught it. See finding 1.

### ⚠ FINDING 1 — the gate order was wrong, and the assertion that caught it wanted the chooser's own why

The first implementation checked `preview.recipients` before `preview.hostList`. **A recipient the
chooser now refuses is not in `recipients` at all** — they are on `hostList` with a why. So every
one of them went through the generic `NOT_THIS_RECIPIENT` branch and **the chooser's own why-code was
thrown away** — which is precisely what `withheldWhy` was separated from `providerError` to carry.

Caught by an assertion that expected `NO_CHANNEL` and read `NOT_THIS_RECIPIENT`. The fix is four
lines moved, and the reason is in the module so the next reader does not reorder them back.

### ⚠ FINDING 2 — a guard that was redundant for the string used to test it

M4 deleted the 401/403 check from `isRetryableProviderError` and the suite **still passed that
assertion**, because `'401 Unauthorized'` does not match the retry patterns either — so the guard was
doing nothing for the case chosen to prove it. The mutation failed a different assertion instead,
which is how it surfaced.

Repaired with a string that matches **both** — *"401 Unauthorized: rate limit on an invalid api
key"* — so the guard's **precedence** is what the assertion holds. **The rule: a guard is only
asserted by an input that would pass without it.** Same family as slice 5a's fixture proving a count
and never the filter.

### ⚠ FINDING 3 — the suite manufactured a crashed attempt, and `ok()` was the reason

The drain was called as `ok(() => dispatch.drainOnce(...)) ? await dispatch.drainOnce(...) : null`.

**`ok()` is for SYNCHRONOUS predicates.** Given an async call it invokes it, gets a truthy promise,
and the ternary then invokes it **again**. So the drain ran **twice** — once un-awaited and racing,
once for real — and the second call found nothing left and reported all zeros. The un-awaited first
left a row with `attemptedAt` set and no outcome.

⚠ **THE SUITE PRODUCED A CRASHED ATTEMPT — the exact state this whole design exists to make
impossible — and reported it as a failing assertion about quiet hours.** The diagnosis pointed at the
code for two runs before the harness was suspected.

**Two more repairs came out of it:**

- ⚠ **The suite was TIME-DEPENDENT.** Quiet hours holds a TEXT row back, so the texted recipient is
  withheld by day and deferred by night — the `SMS_DISABLED` assertion passed or failed on the wall
  clock. The drain now runs on a **fixed clock**, verified against `isQuietHours` rather than
  reasoned about. A time-dependent assertion is a flake that reads as a regression.
- ⚠ **A CONTROL WITH A FALSE LABEL, CAUGHT BY THE RULE WRITTEN THE SAME DAY.** The quiet-hours
  control asserted `deferred === considered` on a batch holding only the text row — satisfied whether
  quiet hours binds one channel or all of them, while its label claimed *"this is the assertion the
  batch-level shape would fail"*. It was not. An email row was added to the night batch so the two
  shapes give different answers, and M2 now fails it. **This is the standing warning's newest rule
  biting in the same session it was written.**

### Assertions checked

- [x] `sendAskEmail` exists, carries `replyTo` and a per-message from-name, and answers through
      `resultOf`.
- [x] The withheld vocabulary is keyed; `SMS_DISABLED`, `OPTED_OUT` and `INVALID_NUMBER` are
      withholdings and `SEND_FAILED` is not.
- [x] A recipient the chooser no longer reaches is withheld **with the chooser's own why**, takes no
      claim, writes nothing into `providerError`, and **leaves the drain** — the last of which is a
      correction to the first half's query.
- [x] The emailed recipient was claimed, the provider refused, the row reached an end state, and
      the provider's words are stored verbatim.
- [x] The texted recipient is withheld `SMS_DISABLED` — the ruling arriving as the only text
      outcome this environment can produce.
- [x] Quiet hours defers a TEXT row **untouched** and lets an EMAIL row in the same batch through.
- [x] Backoff: three attempts then terminal, waits grow, 401/403 terminal **by precedence**.
- [x] Ruling G: `PersonEvent.sentAt` from the acceptance instant, and **first acceptance wins**.
- [x] The dispatcher calls the **real** senders, has no seam, no `NODE_ENV`, no injected transport.
- [x] Neither button says *"I've sent the invites"*, both say *"Review and send"*, neither posts to
      the press, both name [[GTC-321]].
- [x] Adjacent: `test:outbound-drain` **47/47**, `test:outbound-dispatch` **42/42**,
      `test:press` **62/62**, `test:preflight-withholding` **37/37**, `test:ask-preview`
      **100/100**, `test:carried-answer` **43/43**, `test:assignment-answer` **42/42**,
      `test:channel-chooser` **114/114**, `test:lifecycle` **24/24**,
      `test:host-never-messaged` **20/20**, `test:email-send-result` **26/28** (the two are
      slice 4b's recorded environmental `GTC265_PROBE_KEY` failures, unchanged).
      `test:security` **159/159**, `test:security:routes` **118/118**. `tsc --noEmit` exit 0.
- [x] No raw NUL byte in any changed file (KB-006). `gather_dev` unchanged: 29 events, 333 Person,
      335 PersonEvent, `OutboundMessage` **0**, 72 AuditEntry, 45 InviteEvent,
      `PersonEvent.sentAt` 109.

### ⚠ Three tripwires from earlier slices fired, and all three were replaced rather than deleted

1. **`outbound-dispatch-test`**'s *"the dispatch module reaches no provider"* and *"the route takes
   no claim"* — both true of the first half **by design** and deliberately false now. Replaced with
   what must stay true forever: the dispatcher is the **only** thing that claims, asserted over all
   of `src/`.
2. **`press-route-test`** layer W, *"both still read I've sent the invites"* — the tripwire built
   for exactly this moment. Replaced with the press's own standing invariant: **nothing in the UI
   posts to the press**, and 5f wires exactly one caller.
3. **`email-send-result`**'s *"NO CALLER READS IT"* was honest as written — scoped to four files,
   with a comment predicting slice 5 would be the consumer. ✅ **The prediction is now a fact**, so a
   positive assertion was added beside it: the dispatcher reads the id and writes it at acceptance.

### ⚠ What remains unproven, unchanged and restated

**No fake, so the acceptance path is unexercised end to end.** `RESEND_API_KEY` does not
authenticate and `TNZ_AUTH_TOKEN` is absent, so every email is rejected and every text is withheld
`SMS_DISABLED` before a provider is reached. `acceptedAt`, `provider`, `providerMessageId` and —
through ruling G — `PersonEvent.sentAt` have no path to being written by the dispatcher here.

⚠ **AND THE ONE DISTINCTION THE RULING DOES NOT REACH, STATED SO IT IS NOT MISTAKEN FOR A FAKE.**
The outcome-recording functions are called **directly** by the suite with the values a provider would
have returned. That is not a fake provider: it is this ticket's own code called with data, and it is
the only way ruling G's clock can be asserted at all on this machine. **The dispatcher has no seam
and no branch** — layer N asserts it. End-to-end acceptance stays unproven and is not claimed.

**Still unsizeable:** the drain's query cost (`OutboundMessage` holds 0 rows; `DRAIN_BATCH` of 100 is
a guess and the `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` the schema asks for is still owed), whether two minutes
is right, and `isRetryableProviderError`'s accuracy — it matches on a message string because neither
sender returns a status code, and [[GTC-289]]'s investigation is what should replace it.

> ✅ **AND IT DID — [[GTC-289]]'s INVESTIGATION IS DONE (2026-09-19) AND THE FIX IS RULED ITS.**
> Recorded here as well as there, so the owed string-match is findable from both ends.
>
> The SDK declares `ErrorResponse { message: string; statusCode: number | null; name:
> RESEND_ERROR_CODE_KEY }`, and `RESEND_ERROR_CODE_KEY` is a **closed 21-value union**. So the
> predicate matches prose where a code exists — **and the reason is upstream of the predicate:**
> `resultOf` in `src/lib/email.ts` collapses the whole error to `error?: string` and throws the code
> and the status away. `isRetryableProviderError` reads a string because a string is all it is given.
>
> **Founder ruling: widen `SendResult`, and it is slice 6's to make rather than slice 5's to
> revisit** — *"slice 6 is the ticket that needs the distinction, and slice 5's own evidence already
> names the string match as owed."* On the declared vocabulary the retryable set is
> `rate_limit_exceeded`, `internal_server_error` and `application_error`; the auth-terminal set this
> slice's guard exists for is `missing_api_key`, `restricted_api_key`, `invalid_api_key` and
> `invalid_access`.
>
> ⚠ **So `isRetryableProviderError` as shipped in this slice is KNOWN to be reading the wrong field,
> and the replacement is scoped elsewhere.** It is not a latent defect anybody has to rediscover.

> ✅ **AND IT IS FIXED — [[GTC-289]] PHASE 2, 2026-09-19. This note is closed.** Founder ruling: the
> widening went BEFORE that ticket's migration, *"because a shipped predicate known to be reading the
> wrong field should not wait behind a migration and a rehearsal."*
>
> `SendResult` now carries `providerErrorCode` and `providerStatusCode`; `isRetryableProviderError`
> takes a `ProviderFailure` and reads **the code, then the status, then the message**. This slice's
> three prose assertions are unchanged in what they claim — same strings, same expectations, now in
> `error` — and they exercise the third reading, which is what a text send and a thrown email send
> still arrive with. The vocabulary lives in
> `src/lib/email-delivery/resend-error-contract.ts`; full evidence in [[GTC-289]] *Evidence (phase 2)*.
>
> ⚠ **AND PHASE 2 FOUND TWO FALSE LABELS IN THIS SLICE'S OWN DRAIN SUITE, WHICH IS THE MORE IMPORTANT
> HALF OF THE FINDING.** They said *"THE PROVIDER REFUSED"* and *"THE PROVIDER'S WORDS ARE STORED
> VERBATIM in providerError"*. **Neither was true in that process.** Under `tsx`, `.env.local` is not
> loaded, so `RESEND_API_KEY` is **absent**, `getResendClient()` **throws**, and the sender's `catch`
> returns the SDK's own message — measured: `'Missing API key. Pass it to the constructor ...'`. **No
> request left the process and Resend never saw it**, so the words in `providerError` on those rows are
> nobody's provider's.
>
> The conditions were true and the sentences were false, which is [[GTC-192]]'s standing warning
> exactly — a true assertion carrying a false label goes red for nobody — **and it survived this
> slice's entire review, including its mutation table.** Both labels are corrected and a new assertion
> pins the truth by matching the stored words.
>
> ✅ **The distinction is now machine-readable, which is a second consequence of the widening:** on an
> EMAIL row a failure with **no** `providerErrorCode` means the provider was never reached, because no
> `catch` block in `src/lib/email.ts` sets that field. Before phase 2 there was nothing to tell *the
> provider refused us* from *we never called the provider* — and only one of those is
> [[GTC-247]]'s to close. The dev server does hold the key, so a drain through the cron route gets a
> real refusal; only an in-process suite does not.

## Evidence (slice 5f) — THE BUTTON. Executor-Completed, 2026-09-19

> **Slice 5f of eight, and with it SLICE 5 IS COMPLETE.** GTC-189 stays open: slice 7b (ruling U's
> door) is a precondition of the DEPLOY rather than of anything else, and slice 8 is unbuilt.
>
> **This is the commit that turns the press on.** Two surfaces posted to it until 5c took them down;
> now exactly one does, and it is the one the pre-flight was built to be.

### What was built

- **`src/lib/press/press-words.ts`** (new) — the threshold script and one sentence per refusal.
  ⚠ **A separate file from `press.ts` for a reason that is not tidiness:** the pre-flight is a CLIENT
  component, and importing a VALUE from the press module would pull Prisma into the browser bundle.
  `PressRefusalCode` comes in as a TYPE, which is erased. The suite asserts the module holds no
  database handle, the same fence `state.ts` carries.
- **`PRESS_REFUSAL_WORDS`** is a `Record` over the refusal union, so **an eighth refusal code does not
  compile until somebody writes the sentence a host reads.** Mutation R4 proved it: `tsc` refused the
  tree with `TS2741`. The alternative is a default — *"something went wrong"* — for a state the press
  named precisely, which is the press knowing more than the screen and telling her less.
- **The pre-flight's step 5** now posts to `POST /api/events/[id]/send`, which slice 5a built and
  nothing called.
- **The two-sentence threshold script**, verbatim from Hinge §2, shown at commitment — and with no
  roadmap door beside it. The suite asserts neither sentence names a nudge, a day, an escalation or a
  tone, which is what stops it growing into the options screen the spec refused.

### ⚠ THREE GUARDS AGAINST A SECOND PRESS, AND THEY FAIL DIFFERENTLY

The press is ruled ONE ACT, NO RECALL, so a double-click must not be able to produce a second anything:

1. **`pressing`**, checked at the TOP of the handler and not only in the button's `disabled` — because
   **a `disabled` attribute is a rendering**, and a second click can land before React re-renders.
2. **`pressed`**, so the button stays spent for the life of the page.
3. **The route's own `ALREADY_SENT`**, which is the only one that survives a reload and is therefore the
   real guarantee. The first two make the common case quiet; this one makes it correct.

⚠ **AND A NETWORK FAILURE IS NOT A REFUSAL.** The request may have reached the press and committed
before the connection dropped, so that branch says *"Gather could not tell whether that went through.
Reload the page to check."* Every other sentence can say **nothing was sent**, because the route
answered. This one cannot, and claiming it would be the one lie on the screen.

### ⚠ WHAT PRESSING IT DOES IN THIS ENVIRONMENT — RECORDED AT THE BUTTON, ON FOUNDER INSTRUCTION

> ⚠ RECORD IT AT THE BUTTON, not only in the ticket. Whoever meets that button next should meet the
> consequence in the same place.

The comment at step 5 carries it, measured on the one pressable board (Henderson Family Christmas 2025,
43 memberships, 38 recipients):

- The press writes **38 rows**, sets `Event.sentAt` and the anchors, writes `SEND_PRESSED`. Irreversible
  at the mechanism level; a second press is refused.
- The dispatcher then **rejects all 37 email rows** — `RESEND_API_KEY` does not authenticate
  ([[GTC-247]]), the observed answer is `validation_error` / 401 / *"API key is invalid"*, terminal on
  the first attempt — and **withholds the 1 text row** `SMS_DISABLED`.
- ⚠ **THE BOARD THEN READS RED, "never got it", FOR 37 PEOPLE WHOSE ADDRESSES ARE FINE** (slice 7a's
  `NOT_DELIVERED`), and the summary says 37 need her.
- ⚠ **AND THERE IS NO PRODUCT PATH BACK.** Ruling U's door is 7b and does not exist, the remind is
  withdrawn on that red, the press cannot be repeated. **Those reds are permanent on that board until
  7b ships or somebody edits the database.**

**Founder ruling, accepting it as stated:** *"That is the press behaving exactly as designed against a
broken key, and it is the state Q7 chose over a fake."* And the reason the button is not fenced behind
the environment: *"the button is the ticket, and a fence existing only because the environment is broken
is a fence somebody has to remember to remove."*

⚠ **AND PRESSING SENDS NOTHING AT ALL LOCALLY, WHICH IS ALSO AT THE BUTTON.** Vercel's cron does not
run on a dev machine, so the rows sit unattempted until somebody calls
`/api/cron/outbound-dispatch` with `CRON_SECRET`. **Anyone testing this presses, sees nothing happen,
and that is not a bug** — the press and the sending are two acts here.

### ✅ Four markers inverted, in three suites, each on schedule

Every one of them named this commit in its own label, which is the difference between an assertion that
inverts and one that broke:

1. **`tests/press-route-test.ts`** — *"NOTHING IN THE UI POSTS TO THE PRESS. Slice 5f wires exactly one
   caller … and until then the count is zero."* Now: **exactly one, and it is the pre-flight.** The
   invariant it holds is unchanged — how many surfaces can reach the press.
2. **The same suite** — *"the pre-flight Send button is still wired to nothing."*
3. **`tests/ask-preview-test.ts`** — *"the Send button is still disabled outright"* and *"the page calls
   no send route"*. Rewritten to the thing that survives the inversion: **the preview is not the press**,
   and the page's one write is the press itself, called once.
4. ⚠ **`tests/preflight-withholding-test.ts`'s CONTROL broke rather than inverted**, and it is worth the
   distinction. It proved the page *"really was read and stripped"* by checking it still contained
   `'wired to nothing'` — **a control anchored on a string with a known expiry date.** Re-anchored on the
   page's own heading. **A control's anchor should outlive the slice it was written in**, or it fails for
   the wrong reason on the day its subject changes.

### Test results

- **RED:** `test:press` 60/69 — nine failing, the whole of 5f.
- **GREEN:** `test:press` **74/74** (69 + the five the two surviving mutations forced),
  `test:ask-preview` **101/101**, `test:preflight-withholding` **37/37**.
- **Unaffected, all re-run:** `outbound-drain` 52/52, `glance-read` 169/169, `glance-grid` 138/138,
  `glance-actions` 82/82, `glance-replay` 298/298, `delivery-poll` 45/45, `resend-errors` 68/68,
  `mini-send` 33/33, `child-tokens` 29/29, `security` **159/159**, `security:routes` **118/118**.
  `tsc --noEmit` exit 0.
- ✅ **And the page RENDERS: `GET /plan/<id>/pre-flight` answers 200** on the live dev server, which
  after KB-008 is a check worth making rather than assuming — a client component that does not compile
  fails here and nowhere else.

### Mutations — five planted, and ⚠ TWO SURVIVED

1. **R1 — the in-handler latch removed.** One assertion failed.
2. ⚠ **R2 — the screen shows the ROUTE's prose instead of the host-facing words. SURVIVED: 69, 101 and
   37, all green.** Caught only by `tsc` noticing an IMPORT had become unused — an accident, not a
   guard. Remove the words and the import together and nothing would have failed. **Nothing asserted
   the one line this slice exists to place.** Repaired with two assertions plus a control — the screen
   reads the CODE, and it NEVER renders `outcome.message`, which is written for a log — and R2 was
   re-planted and now fails both.
3. **R3 — the threshold script paraphrased.** One assertion failed.
4. **R4 — one refusal loses its words.** `tsc` refused the tree: `TS2741`, naming the missing code.
5. ⚠ **R5 — the script never renders. SURVIVED.** Everything above asserted the WORDS EXIST in the
   module; the acceptance item is that the script *"appears verbatim AT COMMITMENT"*, and **the render
   is the requirement.** Repaired with two more assertions — the page maps `THRESHOLD_SCRIPT`, and the
   gate is `pressed !== null` and not its inverse — and R5b, which shows the script BEFORE the press,
   now fails.

⚠ **BOTH SURVIVORS ARE THE SAME DEFECT AND IT IS [[GTC-192]]'s FIFTH FACE:** the mutations that bit were
aimed at what was easy to reach, and the two that mattered were aimed at what the slice was FOR. Five
of the slice's fourteen assertions exist only because two mutations survived.

⚠ **AND BOTH WERE CAUGHT BY `tsc` FOR THE WRONG REASON**, which is worth naming on its own: an unused
import and a null-narrowing complaint. **A type error that happens to coincide with a defect is not a
guard** — it moves with the shape of the edit rather than with the behaviour, and a slightly different
mutation would have been silent in both cases.

### What slice 5f does NOT do

- **No I3.** The minutes-after screen is downstream and named out of scope by the ticket.
- **No roadmap door**, per the spec's explicit refusal.
- **No change to the press, the dispatcher, the poller or the board.** This is a surface and a wiring.
- **No new route and no schema.**
- ⚠ **And it does not make the environment able to send.** [[GTC-247]] is unchanged; what 5f changes is
  that a host can now reach the press from the screen the pre-flight was built to be.

**`npm run build` not run** — same call and reason as every slice since 4a (KB-005).

## Evidence (slice 7a) — THE FOURTH AND FIFTH REDS. Executor-Completed, 2026-09-19

> **Slice 7a of eight. GTC-189 stays open.** The derivation half of slice 7: two red reasons, the
> per-person delivery fact, two why-lines, ruling S's carried red, and the remind withdrawn. Ruling U's
> door is **7b** and is not built.

**⚠ THE ENTRY SAID FOUR THINGS AND CONTAINED NINE, WHICH IS WHY THE SHAPE WAS REPORTED FIRST.** The
largest surprise: **two reds, not one.** Ruling M's unreachable person is a SECOND reason with its own
why-line, and ruling M says plainly it *"does not depend on bounce ingest"*.

### The words, ruled 2026-09-19

- **`NOT_DELIVERED`** — *"never got it"*.
  ⚠ **NOT `BOUNCED`, and the name is the ruling:** it covers THREE mechanisms — a rejection at
  submission, a bounce after acceptance, and a provider failure — which are **one fact to the host: it
  did not arrive.** Naming it for one would make the other two read as a different red, and they are
  not.
- **`UNREACHABLE`** — *"nowhere to send"*. Ruling M's own word for the person.

⚠ **AND THE SECOND LINE WAS RULED AS "nowhere to send it", AT 18 CHARACTERS, WHICH AN EXISTING PIN
REFUSES.** Every why-line is held to ≤16 by `tests/glance-grid-test.tsx`, from the reference's 160px
columns. The trailing word is dropped rather than the pin widened. **The pin caught the executor's own
proposal**, which is what a pinned number is for — and the two new reasons were ADDED to that
assertion's list, so the label *"every why fits a strip"* is true of five rather than three.

### What was built

- **`src/lib/glance/state.ts`** — `RED_REASONS` 3 → 5; `DeliveryFact { failure }` shaped exactly like
  `ExhaustionFact` (a DECISION handed in, not telemetry, with **null claiming nothing**);
  `GlancePersonContext.delivery`, optional so every existing caller goes on meaning *"nothing known"*
  without being edited.
- **The branch's POSITION is the design.** Below the three response branches — **an answer is proof the
  ask arrived**, so an accepted, declined or maybe row is never reddened by a delivery failure — below
  `isHost`, and **above exhaustion**, because *"gone quiet"* is false of somebody who never got the
  message. The silence has a cause and that is the reason to say.
- **`src/lib/glance/delivery-fact.ts`** (new) — the only place the press's vocabulary and the glance's
  meet. Two `Record`s over closed unions, so a fifteenth withheld code or a tenth delivery kind does
  not compile until somebody decides what it means.
- **`src/components/glance/strip.ts`** — the two lines, and the fall-through closed.
- **`src/lib/glance/actions.ts`** — the remind withdrawn on both reds.
- **`src/lib/glance/read.ts`** — one more query and one more field.

### ✅ The fact is READ BACK, not re-derived — and it supersedes ruling M's aside

Ruling M says in passing that `read.ts` would consume slice 1's chooser to know who is unreachable.
**Superseded, on the founder's ruling, 2026-09-19:** the press already recorded both facts per person —
a delivery state for the message that failed, a withheld code for the person who could never be sent to
— so the board reads them off the row. *"Reading them back means the board and the press cannot disagree
by construction."*

⚠ **THE COST, ACCEPTED AT THE RULING:** before the press there are no rows, so neither red shows. The
pre-flight is where an unmessageable person is seen beforehand (slice 5b), and the two surfaces divide
cleanly.

### ⚠ The test applied to every withheld code: is this a fact about the PERSON, or about GATHER?

Ruling M's red hands the host a job. A code describing **our** broken configuration hands her a job she
cannot do and tells her something false about her guest.

- **UNREACHABLE:** `NO_CHANNEL`, `SMS_OPTED_OUT`, `OPTED_OUT`, `PHONE_UNUSABLE`, `INVALID_NUMBER`.
- **NOT a red:** `SMS_DISABLED` — and it is **the differential the suite asserts**, because
  `TNZ_AUTH_TOKEN` is absent here ([[GTC-247]]), so every text recipient carries it. Reading it as
  unreachable would paint them *"nowhere to send"*: a false sentence about a guest, caused by an
  operator failure. Also not a red: `NO_REPLY_TO`, `NOT_THIS_RECIPIENT`, `HOST_OWN_ASK`, and the four
  child-routing codes that cannot appear on a row at all.
- ⚠ **`NO_LINK` IS LEFT ALONE DELIBERATELY** — reachable, with nothing to send. Arguably its own red;
  ruling M does not reach it and the pre-flight already has words for it. Named rather than folded in.

### ⚠ Four findings that were not in the entry

1. **THE LATEST ROW IS THE FACT, OR 7b's DOOR CANNOT CLEAR THE RED IT OPENS.** Ruling U's resend writes
   a NEW row — the schema allows it on purpose, and its own note says a
   `@@unique([personEventId, kind])` *"would have broken ruling U's resend"*. Reading *"any row ever
   failed"* means the host resends, it works, and the board goes on saying it never arrived. **The door
   would be a button that changes nothing.** Ties break on `id` so the answer is stable rather than
   whichever row the planner returned first.
2. **THE FACT MUST BE APPLIED AT THE PERSON LEVEL TOO.** An itemless person has NO ROW to carry a
   colour, so `derivePersonState`'s `worst === null` branch needed it as well — otherwise ruling 16's
   itemless amber stands for somebody who never got the message, which is the exact falsehood ruling J
   called wrong. ⚠ **The exhaustion fact has the identical hole and was deliberately NOT fixed:** that
   is [[GTC-251]]'s to decide, and changing it quietly here would be answering somebody else's ruling.
3. **THE REMIND WAS A LIVE DEFECT THE NEW REDS CREATED.** `remindOffered` refused exactly one case, so
   a `NOT_DELIVERED` person would have been offered a remind that emails the address which just
   bounced — through the nudge route, not the dispatcher. Ruled: **the door replaces it.** For
   `UNREACHABLE` an action-less red is not a gap at all — ruling M's red MEANS Gather is out of moves.
4. ⚠ **THE FALL-THROUGH IN `whyLineFor` WAS A SILENT FALSEHOOD WAITING FOR THIS SLICE.** Its loop
   ended in a bare `return 'gone quiet'`, so ANY reason added to `WHY_PRECEDENCE` without its own
   branch rendered *"gone quiet"* — false of both new reds — and a reason left OUT of the list rendered
   nothing, which is the gap ruling M recorded. **Two silent failure modes, one absent and one false.**
   Replaced by a `Record` keyed on the precedence union: a missing line is now a COMPILE ERROR and
   there is no default to fall into.

### ✅ Ruling S, for one field and NO extra query — measured, because it was asked

The founder asked for the carried resolution's cost before building. **Measured:
`resolveCarriedSubjects` runs FIVE queries per carrier** — the event, every membership, every
household, every assignment and the opt-out list — so using it would have added five queries per
household contact to a read that runs six in total.

**It is not used.** The rule it wraps, `resolveHouseholdChannel`, is PURE over
`{ contactPersonEventId, members }`, and the board already holds both — so the answer is the SHARED
rule, called with one extra FIELD on a query that was already running. Neither one query per child nor
five per carrier: **zero.**

⚠ **Its limit, named:** this resolves who a household's messages GO to, not the chooser's whole ladder.
**A child whose household has no messageable contact gets no carrier and therefore no fact** — their
ask reached nobody and their rows read AMBER. That is ruling M's red for a child, it is NOT ruled, and
it is recorded here rather than guessed at. Children never have rows of their own, so it cannot be read
off one.

### ✅ Three things cost nothing, because GTC-192 built them that way

- **The summary is reason-blind.** `summarisePeople` keys on STATE, so both reds land in *"needs you"*
  with no change.
- **Ruling 14 greys them both**, applied after worst-colour-wins precisely *"so any red source added
  later is displaced without a code change"*. Rulings T and M both want exactly that, and the
  docstring predicted this slice.
- **Only two places in the tree read `reasons` at all**, which is the whole blast radius of a new
  reason.

### ⚠ Two process findings, both worth more than the code

1. **A STALE PRISMA CLIENT IN THE DEV SERVER FAILED 22 ASSERTIONS AND LOOKED EXACTLY LIKE A

   while the API-route assertions passed. The server had been running since before phase 3a's

   were spent on the code first.** Diagnosed by `git stash`, re-run, `git stash pop` — baseline green
   on the same server — and fixed by restarting it. **Filed as KB-008**, with the diagnosis move,
   because it had been latent for three commits and nothing had driven an HTTP page that reads the
   board since the migration.
2. ⚠ **THE EXECUTOR DESTROYED UNCOMMITTED WORK WITH `git checkout <file>`**, restoring `actions.ts`
   from HEAD in the middle of the mutation table — which discarded the slice's own change rather than
   the mutation. Caught immediately by a grep, redone, and verified. **The rule: a mutation is restored
   from the PRISTINE COPY it was taken from, never from git, because git's idea of pristine is the last
   commit and the work is not committed yet.** Four files were restored correctly from scratchpad
   copies; the one restored from git was the one that broke.

### ⚠ ADDENDUM, 2026-09-19, FOUND WHILE MEASURING THE PRESS FOR 5f — THE `UNREACHABLE` RED FIRES FOR NOBODY

**Read this before reading the rest of this evidence as complete.**

Ruling M's red is *"a line on the host's list reads red on the board, from the press."* This slice built
it by reading the fact off the row the press wrote. **The press writes no row for a host-list person** —
`pressSend` iterates `addressed`, which comes from `preview.recipients`, and the chooser puts an
unreachable person on `preview.hostList` instead.

**So `UNREACHABLE` can fire only where the chooser's answer CHANGES between the press and the drain.**
Measured on the one pressable board: **38 recipients, 4 on the host list, all `NO_CHANNEL`** — and after
a press those four would read AMBER.

**Filed as [[GTC-325]] and raised to High there.** The mechanism in this slice is not wrong; ruling M's
population and the press's row-writing population are different sets, and the difference was invisible
until the press was measured. `NOT_DELIVERED` is unaffected and works on every measurement above.
