## Evidence (slice 7b) — RULING U'S DOOR. Executor-Completed, 2026-09-19

> **Slice 7b of eight, and with it SLICE 7 IS COMPLETE.** GTC-189 stays open: slice 8 is unbuilt.
>
> **This is the commit that gives the two new reds an action again.** 7a withdrew the remind on
> both and shipped a red with no action at all, on the founder's chosen order. This is the other
> half of that ruling.

### The mechanism, in one sentence

Each action writes ONE NEW `OutboundMessage` row of kind `ASK` for the same membership and
returns. **It sends nothing.** Everything after it is `drainOnce` on the two-minute cron — the
chooser re-run, the host-list gate, the link gate, the reply-to gate, quiet hours, the claim, the
real senders. There is no second send path and no resend branch in the dispatcher.

⚠ **THE TREE WAS BUILT EXPECTING THIS, IN FOUR PLACES, AND THIS IS WHAT THEY WERE EXPECTING.** The
schema refuses `@@unique([personEventId, kind])` because it *"would have broken ruling U's
resend"*; `recordAcceptance` predicates the clock on `sentAt: null` so a resend does not restart a
person's four days; `enrolMiniSends` names ruling U's resend as *"the one case that makes a second
ask row deliberately"*; and `latestRowByMembership` exists so the newest row is the fact, *"or the
door is a button that changes nothing."* **Slice 7a built the read side of this door before the
door.**

### What was built

- **`src/lib/press/resend-door.ts`** (new) — CLIENT-SAFE: `doorActionsFor`, `DOOR_WORDS`,
  `RESEND_NOTES`, `RESEND_REFUSAL_WORDS`, and the two types. Every import in it is `import type`
  and therefore erased. ⚠ **A separate file from the mechanism for `press-words.ts`'s reason and
  not for tidiness:** `PersonSurface` is a client component, and importing a VALUE from the server
  module would pull Prisma into the browser bundle.
- **`src/lib/press/resend.ts`** (new) — `readDoor` and `resendToPerson`. One `resolveSubject` both
  entry points share, so the look and the press cannot disagree about who has a door.
- **`src/app/api/events/[id]/people/[personId]/resend/route.ts`** (new) — one route, HOST only.
  `GET` is the last look, `POST` is the press. One endpoint on decision 30's ground.
- **`src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts`** — `textAskReachOf`, the chooser's own ladder with
  the email set aside.
- **`src/lib/preflight/ask-preview.ts`** — `smsOptedOutFact` extracted; [[GTC-301]]'s two facts now
  have ONE definition of the merge, which the door asks and the pre-flight keeps using.
- **`src/lib/sms/send-sms.ts`** — `smsProviderConfiguredFor`, founder answer 1's fence, beside the
  sender whose branch it is.
- **`src/lib/ledger.ts`** — `EDIT_PERSON_CONTACT`, placed explicitly in `whyTrigger`.
- **`src/lib/glance/actions.ts`** — `DOOR_REASONS`, `doorOffered`, `readDoorView`, `resendAsk`.
- **`src/components/glance/PersonSurface.tsx`** — the door section.

### The six answers of 2026-09-19, and how each is met

1. **THE FENCE, AND IT IS NOT 5f REVERSED.** `doorActionsFor` drops the third action when no
   provider is configured, and `resendToPerson` refuses it at the press. The distinction is
   recorded at `smsProviderConfiguredFor` in the founder's words, so nobody reads it as 5f
   reversed: *"5f's fence would have hidden a true failure... This fence prevents a FALSE
   improvement."*
2. **THE LAST LOOK.** The `GET` serves the address and the composed message; the panel renders
   both; the control is a press. ⚠ **And the panel says at its own site that it is NOT
   [[GTC-311]]'s one-person pre-flight** — *"that is a ruled object with a chase question in it"* —
   because that file is where somebody would mistake it for one.
3. **[[GTC-293]] IS A PRECONDITION OF THE DEPLOY.** The door catches `P2002` and answers
   `ADDRESS_TAKEN` with a true sentence. It does not fix it.
4. **THE UNREACHABLE ARM SHIPS UNEXERCISED**, with `DOOR_WORDS.UNREACHABLE.EDIT` reading *"Add a
   way to reach them"* — the founder's words, the full stop dropped because it is a control and
   not a sentence.
5. **EDIT-AND-SEND WRITES A LEDGER ENTRY**, inside the same transaction as the write it describes.
   The `PATCH` people route is untouched, and the suite asserts it stays untouched.
6. **THE DOOR FETCHES THE ADDRESS WHEN IT OPENS.** `GlancePerson` is unchanged and the suite
   asserts it carries no contact detail.

### ✅ THE ACTION SET IS DERIVED, AND THE UNREACHABLE ARM'S ONE ACTION FALLS OUT OF IT

`doorActionsFor` asks three questions — is there an address, may the ask reach them by text, is
there a provider — and the two reds fall out of the answers rather than out of a branch on the
reason. **Every withheld code slice 7a maps to `UNREACHABLE` means the chooser found no email (it
had to reach the phone branch to say what it said) and no phone the ask may use**, so an
unreachable person is offered exactly one action by construction. The suite asserts that property
over `WITHHELD_MEANS_UNREACHABLE` itself rather than over a list written in the test, so a sixth
code cannot quietly acquire a second action. **What is keyed on the red is the WORDS**, and they
had to be: ruling M's red is not a wrong address being corrected.

### ⚠ FOUR FINDINGS THAT WERE NOT IN THE PROPOSAL

1. ⚠ **THE DOOR IS ONE PRESS PER FAILURE — A CONSEQUENCE FOUND RATHER THAN DESIGNED, and it is
   filed under that name on the founder's instruction of 2026-09-19. The proposal described the
   guard and missed what the guard produced; an assertion failing is what surfaced it.** The suite
   pressed `AGAIN` and then `EDIT` on the same person and the second was refused `NOTHING_FAILED`.
   That is the mechanism working: the row she just queued is now the latest, it has not failed, so
   the door CLOSES until it does. **She cannot stack three actions on one dead address**, and the
   same guard seen from the front is what stops a double-click sending twice. The proposal
   described the guard and did not notice it had this consequence.
2. ⚠ **THE REFUSAL ORDER WAS WRONG AND AN ASSERTION CORRECTED IT. ✅ RULED, FOUNDER 2026-09-19,
   with the general form the executor did not state:**

   > A fact about the person outranks a fact about us when the fact about the person is the one
   > that settles it.

   The first version put the fence ahead of the person's own facts, reasoning that our broken
   configuration must never be dressed as a fact about the guest. Slice 7a's test applied properly says the opposite here:
   *they have no mobile number* is true of the person whatever our configuration is, and
   fence-first told somebody with no phone at all that *"Gather cannot send texts at the moment"* —
   a temporary operator failure standing in front of a permanent fact, leaving her waiting for a
   provider that would change nothing for that guest. **Reach first, fence second**, with a control
   asserting the same person answers `NO_PHONE` on both sides of the seam.
3. ✅ **THE DON'T-CHASE REFUSAL WAS PROPOSED, NOT BUILT, AND THE DEPARTURE IS RULED CORRECT —
   FOUNDER, 2026-09-19. IT IS A FOUNDER ERROR CORRECTED AT THE BUILD, and it is recorded as one
   because the mechanism that caught it is worth more than the line it saved:**

   > THE DON'T-CHASE DEPARTURE IS RULED CORRECT. I signed a refusal that would have narrowed
   > ruling T, and you caught it by reading the ruling rather than the proposal. A remind is a
   > chase and the mark suppresses it; this is the ask re-sent, which the mark never touched.

   The shape proposal said the route would refuse a marked person *"exactly as `remindRefusal`
   does"*, and the founder signed it. Checked against ruling T before building and the copy was
   wrong: *"The ask still goes out. The mark is not an exclusion — he is invited like anyone
   else."* `chooseAskRoute` consults no mark and the press sends to marked people. **A remind is a
   CHASE, which the mark suppresses; this is the ASK, re-sent, which it never touched.**

   ⚠ **AND WHY THE ABSENCE IS SAFE IS NOT THE REASON THE PROPOSAL GAVE, WHICH IS THE HALF WORTH
   KEEPING.** The proposal said the refusal *"exists anyway, because the rule is about the system
   and not about which strips happen to be tappable"* — `remindRefusal`'s own ground, carried
   across without checking whether it applied. It does not. The door is unreachable for a marked
   person because **Ruling 14 greys them and only reds are doors**, and that is a fact about
   WHICH STRIPS ARE TAPPABLE — exactly the kind of fact `remindRefusal` refuses to rely on. It is
   not what makes the absence correct. **What makes the absence correct is that there is no rule
   to enforce:** ruling T never suppressed the ask, so there is nothing for this door to refuse.
   A refusal resting on the greying would have been a right answer resting on the wrong reason,
   and it would have become a wrong answer the day a marked person's strip became tappable.
4. ⚠ **A FENCE WAS ANCHORED ON A PROVIDER'S BRAND NAME AND RULING U'S OWN WORD COLLIDED WITH IT.**
   `tests/glance-actions-test.ts` proved the surface reaches no provider by matching the bare word
   `resend` — which is the EMAIL PROVIDER, and is also what ruling U calls its own action. The
   fence was about to fail for the wrong reason on the day its subject changed, which is 5f's
   finding about controls arriving at a fence. **Re-anchored STRICTER, on the import specifiers:**
   whole modules (`@/lib/sms`, `@/lib/email`, `@prisma/client`) rather than two named functions, so
   a module these files may not reach cannot be reached by any name, including one nobody has
   thought of. A control asserts the import reader really read.

### ✅ Three things cost nothing, because earlier slices built them that way

- **The remind and the door read ONE list.** `REMIND_WITHDRAWN` is now spread FROM `DOOR_REASONS`,
  so the relationship is structural rather than asserted and the two cannot drift apart.
  `ATTENDANCE_NO` stays withdrawn and is not a door reason — a guest who pulled out got their
  message.
- **The red clears with no code.** `latestRowByMembership` already makes the newest row the fact.
- **The drain needs no branch.** A row with `attemptedAt: null` and `withheldAt: null` is exactly
  `findNeverAttempted`'s predicate, which is how slice 5e's mini-send works too.

### ⚠ THE RED CLEARS BEFORE ANYTHING IS SENT, AND IT IS RECORDED AT THE SITE

Founder instruction, 2026-09-19: *"honest by amber's own definition, and record it as such at the
site... but say plainly that the visible effect of pressing is the red disappearing, because a host
who presses and watches will draw a conclusion from it."*

The new row is immediately the latest, it carries no end state, `deliveryFactFrom` answers no
failure, and the strip goes **AMBER** on the refresh the action dispatches — **before the cron has
run.** That is amber's own meaning: Gather has a next move and the row is waiting. It is recorded
in `resend-door.ts`, in `resend.ts` at the row write, and asserted in the suite against a control
that reads the rows AS THEY WERE. **`RESEND_NOTES` says "Sending it again", never "Sent"**, for the
other half of the same honesty.

### ⚠ THE SEGMENT COST ON AN OVERRIDDEN TEXT ROW — RECORDED, NOT SOLVED

`composePreview` counts SMS segments off the **chooser's** channel, not the row's. So a `PHONE` row
carries the body the email would have carried, sent as a text, at a cost the pre-flight's *"the
longest is N texts"* never counted and the host has never been shown. `composeAsk` writes one text
for both channels, so **the body is fine and only the cost is unknown.** Recorded at the override,
on the founder's instruction.

⚠ **BOTH SURVIVORS WERE AIMED AT WHAT THE SLICE IS FOR, AND BOTH TIMES THE ASSERTION WAS ANCHORED
ONE LINK SHORT OF THE THING IT CLAIMED.** M8 proved the entry was written and not that it was
written atomically; M11 proved the address was fetched and not that it was shown. **Three of this
slice's assertions exist only because two mutations survived.**

⚠ **AND NEITHER IS A NEW SHAPE — BOTH ARE DEFECTS THIS TICKET HAS ALREADY CAUGHT, ARRIVING AGAIN
SOMEWHERE ELSE. Named on the founder's instruction, 2026-09-19, because a repeat that is not
recognised as one gets solved from scratch every time:**

- **M8 is FOUNDER Q2's TRANSACTION DEFECT AT A SECOND SITE.** At the press it was the recipient
  count: *"A recipient count written outside it is a number that can disagree with the rows it
  counts, and that is the defect this ticket has caught four times in other guises."* Here it is
  the ledger entry: written outside the transaction, **it claims an address change that rolled
  back.** Same shape, different record — a fact written beside a write it claims to describe,
  free to survive the write's failure.
- **M11 is 5f's R5 WITH A DIFFERENT FIELD.** There the threshold script existed in the module and
  was never asserted to RENDER — *"the acceptance item is that the script appears verbatim AT
  COMMITMENT, and the render is the requirement."* Here the address was asserted through a regex
  the FETCH satisfies on its own, so **the panel could show her nothing while the suite stayed
  green.** Same shape, different field: an assertion that proves the value exists somewhere in the
  chain and stops short of the end of it.

**The rule both of them give, stated once: an assertion anchored one link short of its claim
passes for the wrong reason, and the link it stops at is always the one that was easy to reach.**
That is GTC-192's fifth face again, which slice 5f named for the same pair of survivors.

## Evidence (slice 8a) — THE BLOCK. Executor-Completed, 2026-09-27

> **Slice 8a of eight — [[GTC-324]]'s three rulings, built.** GTC-189 stays open: 8b, the chase, is
> next. **Split on founder ruling D1 (2026-09-27)** because the entry is two kinds of work: this is
> the provider's facts, a migration and the words; 8b is the sending path. Separate commits, suites
> and Evidence; **they deploy together with [[GTC-311]].**
>
> ✅ **THE MIGRATION'S SQL WAS APPROVED AND IT IS APPLIED TO `gather_dev`, 2026-09-27** — after
> the rehearsal, on the founder's word, and before the commit (Zone 5's standing rule held both
> until then). The dev server was restarted on the new client and the clone dropped. The first
> GREEN runs were against the clone; the final gate below ran against `gather_dev`.

### The mechanism, in one sentence

**The poll writes the provider's refusal against the ADDRESS, and every guest email path reads
it as "no address".** A complaint, a hard bounce or a suppression read by `pollOne` writes an
`EmailBlock` row keyed on the address Resend reports it sent to, in the same transaction as the
outcome. A complaint also writes the event's `EmailOptOut`. The chooser treats a blocked address
as absent — text where there is a usable mobile, otherwise the host's list — and the dispatcher,
the door, the by-hand nudge and the wrap-up each refuse it at their own send.

### The rulings of 2026-09-27, and where each went

- **GTC-324 ruling 1 — a spam report is an unsubscribe from that event.** `pollOne` in
  `src/lib/email-delivery/delivery-poll.ts` upserts `EmailOptOut` on `COMPLAINED`, so everything
  [[GTC-296]] built applies to that event.
- **Ruling 2 — address-wide, matching Resend.** `EmailBlock` (new model); `recordEmailBlock`,
  `listEmailBlocks`, `emailBlockStateOf` in the new `src/lib/eligibility/email-block.ts`;
  `ChooserPerson.emailBlocked` in `src/lib/eligibility/channel-chooser.ts`, required.
- **Ruling 3 — the founder's three sentences.** `EMAIL_REPORTED_WORDS` in the new
  `src/lib/eligibility/email-block-words.ts`, byte-exact. `ChooserPerson.emailReported` is its own
  refusal code, `EMAIL_REPORTED`, ahead of `EMAIL_OPTED_OUT`, so the reported host reads ruling 3
  rather than GTC-296's sentence.
- **D3 — the board.** `GlancePerson.emailNote`, composed server side by `readEmailNotes` in the
  new `src/lib/glance/email-note.ts`; rendered in `PersonSurface`. A sentence, never the address:
  the suite asserts no guest address appears anywhere in either board's payload.
- **D4 — the middle sentence on the reported event only.** `emailBlockStateOf` answers `REPORTED`
  only when the block's `eventId` is this event. On any other event, the same host's included, it
  is `BLOCKED` and neutral.
- **D6 — hard bounces block.** `BLOCKING_OUTCOMES` is `COMPLAINED`, `BOUNCED`, `SUPPRESSED`.
  `PROVIDER_FAILED` and `CANCELLED` do not block.
- **D7 — Zone 9, "Email Opt-Out and Block"**, is in `GATHER-BUILD-CONSTANTS.md`, mirroring Zone 7.
- **D8 — W1 to W9.** W1–W4 in `email-block-words.ts`; W5 in `RESEND_REFUSAL_WORDS` (kept in the
  door module so it stays `import type` only — 7b's fence); W9 in `WITHHELD_MEANS_UNREACHABLE`.
  W6–W8 are 8b's.
- **F4 — ruling U's "send again", recorded as the founder instructed.** Ruling U's *"send again to
  the same address"* was ruled for a **full mailbox**. Resend reports a full mailbox as
  `delivery_delayed` — a delay, in flight, **never a failure** — and documents `bounced` as
  permanent. So after this slice `doorActionsFor` offers AGAIN only when the address is not blocked:
  **after a refusal at submission or a provider failure**, never after a bounce, a suppression or a
  complaint. `resendToPerson` refuses it anyway with `ADDRESS_BLOCKED`, and refuses to move a
  person onto a blocked address.

### ⚠ FIVE FINDINGS THAT WERE NOT IN THE PLAN

1. ✅ **THE FOUNDER'S FIRST D8 ADDITION IS WITHDRAWN — RULED 2026-09-27, AND WHY IS THE RECORD.**
   The addition gave the reported host *"… Their email reported your invitation as spam. I'll
   text them instead."* when the guest can be texted. But ruling 1 makes the complaint GTC-296's
   opt-out, GTC-296 ruling 3 stops the chase **on every channel** for that event, and D4 confines
   the middle sentence to that same event. So wherever the sentence could appear, Gather never
   texts them, and *"I'll text them instead"* would be false. The founder: *"you are right, and my
   D8 addition was wrong."* **W1 whole stands on the reported event**; its *"Text them"* is the
   host's own by-hand text, which ruling 1 keeps. The suite asserts no word pairs the middle
   sentence with *"I'll text them instead"*. **The opted-out variant is ruled as proposed:** *"I
   can't email this address anymore. Their email reported your invitation as spam. They've opted
   out of texts too, so I'll put them on your list."*
   **Also ruled at the hold:** both chase refusals as proposed, and the reported child's line with
   *"Their email"* changed to *"That email"*, so it cannot be read as the child's: *"Their
   household's contact can't be emailed anymore. That email reported your invitation as spam."*
2. ⚠ **THE DISPATCHER'S FENCE CAN PUT A TEXTABLE PERSON ON A RED THAT SAYS "nowhere to send".**
   F5's fence withholds a queued EMAIL row to a blocked address as `EMAIL_BLOCKED`, which W9 maps
   to `UNREACHABLE`. As the chooser produces that code the person has no usable mobile, so the
   strip's *"nowhere to send"* is true and the door has one action. As the fence produces it — a
   row queued before the block was learned — the person may hold a usable mobile: the door then
   offers EDIT **and** PHONE (asserted), and the strip's words are false. The fence does not send
   by text itself, because the drain honours the stored channel (7b's seam).
   ✅ **RULED 2026-09-27 — IT GETS ITS OWN WORDS.** *"'Nowhere to send' is false of someone Gather
   can text."* `WHY_LINES.UNREACHABLE` in `src/components/glance/strip.ts` now reads the person:
   **"can't email"** (11 characters, under the 16 pin) when Gather may text them, **"nowhere to
   send"** (ruling M's line, unchanged) otherwise. It reads one new fact, `GlancePerson.textable` —
   a usable mobile not opted out of texts, a child's from their carrier (ruling S); a boolean
   derived on the server, never the number, so 7b's no-contact-detail assertion still holds. No
   collision with the ruled strip words (*never got it, nowhere to send, opted out, handed it
   back, maybe timed out, gone quiet, was in, now out*). **The person view's long-form line is
   ruled, with "out" added:** *"I can't email this address anymore, so their invitation hasn't
   gone out. You can send it as a text."* — shown in place of W2, which would be
   false of an invitation nothing will text unless the host does.
3. ⚠ **THE POLL MUST READ THE PROVIDER'S `to`, NOT `Person.email`.** `readResendPollResponse` now
   carries `GetEmailResponseSuccess.to`. Ruling U's edit-and-send can change the person's address
   between a send and its poll; a block written from `Person.email` would land on the new, good
   address. A response with no `to` writes no block rather than guessing (asserted).
4. **THE DEV SERVER SERVED 8a's CODE AGAINST A DATABASE WITHOUT THE TABLE, UNTIL THE APPLY.**
   Turbopack compiles the working tree, so between the build and the apply the pre-flight, the
   board and the door would have errored locally. Nothing was written or sent. Cleared by the
   apply and a restart (KB-005), 2026-09-27.
5. **Two suites were already failing at HEAD, measured in a worktree at `eb03dfd`, not argued:**
   `nudge-provider-gate` 36/37 (*"isSmsEnabled() has exactly 2 call sites … found 3"* — the third is
   `smsProviderConfiguredFor`, slice 7b's), and `glance-replay` 297/298 (*"the surface is 81
   routes"* — `route-classifications.json` holds 83 since [[GTC-296]] and [[GTC-311]]). Neither is
   touched here.

### Zone 5 — generated, rehearsed, held

Generated with `--create-only --skip-generate`. The SQL is one `CREATE TABLE`, one unique and two
plain indexes, and two foreign keys `ON DELETE SET NULL`. Rehearsed on `gather_rehearse_8a`, a
`pg_dump --no-owner --no-acl` clone, with `prisma migrate deploy`:
`pg_class.relfilenode` for `Event` (62688) and `OutboundMessage` (62789) **identical before and
after**, so neither parent was rewritten; 48 → 49 migrations. Probed in a rolled-back transaction:
deleting the outbound row and then the event each **kept the block and nulled the key**; a second
row for the same address was refused by `EmailBlock_address_key`; 0 rows after rollback.
**Applied to `gather_dev` on the founder's word**, with `migrate deploy`: `relfilenode` for
`Event` (18012) and `OutboundMessage` (27599) identical before and after, 48 → 49 migrations,
*"Database schema is up to date!"*. The client was regenerated after the apply (KB-005), the dev
server restarted, and **the clone dropped.**

## Evidence (slice 8b) — THE CHASE. Executor-Completed, 2026-09-27

> **Slice 8b of eight — the chase, wired to the chooser, sending by email and by text through the
> dispatcher.** With it every slice of GTC-189 is built; the ticket stays open on its deploy
> preconditions and the rulings asked for below. **Ships with [[GTC-311]] and slice 8a, together.**
> No migration: `OutboundKind` has carried CHASE_FIRST and CHASE_SECOND since slice 4a.

### The mechanism, in one sentence

**The scheduler asks the chooser who is owed a reminder and writes one row per reminder; the
dispatcher sends it.** `findNudgeCandidates` reads `readAskPreview` — the walk the pre-flight, the
press and the drain already run — and takes each recipient's `chooseChaseRoute` from it, so the
pre-flight's "Chased by" and the reminder cannot disagree. `queueChase` writes a CHASE_FIRST or
CHASE_SECOND `OutboundMessage`; `drainChaseRow` in `src/lib/press/dispatch.ts` re-runs the chooser,
re-checks D5, and sends — `sendChaseEmail` or `sendSms`. There is no second send path.

### The rulings, and where each went

- **The finder (the plan's first bullet).** The SQL `phoneNumber: { not: null }` filter is gone.
  [[GTC-296]]'s gate, which lived in the finder AND in `chaseChannelOf`, now lives only in the
  chooser; the finder keeps one Zone 7 re-check on the text leg, `isOptedOut`, as belt and braces.
- **D2 — rows.** `queueChase` in `src/lib/sms/nudge-sender.ts` replaces `processNudges` and sends
  nothing. One row per leg, written under a transaction-scoped advisory lock, so two ticks cannot
  write two. `sendNudge` is gone. The host's `trigger-nudges` route queues too.
- **GTC-311's two chase lines, met with a stubbed send.** Default on, email only, no exception: a
  CHASE_FIRST row by EMAIL at day four, sent to her address, and CHASE_SECOND at day seven. Excepted:
  no row, no send.
- **D5 — both reminders need the guest still unanswered; F7 fixed and recorded as a defect.**
  `stillUnanswered` in `nudge-eligibility.ts`; asked by the finder and again by the dispatcher at the
  send (`readChaseOwed`), so a guest who answers between the tick that queued a reminder and the tick
  that sends it is withheld `ANSWERED`.
  **F7, as the founder asked it recorded:** the first leg fired on elapsed time alone, so on day four
  a guest who had already said yes was told the host *"is waiting for your response"*. GTC-178 Ruling
  5 deleted an OPENED gate from that leg and never ruled that a RESPONSE should not stop it.
- **Ruling R — carried items chased through the chase, to the carrier.** `RecipientChase.carried` in
  `ask-preview.ts` lists the children whose chase route is CARRIED to that recipient; their pending
  rows keep the carrier owed, and their names go in the reminder.
- **Rulings V and AE — the proxy reminder retired for adults.** `runNudgeScheduler` no longer calls
  it. Its `contactMethod` read is removed — **[[GTC-295]]'s last read**: nothing in `src/` selects or
  compares `PersonEvent.contactMethod` now (asserted).
- **W6–W8.** `composeChase` in the new `src/lib/messages/chase-register.ts`. The ask's subject; *"Hi
  {first} - Gather here again, helping {host} with this one."* — greeting by name because the ask does
  (the founder's addition at the 8a hold); W7 and W8. W8's two carried forms were approved at the 8b
  hold, subject to the rule below.
- ✅ **THE 8b-HOLD RULE — founder, 2026-09-27: *"a reminder names only what is still unanswered, and
  never tells someone who has answered anything 'I haven't heard from you'."*** Under D5 a guest who
  answered one dish of two is reminded about the other, and W7's *"I haven't heard from you yet"*
  would be false of her, in the host's name. `composeChase` now builds the sentence from each
  party's state, read by `readChaseOwed`: the recipient WHOLE is *"you"*; the recipient PARTIAL is
  the open rows by name (*"the pavlova"*); a child WHOLE is the child (*"Ollie"*); a child PARTIAL is
  the child's open rows (*"Ollie's trifle"*); anybody DONE is not named. *"I haven't heard from you"*
  is said only where "you" is the whole of it AND the recipient has answered nothing at all — which
  keeps W7's ruled sentence exactly where it is true.
  ✅ **RULED 2026-09-28, with one change:** *"'you' is used only when nothing at all is answered.
  Once someone has answered anything, name her own open things instead: her open rows by name, or
  'whether you can make it' when she has no rows of her own (ruling AA)."* A carrier who answered for
  Ollie and not for herself would otherwise have read *"I haven't heard back about you yet"*. Every
  combination is pinned byte-exact in layer A, in both legs; Pat (one dish of two answered) and Kim
  (her own open, her child's answered) prove it end to end in layer D. No provisional marker remains.
  **Every form, both legs — generated from `composeChase` itself, so this list cannot drift from the
  code.** Each reminder opens *"Hi {first} - Gather here again, helping {host} with this one."* (first)
  or *"Hi {first} - Gather here, checking in once more for {host}."* (second); what follows is:

  - **Nothing answered, own rows**
    - first: *"I haven't heard from you yet. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
    - second: *"I still haven't heard from you. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
  - **Nothing answered, no rows (itemless)**
    - first: *"I haven't heard from you yet. One tap to say whether you can make it: {link}"*
    - second: *"I still haven't heard from you. One tap to say whether you can make it: {link}"*
  - **Answered one of two**
    - first: *"I haven't heard back about the salad yet. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
    - second: *"I still haven't heard back about the salad. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
  - **Answered one of three**
    - first: *"I haven't heard back about the pavlova and the salad yet. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
    - second: *"I still haven't heard back about the pavlova and the salad. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
  - **Nothing answered, carries Ollie**
    - first: *"I haven't heard back about you and Ollie yet. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
    - second: *"I still haven't heard back about you and Ollie. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
  - **Own done, Ollie open**
    - first: *"I haven't heard back about Ollie yet. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
    - second: *"I still haven't heard back about Ollie. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
  - **Own partial, Ollie open**
    - first: *"I haven't heard back about the salad and Ollie yet. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
    - second: *"I still haven't heard back about the salad and Ollie. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
  - **Own partial, Ollie partial**
    - first: *"I haven't heard back about the salad and Ollie's trifle yet. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
    - second: *"I still haven't heard back about the salad and Ollie's trifle. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
  - **Own done, Ollie partial**
    - first: *"I haven't heard back about Ollie's trifle yet. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
    - second: *"I still haven't heard back about Ollie's trifle. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*
  - **Own open, answered some of Ollie's**
    - first: *"I haven't heard back about the pavlova and Ollie's trifle yet. One tap to say yes, no or maybe: {link}"*

### ⚠ FINDINGS THAT WERE NOT IN THE PLAN

1. ⚠ **THE CHASE BRANCH THREW AWAY THE CHOOSER'S REASON — SLICE 5c's FINDING, AT A SECOND SITE.** A
   reminder to an address blocked after it was queued was withheld `NOT_THIS_RECIPIENT`, because the
   blocked guest had moved from the recipients to the host's list and the branch looked among the
   recipients first. The ask's path checks the host's list first for exactly this reason. Caught by
   an assertion written for a mutation that had survived; fixed the same way.
2. ⚠ **THE NEW FINDER DROPPED GUESTS REFUSED THE ASK SILENTLY.** It walked `preview.recipients`, so a
   guest who unsubscribed after being asked (now on the host's list) produced no recorded skip — the
   rule every skip on this path holds. `tests/email-opt-out-test.ts` layer I caught it. Fixed with
   `HOST_LIST_SKIP_REASON`, a `Record` over `HostListWhy`.
3. ⚠ **D5 CHANGES MORE THAN F7, AND THE WIDER PART IS A READING, SAID PLAINLY.** "Still has not
   answered" is built as: said they cannot come → answered; any row of their own or of a child they
   carry still PENDING → unanswered; no rows at all → unanswered until they say whether they can
   come. Ruling R's own case needs the middle clause, and it changes the old second leg, which stopped
   on ANY one answer: **a guest with two dishes who answered one is now reminded about the other.**
   And **a MAYBE is an answer, so a maybe gets no reminder** — which is Hinge §8, and which the old
   first leg violated on day four. Two suites had pinned F7's behaviour and are replaced (below).
4. ✅ **RULED D1 AND FIXED — THE GATE WROTE REMINDER ROWS INTO `gather_dev`, AND THIS SLICE CREATED
   THAT.** *[At the hold: `nudge-provider-gate`'s scheduler call is scoped to a tagged fixture event
   and removes it; its layer D, which calls the real cron route and cannot be scoped, records the
   rows before the call and removes every row it wrote. Both assert the outbound count restored.
   **The two rows left behind were removed: 74 outbound rows before (ASK 72, CHASE_FIRST 1,
   CHASE_SECOND 1), 72 after, all ASK.** They carried new ids each gate run — layer D, not layer B,
   was writing them.]* Original finding:
   `tests/nudge-provider-gate-test.ts` calls `runNudgeScheduler()` unscoped. Before this slice that
   run attempted sends with every credential stripped and left nothing; now it QUEUES rows, and any
   later suite whose drain is unscoped processes them. Measured after one gate run: **two CHASE rows
   on the security fixture's live event** (*Nudge Candidate Live*), both withheld `SMS_DISABLED`,
   nothing sent. With a TNZ token or a working Resend key in the environment of a later drain, they
   would be sent to fixture contacts. **Raised, not changed** — the obvious fix, scoping that call,
   edits the suite the founder kept in the gate to guard this scheduler.
5. ✅ **RULED D2 — THE PROXY MODULE IS DELETED IN ITS OWN COMMIT AFTER 8b**, with the one Zone 6 edit
   approved for it: the security suite's check becomes a tombstone asserting the file stays gone. In
   8b it is unwired and carries an `ANCHOR(GTC-189)` that the deletion ends.
6. ✅ **RULED D3 — LEFT AS IT IS, AND FILED AS [[GTC-334]].** The scheduler's health is still GTC-214's
   `smsConfigured`; the run sends nothing, so send health belongs to the dispatcher, which GTC-334
   moves it to.
7. **F2's COST, IN 7a's OWN TERMS.** 7a read every kind because *"a chase leg that bounces has found the
   same dead channel an ask would have."* Pinned to ASK, a reminder that bounces does not redden the
   strip; slice 8a's block still learns it, the next reminder refuses, and the person surface carries
   the sentence. Recorded at the site in `read.ts`.
8. **THE AUTO-NUDGE FINDER CAN NO LONGER CHASE THE HOST, WHATEVER TOKENS EXIST.** It used to be gated
   only by the withheld token, so a stale one let it chase her (GTC-256 build decision 3). The
   chooser's `HOST_OWN_ASK` reads no token. `host-never-messaged`'s precondition, which demonstrated
   the hole, is replaced by the assertion that it is closed; the decide-by finder is still
   token-gated, so the revocation still matters.

### ⚠ TEN TRIPWIRES FIRED IN THE FIRST GATE RUN — TWO WERE DEFECTS AND EIGHT WERE REPLACED

The two defects are findings 1 and 2. The eight replacements, each with its reason at the site:

- `nudge-cadence` — *"responding does not retroactively suppress the first leg"* flipped to D5.
- `coordinator-holds-ask` — two assertions expected a MAYBE chased at day four (F7); the control moved
  to the itemless coordinator, and GTC-294's property is asserted as the coordinator getting exactly
  the ordinary guest's outcome.
- `host-never-messaged` — the precondition demonstrating the stale-token hole, replaced by the
  assertion that it is closed (finding 8).
- `nudge-dedup-scope` — the stamp moved with the send; re-pointed at `recordAcceptance`.
- `nudge-provider-gate` — `proxyCandidates` as proof the run reached its `try`, replaced by "no error,
  finder returned", plus an assertion that the proxy branch is gone.
- `guest-response` — a word near the opt-out check, replaced by the call itself, `isOptedOut(`.
- `outbound-drain` — `resultOf` one link further along, through `sendHostVoiced`.
- `chase-channel` — the preview's chase objects gained `carried`; Sarah's carries Ollie's.

`host-list-row` and `resend-errors` fired too, and needed no change to a test: both measure the shape
of `drainOnce`, and lifting the chase branch out of it restored that shape.

### Mutations — eleven planted, ⚠ THREE SURVIVED AT FIRST, each verified landed and restored byte-identical

1. **N1** — the finder ignores D5. **3 failed.**
2. ⚠ **N2 — the finder ignores a taken leg's row. SURVIVED**, because the queue refuses it too.
   Repaired with an assertion on the finder alone. Re-planted: **2 failed.**
3. ⚠ **N3 — the queue writes without its existence check. SURVIVED**, because the finder refuses it
   too. Repaired by handing the queue a stale candidate directly. Re-planted: **2 failed.**
4. **N4** — a chase row falls through to the ask's path (F1). **5 failed.**
5. **N5** — no D5 re-check at the send. **3 failed.**
6. **N6** — acceptance does not stamp the leg. **2 failed.**
7. **N7** — the board reads every kind again. **4 failed.**
8. **N8** — carried children not collected for the chase. **3 failed.**
9. **N9** — the reminder does not greet by name. **3 failed.**
10. ⚠ **N10 — the email chase ignores the block. SURVIVES, BY CONSTRUCTION.** A chase re-chooses its
    channel through the chooser, which reads the block: a blocked, textable guest is re-chosen to
    text, and a blocked guest with no mobile is on the host's list (finding 1). The fence on a chase
    row cannot be reached without breaking the chooser. It stays, failing safe; the behaviour is
    asserted through the chooser.
11. **N11** — the finder ignores the host's exception. **7 failed.**

⚠ **N2 AND N3 ARE THE SAME SHAPE AS 8a's M1: two guards covering each other, measured by one test.**
A test that passes when either guard is present proves neither.

**At the 8b hold, three more on the naming rule:**

- **P1** — *"heard from you"* whenever "you" is the whole of it, ignoring what was answered. **3 failed.**
- **P2** — a PARTIAL recipient called *"you"*, the old wording. **Failed** (the layer did not compile).

  another business; new words for the pre-flight and the privacy page.
  ✅ **Done 2026-10-02** — a guest's text reply puts them in the host's hands until she hands them
  back: no reminder and no "please decide" follow-up goes to them while the reply is in force, at
  the sweep and at the drain (withheld `REPLIED`); their card is red "replied by text" and every
  reply shows on the host's board, newest first, with when it came (the poll carries the words for
  the host only); the hand-back reaches a guest who replied, offering only what would still be
  sent. The pre-flight and privacy page carry Q4's and Q5's words. No migration; nothing to
  configure. See GTC-350's Evidence.

The same day, on TNZ's answer C1 (on the shared shortcode an opt-out attributes to the last
message sent, whoever sent it), the founder ruled to order a dedicated 6-digit shortcode,
*"Order it now (Recommended)"*, verbatim in the TNZ record. TNZ's page gives about four weeks
to set up, so it lands around the end of October; until then texts use the shared shortcode.
⚠ *Superseded 2026-10-02* (founder ruling, verbatim in the TNZ record): *"Hold it, keep "Reply
STOP""* — *"Don't order yet and change nothing. The rare guest whose STOP lands with another
business keeps getting Gather's texts. Revisit once texts are going out in volume."* Nothing had
been ordered. At launch, texts use the shared shortcode.

**Security suite:** 159/159, and `security:routes` 118/118.

**Commit hash:** **`b3e69c4`** — the build, the suite, the ten replaced tripwires and this Evidence,
approved by the founder 2026-09-28. This line is the documentation-only commit after it, the shape
8a and GTC-311 used. ⚠ The pre-commit hook ran `prettier --write` over the staged files; re-verified
on the committed tree: `tsc` exit 0, `chase` 76/76, `security` 159/159, `security:routes` 118/118,
`chase-channel` 123/123, `nudge-cadence` 54/54, `email-block` 92/92.

## Evidence (slice 5e) — Executor-Completed

> **The mini-send.** A person added after the press gets their own send, on their own clock, through
> the dispatcher slice 5c built. ⚠ **Two things ship unmet and both are named rather than hidden:**
> ruling AJ's question (by founder ruling) and the token minting a late arrival needs (blocked on
> [[GTC-316]], found by building).

### ⚠ THE FINDING OF THIS SLICE, AND IT LEADS THE EVIDENCE ON FOUNDER INSTRUCTION

*Recorded in the founder's words, 2026-09-19, because the wording is the record:*

> A sweep keyed on the old press's stamp re-sent whole legacy guest lists, on four real boards
> including a demo board flagged as not-throwaway, and the two reasons nothing was delivered were a
> broken key and reserved domains. Both luck.

**The measurement behind it.** `Event.sentAt` has been written since [[GTC-169]] by the OLD press —
the one that stamped clocks and sent nothing — so an event pressed before this slice has `sentAt` set
and **zero** `OutboundMessage` rows. Keyed on `sentAt` alone, `enrolMiniSends` read every recipient on
such an event as a late arrival. The first run of `tests/mini-send-test.ts` created **20 rows across
four real boards** — three security-test events and **`GTC-192 replay — arrival`**, which this repo's
own notes flag as a seeded demo board and not a throwaway fixture — and `drainOnce` then **claimed and
attempted a send for every one.**

**Verified row by row before anything was deleted:** all 20 attempted, all **rejected**, none
accepted, `PersonEvent.sentAt` unmoved at 109, every address on `test.local` or `example.com`. So
nothing was delivered — **and the two reasons are a broken key and reserved domains, which is luck
twice over and not design once.**

⚠ **AND THE PREDICATE IS THE FIX, NOT A GUARD** — recorded that way on founder instruction, because
the distinction is what stops the next reader treating it as a safety net that could be relaxed:

> **A mini-send is a send after a send, and a legacy pressed event never sent — so there is nothing
> for a late arrival to be late to.**

`enrolMiniSends` therefore requires `outboundMessages: { some: { kind: 'ASK' } }`: an event **the new
press has written for**. That is the definition of the population, not a filter over a wider one. The
suite's stray count fell from **22 to 2** — the 2 being its own fixture's — and layer G builds a
legacy event (`sentAt` set, a READY recipient, no rows) so the skip is asserted rather than assumed.

⚠ **The consequence for those events is filed as its own question, [[GTC-322]]**, on founder
instruction — their guests are never messaged by Gather at all, and re-pressing them is a migration
decision rather than a line in this evidence.

### Preflight

`npm run db:migrate` in sync, no migration. Dev server probed. `test:security` **159/159** and
`tsc --noEmit` exit 0 before and after. Tree at the start: `922e13c`.

### The rulings this slice carries

- ✅ **NO_RECIPIENTS is ruled**, not a reading. *"An event with nobody to message is not an event
  ready to send, and refusing is the same fail-closed direction as ruling AC."* Recorded at the
  refusal.
- ✅ **409 covers the whole family — widened deliberately.** *"A refusal about the state of the event
  is a 409 whatever the specific state, and two shapes for one family is how a caller learns to read
  the wrong one."* The type's docstring now forbids "restoring" a 400 for the two that predate the
  rest.
- ✅ **First-acceptance-wins is ruled.** *"Ruling G says the clock starts when that person's provider
  accepts; a second acceptance for the same person is the same person's clock already running."*
- ✅ **[[GTC-321]] stays filed and unruled**, with D holding, and the ticket says so at the top so an
  executor reaching it from the build stops there.
- ✅ **[[GTC-316]] is a precondition of 5f, not of 5c or 5e**, with the chain recorded on that
  ticket: the token is minted at the press, the directory publishes it, and **5f is the commit that
  makes a press reachable** — `tests/press-route-test.ts` asserts no UI file posts to the press today.

### What was built

**`enrolMiniSends` in `src/lib/press/dispatch.ts`**, run first in the cron tick. For every pressed
event, any addressed recipient with no ask row gets one; `drainOnce` does everything after that.

**A sweep, not a hook at capture**, and the precedent is exact: `generateWrapUpLinks` is keyed on
(event, person) rather than on the event for the same reason its own comment gives — *"a guest added
AFTER the press must still get their link... an event-level 'already done' check would pass every
duplicate test and silently strip late guests instead."* Three capture paths exist and a fourth can
be added tomorrow; a hook in each is three writers of one rule.

**And there is no mini-send branch anywhere.** Ruling G made the clock per person, which is what
removes the need for one, and decision 33 removed `isMiniSend` for the same reason. Asserted: the
dispatcher names no `isMiniSend`, `miniSend`, `isLate` or `lateArrival`.

### ⚠ FINDING 1 — the sweep re-sent whole legacy guest lists, and it did it to real boards

**Keyed on `Event.sentAt` alone, the sweep enrols every recipient of every event pressed before this
slice.** `sentAt` has been written since [[GTC-169]] by the OLD press — the one that stamped clocks
and sent nothing — so such an event has `sentAt` set and **zero** ask rows, and every recipient on it
reads as a late arrival.

⚠ **IT HAPPENED, AGAINST `gather_dev`.** The first run of the suite created **20 rows across four
real boards** — three security-test events and **`GTC-192 replay — arrival`, a seeded DEMO board that
this repo's own notes flag as "not throwaway fixtures"** — and `drainOnce` then **claimed and
attempted a send for every one.**

**Nothing was delivered.** All 20 were rejected by Resend, none accepted, and
`PersonEvent.sentAt` did not move — verified row by row before anything was deleted. Every address
was on `test.local` or `example.com`, both reserved. ⚠ **Both of those are luck rather than design**:
the key being broken and the seeds using reserved domains are the only two reasons this was not 20
real invitations.

**The fix is the predicate:** the sweep requires `outboundMessages: { some: { kind: 'ASK' } }` — an
event **the new press has written for**. A mini-send is a send after a send, and there is nothing for
a late arrival to be late to on an event that never sent. Layer G builds a legacy event — `sentAt`
set, a READY recipient, no rows — and asserts it is left alone. After the fix the suite's stray count
fell from **22 to 2**, the 2 being its own fixture's.

**What it leaves open, named:** a legacy pressed event never gets ask rows from anything, so its
guests are never messaged by Gather at all. Correct — nobody decided to send them anything through
this machinery — and re-pressing them is a migration decision and a founder ruling, not a sweep's.

### ⚠ FINDING 2 — the standing warning's newest rule, arriving as a feature rather than a mutation

The rule added hours earlier: *a mutation of a query with no tenant filter is not scoped to the
fixture; its blast radius is the table.* Finding 1 is that rule with no mutation involved —
`enrolMiniSends` and `drainOnce` are **cron functions and a cron has no tenant**, so a suite that
calls them reaches every row in the database by design.

The `finally` block deleting what the FIXTURE created was never going to be enough. Both suites now
**snapshot every `OutboundMessage` id before running and delete anything not in that set afterwards**,
with the reason in place. `tests/outbound-drain-test.ts` got the same treatment even though it was
safe by circumstance — it drains globally too, and it was only safe because no other event had rows.

### ⚠ FINDING 3 — the blocker: a mini-send needs a token, and minting it is GTC-316's

A late arrival holds no PARTICIPANT token, so `linkState` reads `AT_PRESS` and there is no link to put
in a message. The only thing that mints one is `ensureEventTokens` — whose live callers are the
person-edit `PATCH`, the tokens route, `workflow.ts`'s transition and `pressSend`, **none of which
runs when a host simply adds somebody.**

⚠ **CALLING IT FROM THE SWEEP IS REFUSED.** It mints a PARTICIPANT token for every CHILD membership
and the unauthenticated family directory publishes it. **On a cron that fires every two minutes on
every pressed event, with no host action at all** — which is strictly worse than the state the
ordering ruling reasoned about, because *"nothing presses until 5f wires the button"* and **a cron
does not wait for a button.**

So the sweep enrols only recipients who already hold a link, counts the rest as **`awaitingLink`**,
and minting is a precondition on [[GTC-316]]. Asserted in both directions, with a tokenless late
arrival in the fixture so the skip is exercised rather than assumed — and asserted that the sweep
names no token issuer at all, because the fix that looks obvious is one import away.

⚠ **AND ONE PREMISE OF MY OWN WAS WRONG, AND THE RECORD IS THAT IT WAS CHECKED BEFORE IT WAS
REPORTED** — the founder's instruction, 2026-09-19: *"That is the rule working rather than the rule
being quoted."*

I suspected `GET /api/events/[id]/invite-status` minted tokens on the plan page's **30-second poll**,
which would have meant [[GTC-316]] was **already firing continuously** for any host with the plan
page open — and would have made the ordering ruling's premise false before this slice touched
anything. It was a large claim and it was about to be reported.

**It is false. The route names `ensureEventTokens` only in a comment.** The live minting callers are
the person-edit `PATCH`, the tokens route, `workflow.ts`'s transition and `pressSend` — four, and none
of them a poll. Checked by grep against the route file before the sentence left the session.

**The rule this is an instance of, rather than a citation of:** a claim that would change an ordering
ruling is a claim to measure first. The cost of checking was one grep; the cost of reporting it wrong
would have been a founder re-ruling an ordering on a premise that did not exist — which is exactly
the shape of the seventh-call-site finding recorded on [[GTC-320]], in the other direction.

### ⚠ Ruling AJ ships UNMET, by founder ruling

*"5e ships the mini-send without the question, records AJ as unmet with GTC-311 named as what meets
it... Say plainly that this is a ruling shipping incomplete, not a ruling met — and that a mini-send
recipient therefore falls to the event default with no chance to except them."*

**So: a mini-send recipient falls to `Event.chaseWhenNoMobileDefault` and the host is never asked
about them.** Under ruling AH that default is *chase by email when there is no mobile*, ON — so a
late arrival with no usable phone is chased by email without ever being offered the *hand this one to
me* exception every other recipient was offered. Decision 23's hole, still open, with the mini-send
shipping over it. Named in the module and asserted; the column is asserted still null for the late
arrival, so the sentence describes the tree and not only the comment.

### The Auto-Assign tombstone — one of two conditions met, and it stays

Its comment named two expiries: this ticket for mini-sends and [[GTC-178]] (E1) for the cadence.
**Mini-sends now exist**, so the first is met. E1 is not built, and ruling AJ's question is not met
either — a thing the comment could not have named in 2026-08. The comment now distinguishes **MET**
from **NOT MET** by name, and the assertion reads for both, because *a tombstone that says only "not
yet" is a tombstone the next reader deletes.*

- **GREEN — 33/33**, run twice, identical, with the stray-cleanup line reporting 2 both times.
- ⚠ **AND I WROTE THE `ok(async () => …)` ANTI-PATTERN INTO THIS SUITE THREE TIMES** — the one the
  standing warning gained the same day, from slice 5c's second half. Caught and removed before the
  first run, because the rule was fresh; without it they would have been three silent double-invokes
  of a counting query. **Recorded because a rule written and then broken by its own author in the
  next file is the measure of how easy the mistake is.**

**Mutations** — each verified landed, each restored byte-identical:

- **the legacy predicate removed** (`sentAt` alone) → fails 2, including the finding-1 assertion.
- **the link check removed** (enrol a tokenless recipient) → fails 4.
- **the already-dealt-with check removed** → fails 7, which is the idempotence layer doing its job:
  a non-idempotent sweep creates a second invitation every two minutes.
